# Governed Runtime Roadmap — implementation progress

Tracks from `docs/Actrone_Governed_Runtime_Roadmap.md`. Every track's code is built + unit/contract-tested to the codebase's standard bar.

> **HONESTY RELABEL (2026-07-04):** earlier notes used "deployment-gated" too loosely. The real remaining work splits three ways, and the distinction matters: **(A) genuinely run-infra / config** — image + manifests + code all exist, someone runs `apply` or sets secrets (e.g. Stripe keys, Data-Residency terraform apply, WS2 vendor creds); **(B) unbuilt wiring** — code existed but wasn't reachable (constructed nowhere / no entrypoint / no Dockerfile / orphan UI / no caller) — these are **engineering**, closed in "Cycle 4" below; **(C) unbuilt features** — genuinely new multi-part work (a token-minting lifecycle, a durable multi-agent workflow, a browser-pool service, ML models) — listed in **"Honest remaining FEATURE work (Tier-C)"**. Several items I'd tagged "deployment-gated" were actually (B) or (C). All work is **uncommitted** (owner commits).

---

## Cycle 11 — Optional Agent Scheduling + Autonomy (`docs/Actrone_Agent_Scheduling_Plan.md`)

Give agents **optional** proactive autonomy — run themselves on a cadence, unattended, via Temporal **Schedules** — with zero governance bypass. Implemented phase by phase (P1→P5), each prod-grade (CLAUDE.md) → tests green → marked ✅ → next. All uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **P7 — Final plan-vs-code sweep: 3 genuine wiring gaps found + CLOSED, 21/21 deliverables verified.** A skeptical audit of the plan against the *code* (not progress.md claims) surfaced three items the plan calls for that weren't actually wired — now closed. **(1) "Next 5 runs" preview (plan §5/§6) — was unwired** (`NextRuns` existed but surfaced nowhere): new **`POST /v1/schedule/preview`** (`AgentHandler.PreviewSchedule`, pure compute over the same robfig/cron parser Temporal uses → next 5 fire times; invalid cron → 422) + route + `agentsApi.previewSchedule` + a **debounced live preview in the Studio `ScheduleConfig` panel** (shows the next 5 local run times as you edit cron/interval/tz). Tests: `TestPreviewSchedule` (valid cron→5 · interval · invalid→422 · no-cadence→empty); control-tower `tsc`+`eslint` clean. **(2) Env-promotion → reconcile (plan §3) — was unwired** (`promotion.Service.Promote` never reconciled the schedule): `PromotionHandler` now holds the coordinator and, after BOTH apply paths (`Promote` + `Approve`), reconciles the promoted agent's schedule **in the target env** (`reconcileAfterPromotion` → `WithEnv(ctx, res.Promotion.TargetEnv)`), so a scheduled agent promoted into production starts firing there instead of waiting for the next sweep. Wired in main.go (`WithScheduleCoordinator` — now on **3** handlers: agent + control + promotion). **(3) Budget-exhaustion → schedule pause (plan §4/§8) — honestly NOT fully wireable:** verified per-run spend (`max_task_cost_usd`) is enforced at run time (the loop wraps up cleanly at the cap — inherited by scheduled runs) but it COMPLETES (not FAILED, so the failure-monitor can't catch it), and there is **no per-agent monthly budget-status query** (`CostRepository.Summary` is tenant-level). So `Lifecycle.BudgetExhausted` is documented as a **reserved seam** (wired into `Desired()` — flips the schedule to paused the moment a budget-status source populates it) rather than faked. **Verification:** a 21-check plan-vs-code sweep all green (spec · validation · reconciler · cron/interval/tz · RunTimeout · session · interpolation · same-workflow · active-window · A/B/C/D · provenance · main.go construction+injection ×3 · preview · promotion · metric); `go build ./...`, `go vet`, all touched unit suites + 5 integration tests vs real Temporal green. The plan is fully implemented + wired, with the single budget-schedule-pause item honestly flagged as source-gated.
- [x] ✅ **P6 — The 5 previously-deferred deep integrations BUILT + WIRED + tested against a REAL Temporal server (Docker).** No longer honest-deferred — all live. **Item 5 (live Temporal path):** `temporalstore/integration_test.go` (build-tag `integration`) runs against a real Temporal (Docker `temporalio/temporal server start-dev` on `:7233` via `TEMPORAL_TEST_ADDRESS`, or SDK `StartDevServer` fallback) — **3 tests green:** ensure→describe→idempotent-update→delete→idempotent-redelete (asserts the cadence is server-translated to Calendars + the run-timeout/task-queue mapped onto the action), the Reconciler create→pause→delete round-trip, and the inspector+pause surface. **Item 2 (fresh per-run L1 session):** confirmed the L1 key = `AgentTaskInput.IdempotencyKey` (threaded into RetrieveContext + WriteBack; L1 Redis keyed by agent+session, L2 Qdrant by agent only) and that a scheduled run had `SessionID==""` (shared L1 bug). Fixed: in the scheduled-guard the workflow now derives `sessionID = agentschedule.DeriveSessionID(input.ScheduleID, workflow.Now(ctx))` and uses it at both memory sites — **fresh L1 per tick, L2 persists**. Determinism-safe: guard version **bumped to 2** (v1 history = skip+interpolate only replays byte-for-byte; session only on v2+). **Item 1 (signed-ledger provenance):** added `Trigger`/`CreatedBy`/`ScheduleID` to `AuditAppendInput`, populated from `input.Source`/`.CreatedBy`/`.ScheduleID` at the AuditAppend call site, and stamped into the HMAC-chained audit-spine payload (only when set ⇒ existing rows byte-identical) — audit now distinguishes an autonomous scheduled run from a user one. **Item 3 (companion A non-blocking escalation):** new `notifyScheduledEscalation` — at the DPE + MediaGuard escalate sites, a run with `Source=="scheduled"` **no longer durably pauses**: it persists+notifies the escalation (reusing `CreateEscalation`, so a human still sees it) then ends CLEANLY with an `escalation_required` outcome (not a failed run, not a hang that would freeze every future tick under Overlap=skip). **Item 4 (companion B auto-pause monitor):** new `agentschedule.Monitor` (seam-based: `ScheduleInspector` + `SchedulePauser`) — `RunOnce` pauses any schedule whose recent runs hit the consecutive-failure threshold via the already-tested `ShouldAutoPause`, best-effort per-schedule; `temporalstore.Inspector` lists `agent-schedule:*` schedules, sorts each `RecentActions` oldest-first, and resolves each run's terminal status via `DescribeWorkflowExecution` (COMPLETED=ok; FAILED/TIMED_OUT/TERMINATED=fail; RUNNING/indeterminate skipped); `Store.Pause` is the pauser. Wired in `main.go` (`go Monitor.Start(5m)`). **Tests:** `TestMonitor_RunOnce` (pauses over-threshold only · pause-error collected but pass continues · default threshold 3 · inspector-error surfaced) + the live inspector/pause integration test. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, **every touched unit suite green (agentschedule/temporalstore/agent/domain/http/workflow/service/middleware — no regression)**, + 3 integration tests vs real Temporal. **Both prior "small remaining" items now CLOSED (2026-07-18):** **(per-agent threshold)** `ScheduleDefinition.PauseAfterFailures` (from `EffectivePauseAfterFailures()`) is carried into the Temporal schedule **Memo** on create; `temporalstore.Inspector` decodes it (`converter.FromPayload`) into `ScheduleRuns.Threshold`, and `Monitor.RunOnce` honours each agent's own threshold over the default — proven by `TestIntegration_MemoThreshold` (memo round-trips) + a monitor unit case (own-threshold wins). **(durable auto-pause)** `ReconcileInput.PreserveExistingPause` → `ScheduleDefinition.PreservePauseIfExists`; `Store.Ensure` now Describes the existing schedule and, on a NON-forcing reconcile-to-active, KEEPS an existing pause — so the automatic **startup sweep never clears a monitor/operator auto-pause** (the coordinator's `Sweep` sets the flag), while a human resume/update (flag false) still applies desired state. Proven by `TestIntegration_PreservePause` (self-heal preserves · forcing reconcile clears). **All 5 integration tests green vs real Temporal.** So the plan's deferred list is fully closed — nothing "small remaining."

double-gated (Source + GetVersion) so every non-scheduled run and every existing history replays byte-for-byte. `AgentTaskInput` already carries `Source`/`ScheduleID`/`CreatedBy` (P2) — threaded + logged (`schedule_id` on the skip event). **Tests:** `TestPlanScheduledRun` (in-window runnable + prompt resolved + session matches standalone derivation · weekend tick skipped · deterministic across calls); (b) **fresh-per-run session plumbing** — `DeriveSessionID` is built+tested and each tick already gets a UNIQUE Temporal workflow id (server appends the nominal time), so distinct ticks are distinct executions; threading the derived id into the L1 memory-session activities is deferred; (c) **companion A non-blocking escalation** (a scheduled run recording `escalation_required` + ending instead of hanging) — the `RunTimeout` (P2) already bounds a hung run; the escalation-aware early-end is deferred; (d) **companion B auto-pause MONITOR** — `ShouldAutoPause` decision is built+tested; the loop that observes recent run outcomes and pauses the Schedule needs a run-outcome feed / live Temporal describe (deploy-gated). The run-time active-window enforcement + interpolation — the functional heart of a correct scheduled run — are wired now.
- [x] ✅ **P3 (a+b) — Governance helpers + full lifecycle wiring + companions C & D (scheduling is now live end-to-end from register → Temporal).** **P3a — pure, fully-tested governance (`agentschedule/governance.go`):** `Lifecycle{Active,Promoted,Paused,BudgetExhausted}.Desired()` folding runtime state → Active/Paused/Absent (archived/unpromoted→absent; paused/budget→paused); **`WithinActiveWindow`** — the run-time guard that no-ops a tick fired outside the operator's timezone + active-days + HH:MM window (incl. **midnight-wrapping** windows, tz-correct); **`MeetsIntervalFloor`** (companion D) — checks the SMALLEST gap between the next 6 fires ≥ the tier floor, so it catches an over-frequent *cron* (`* * * * *`) too, not just `every_seconds`; **`ShouldAutoPause`** (companion B decision) — pause only when the last N runs ALL failed (a success resets the streak). **P3b — wiring + authz + limits:** **(C, security-critical)** new `guardSchedule` in `agent/service.go` (wired into all 4 register/update paths beside `guardCapabilityTiers`) — arming a scheduled manifest requires **agent_admin/super_admin**, server-authoritative + **fail-closed** (POST/PUT `/v1/agents` are NOT route-role-gated, so this is the real boundary); **(D)** the same guard enforces a **tier interval floor** (free 1h / pro 5m / scale+enterprise absolute-only), fail-OPEN on a tier-lookup error. New **`scheduleCoordinator`** (`handler/http/schedule_coordinator.go`, best-effort + nil-safe) reconciles an agent's Schedule from request ctx (env/principal) + lifecycle (status + `IsPaused` + dev-gated `Promoted`) — hooked into **AgentHandler** Register (only if scheduled ⇒ no wasted Temporal call for reactive agents) / Update (always ⇒ tears down a removed schedule) / Delete (`remove`), and **ControlHandler** Pause/Resume (`reconcileByID` after the durable flag flips). **`ScheduleID` refactored to `agent-schedule:{agentUUID}:{env}`** (dropped org — the UUID is already globally unique) so the id is reconstructable from agent+env alone and the **startup `Sweep`** (best-effort, production-scoped self-heal over `ListTenantIDs`→`List`) mints the SAME id the lifecycle hooks do (no duplicate schedules). Wired in `cmd/orchestrator/main.go`: reconciler over `temporalstore.New(temporalClient.ScheduleClient())` + coordinator injected into both handlers + `go Sweep()` at boot. Added exported `middleware.ContextWithActroneRole` test seam (mirrors `ContextWithUserSubject`). **Tests:** P3a — `TestLifecycle_Desired`, `TestWithinActiveWindow` (day/time/wrap/tz), `TestMeetsIntervalFloor` (interval + every-minute-cron caught + daily-cron passes + disabled), `TestShouldAutoPause`; P3b — `TestGuardSchedule_Authz` (reactive-ungated · operator-rejected · no-role-rejected · agent_admin/super_admin-allowed), `TestScheduleCoordinator_InputFor` (active+prod→Active · dev→Absent · archived→Absent) + `_NilSafe` (nil coordinator = total no-op). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/{agent,handler/http,middleware,agentschedule/...}`, all suites green, **whole-module build clean with the main.go wiring**. **Honest scope:** the imperative Temporal Create/Update/Delete + the sweep's live reconcile need a running Temporal server (deploy-gated, same as the rest of the Temporal path); companion **B's auto-pause MONITOR** (observing recent run outcomes to feed `ShouldAutoPause` → pause) and the workflow-side active-window guard + provenance + non-blocking escalation are **P3c**.
- [x] ✅ **P2 — Reconciler `internal/agentschedule` built (idempotent Temporal-Schedules engine) + adapter.** The durable per-agent scheduler, structured to dodge the `service → workflow` import cycle: the **core package is a leaf** (imports only `domain`) so the workflow layer can reuse its pure runtime helpers, and the concrete Temporal binding lives in a **separate `temporalstore` sub-package** (the one place importing both `workflow` + the Temporal SDK, constructed in main.go, imported by nobody). **Core (`agentschedule.go`):** `Reconciler` over a narrow 2-method `scheduleStore` seam (`Ensure` create-or-update / `Delete`, both idempotent), driven by a caller-computed `DesiredState` (Active/Paused/Absent — the lifecycle folds active/promoted/paused/budget into it, P3) so the reconciler stays policy-free; `Reconcile` (not-scheduled or Absent → delete; Paused → ensure-paused; Active → ensure-running) + `Sweep` (batch self-heal, per-agent errors collected via `errors.Join`, honours `ctx.Done()`); deterministic `ScheduleID(org,agent,env)`; `BuildDefinition` mapping `domain.ScheduleSpec`→ a Temporal-agnostic `ScheduleDefinition` (cadence, overlap, catchup, **RunTimeout companion A**, run params incl. `CreatedBy` companion C). **Pure runtime helpers (reused by P3 in-workflow, determinism-safe):** `DeriveSessionID = uuidv5(ns, scheduleID+":"+scheduledAt)` (same tick⇒same session = replay-safe+idempotent, distinct ticks⇒distinct fresh L1; L2 persists via agent_id), `InterpolatePrompt` ({{scheduled_at}}/{{run_date}}/{{timezone}} in the schedule's tz, invalid tz→UTC), `CatchupWindow` (companion #4: small 60s default absorbs a blip / wide 24h when CatchUp on), `NextRuns` (the Studio "next 5 runs" preview — cron via the same `robfig/cron.ParseStandard`, interval by step, tz-correct). **Adapter (`temporalstore/store.go`):** implements the store over `client.ScheduleClient` — `Ensure` describes→create-or-Update (idempotent), `Delete` treats not-found as success; **pure translation** `buildSpec` (cron→`CronExpressions` which Temporal translates to a calendar spec server-side / interval→`Intervals` + `TimeZoneName`), `buildAction` (starts **the same `AgentTaskWorkflow` SubmitTask enqueues** — governance unchanged — with `WorkflowExecutionTimeout=RunTimeout` and Temporal appending the nominal tick to the workflow id for per-tick dedup), `buildInput` (prompt→JSON task input, `Source`/`ScheduleID`/`CreatedBy` provenance), `mapOverlap` (**unknown fails safe to SKIP**). Added additive `AgentTaskInput.Source`/`ScheduleID`/`CreatedBy` (omitempty ⇒ existing histories byte-identical). **Tests:** 22 — core (`ScheduleID`, Reconcile active/paused/absent/not-scheduled/idempotent, `Sweep` error-collection, `BuildDefinition` interval+defaults, `DeriveSessionID` determinism/distinctness, `InterpolatePrompt` tz+no-vars+invalid-tz, `CatchupWindow`, `NextRuns` cron/interval/tz/edge) + adapter (`buildSpec` cron/interval, `buildAction` timeout+input mapping, `buildInput` structured-input, `mapOverlap` incl. fail-safe, create-options/update paused+catchup). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/agentschedule/...`, both suites + `internal/workflow` (additive fields, no regression) green. **Honest scope:** the imperative `Create`/`Update`/`Delete` calls need a live Temporal server (exercised at deploy, same gate as the rest of the Temporal path); the reconciler *logic* + all translation is fully unit-covered via the fake store. Wiring into main.go + the lifecycle hooks that compute `DesiredState` + the startup-sweep invocation are P3.
- [x] ✅ **P1 — `ScheduleSpec` extended + validated + Studio capture fixed (the inert stub is now a real, fail-fast spec).** The manifest schedule was a stub (`Timezone`/`ActiveDays`/`ActiveFrom`/`ActiveUntil`/`TriggerMode`) with **no cadence field**, **no task payload**, and Studio silently **dropped everything but timezone + trigger_mode**. Closed all three. **(1) Domain (`domain/agent.go`)** — additive, back-compatible extension: cadence (`Cron` + `EverySeconds`), the load-bearing **`TaskPrompt`/`Input`** (a scheduled agent must carry its OWN input — the reason "scheduled" was never usable), `Overlap`, **`RunTimeoutMinutes`** (companion A — wall-clock cap, separate from the loop's turn/spend caps, so `Overlap=skip` can't be frozen by a hung run), `CatchUp`, `PauseAfterFailures` (companion B); plus `TriggerMode*`/`ScheduleOverlap*` constants, defaults (`DefaultScheduleRunTimeoutMins=30`, `DefaultSchedulePauseAfterFailures=3`, `MinScheduleIntervalSeconds=60`), and accessors `IsScheduled()` / `EffectiveOverlap()` / `EffectiveRunTimeoutMins()` / `EffectivePauseAfterFailures()`. Zero value = reactive, byte-identical. **(2) Validation (`agent/parser.go` `validateSchedule`)** — fail-fast at load: cron validated with **the exact same `robfig/cron.ParseStandard` the Temporal SDK uses internally** (a cron that loads here is guaranteed accepted at schedule creation — no new dep, promoted the transitive `robfig/cron` to direct), IANA timezone via `time.LoadLocation`, HH:MM active window, weekday allowlist, **exactly one** cadence, the 60s interval floor, `task_prompt` required, overlap allowlist, a half-configured guard (cadence/task set without `trigger_mode: scheduled`), non-negative timeouts. **(3) Studio (`agent/studio.go`)** — `studioSchedule` + the compile block now capture the **full** schedule (cadence, task, window, overlap, timeout, catch-up, pause-after-failures); `CompileStudioManifest` already ends in `validate()`, so the studio path gets the same enforcement. **Tests:** domain `TestScheduleSpec_IsScheduled` + `_EffectiveAccessors`; parser `TestValidateSchedule` (14 cases: valid cron/interval, no-cadence, both-cadences, sub-floor, bad cron, no-task, bad overlap, bad tz, bad window/day, half-configured, negative timeout) + `_ValidWindow`; `TestCompileStudioManifest_FullSchedule` (whole schedule round-trips, `IsScheduled()` true). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/{domain,agent}`, `go mod tidy`, full `internal/{domain,agent}` suites all green.

## Cycle 10 — Voice/edge follow-ups (governed translation · edge WAF · worker WS audio bridge)

Three post-launch follow-ups from the 2026-07-09 voice/edge review, each implemented prod-grade (CLAUDE.md) → tests green → marked ✅ → next. All uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **T1 — Governed real-time translation engine built (the interpreter moat).** New `internal/voice/translate.go` + `translate_openai.go`: a **`GovernedTranslator`** that wraps any pluggable **`ModelTranslator`** with the governance a raw translation model can't offer. **Order is the moat:** glossary pre-mask → PII tokenise → translate → PII detokenise → glossary restore, so the model sees **neither the brand terms nor the sensitive data**. **(1) PII-safe** — names/emails/account-numbers are MAL-tokenised (`PIITokeniser` seam over `mal.Service.TokeniseText`/`Detokenise`) before the third-party translator sees them and restored byte-exact into the target (numbers preserved exactly, sensitive data never egresses). **(2) Glossary / do-not-translate** — `Glossary` (whole-word, case-insensitive, longest-first) masks each term to an opaque `[[G<n>]]` placeholder the model can't alter, then restores it to the REQUIRED target rendering (`target==source` ⇒ brand do-not-translate) — deterministic + model-independent. **(3) Fail-closed leak guard** — a surviving `[[G…]]` placeholder (model dropped a marker) is a governance breach → error, never emitted. **(4) Auditable** — `TranslationAuditor` seam records BOTH source + target transcripts to the spine. **(5) Universal plug-in** — `openAICompatTranslator` serves OpenAI, Gemini/xAI (their OpenAI-compat endpoints), or a **self-hosted vLLM/Seamless gateway (sovereign/air-gapped)** by base-URL swap, temp 0 for faithful/reproducible output, deterministic marker-preserving system prompt. Capability gate `EnforceTranslateCapability` (fail-closed on the manifest `live_translate` flag — the flag was already wired for governance; this is the engine it was waiting for). Size-bounded at the boundary (§5.1). **Tests:** 8 (end-to-end PII-safe+glossary property [model sees neither brand nor PII, output carries both + exact PII], leak guard, validation empty/missing-target/oversized, no-governance passthrough, audits-both-transcripts, capability gate, OpenAI-compat chat shape+marker-survival+ctor validation); `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/voice`, full `internal/voice` suite green. **Honest scope:** the s2s low-latency alternative already exists (`realtime.go` — the caller picks the latency↔governance trade); the STT→translate→multilingual-TTS **audio wiring** rides the worker WS bridge (T3).
- [x] ✅ **T2 — Coraza (OWASP CRS) managed L7 WAF built as an Envoy Gateway Wasm extension.** The architecture-correct WAF for our **Envoy Gateway → NLB** edge (AWS WAFv2 can't attach to an NLB; fronting the latency-sensitive voice/gRPC API with CloudFront+WAF would add a hop): **Coraza + OWASP CRS as a `coraza-proxy-wasm` `EnvoyExtensionPolicy`** that runs INSIDE the Envoy data plane — no extra hop, self-host-portable, no AWS-WAF dependency. R&D via 2026 web search (Envoy Gateway Wasm task + coraza-proxy-wasm config schema): `apiVersion gateway.envoyproxy.io/v1alpha1`, `spec.wasm[]` with `rootID` + `code.type Image|HTTP` + `config.directives_map/default_directives`. **New `helm/actrone/charts/gateway/templates/envoyextensionpolicy-waf.yaml`** + an opt-in `waf:` block in the gateway chart `values.yaml`, targeting the orchestrator REST route **alongside `ext_authz`** (ext_authz owns authN/Z, Coraza owns request-content inspection: SQLi/XSS/RCE/LFI/scanner signatures). **Prod-grade guards (CLAUDE.md §1/§5):** default **off** (the wasm is an external artifact needing pin + on-cluster validation); render **fails closed** when enabled without a wasm source, and when an HTTP source lacks a sha256 (integrity mandatory); supports a **digest-pinned OCI image** (recommended, air-gap/supply-chain) or an HTTPS `.wasm`+sha256; ships **`SecRuleEngine DetectionOnly`** as the safe rollout default (tune CRS false positives before enforcing). **Verify:** `helm template` across 5 cases (off→no policy · enabled-no-source→fail · http→renders · image→`type: Image` · http-no-sha256→fail) + `helm lint` all green. Deployment runbook updated with the 4-step rollout (pin → DetectionOnly + `--dry-run=server` rootID/CRD validation → tune → `SecRuleEngine On`). **Honest gate:** minting the pinned wasm artifact + the live-cluster Wasm/CRS validation are the deploy steps over this now-built + lint-validated chart (the same external-artifact gate as the harness base images). Sources: [Envoy Gateway Wasm](https://gateway.envoyproxy.io/docs/tasks/extensibility/wasm/) · [coraza-proxy-wasm](https://github.com/corazawaf/coraza-proxy-wasm).
- [x] ✅ **T3 — Worker real-time WS audio bridge built (the irreducible real-time edge, governed).** New `internal/voice/bridge.go` + `bridge_provider.go`: a **`RealtimeBridge`** that connects a caller leg (telephony/browser — Twilio Media Streams / LiveKit) to a speech-to-speech provider WebSocket (OpenAI/Grok realtime), **governed end-to-end**. **Seam-based (testable without a live socket):** `ProviderConn` (JSON event Send/Receive), `CallerAudio` (μ-law `Inbound()` channel + `Play`/`Clear`/`Close`), `ToolInvoker` (the governance boundary). **Bidirectional pump** via `errgroup`: caller frames → `input_audio_buffer.append` (base64); provider audio → `caller.Play`, handling **both the GA `response.output_audio.delta` and beta `response.audio.delta`** names (2026 web-verified — GA renamed the audio events + dropped the beta header) for provider/version skew; `input_audio_buffer.speech_started` → `caller.Clear()` (**barge-in**). **The moat holds in s2s mode:** the session is pushed with ONLY the governed allowlisted tools (from `RealtimeSession.SessionConfig`), and **every model tool call is routed through the `ToolInvoker` (Tool-Call Supervisor) — the bridge NEVER executes a tool itself**; a nil supervisor or a supervisor error returns a **governed error `function_call_output`** (fail-closed — the model is told the action was refused, never silently performed), then `response.create` to continue. Clean teardown on hangup/cancel (caller-hangup + ctx.Canceled are ordinary end-of-call, not errors); always closes the provider conn. **Concrete transports:** `gorillaProviderConn`/`DialProvider` (dials the realtime WS from a `RealtimeSession`, GA no-beta-header, write-serialised per gorilla's single-writer rule) + `TwilioCallerAudio` (adapts the existing `TwilioStream` onto `CallerAudio`, jitter-buffered, drop-past-teardown to never deadlock the read loop). **Tests:** 8 (audio both directions + session.update pushed first · beta audio-delta name · barge-in clears · **tool call routed through supervisor → function_call_output+response.create** · **tool refused fail-closed without supervisor** · provider-error ends bridge · ctor validation) — channel-synchronised, no sleeps; `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/voice`, full `internal/voice` suite green. **⇒ Voice is now end-to-end: STT/TTS registries (2026-07-09) + s2s realtime provider + governed translation (T1) + this real-time audio bridge.** **Honest scope:** the LiveKit `CallerAudio` adapter is the same seam (a track-reader impl) and the `/twilio/stream` WS-upgrade route + worker wiring are the deployment bring-up over this now-built + tested core — mirrors the existing streaming-STT note. Sources: [OpenAI Realtime GA](https://developers.openai.com/api/docs/guides/realtime) · [Realtime server events](https://developers.openai.com/api/reference/resources/realtime/server-events).

## Cycle 9 — Section-B leftovers (from the 2026-07-05 six-agent audit → driving each genuinely-leftover item inside an otherwise-implemented plan to prod-grade + tests)

Source of truth: `memory/plan-status-audit-2026-07-05.md` §B. Work top-down; each item = implement (prod-grade, CLAUDE.md) → tests green → mark ✅ here → next. All uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **B1 — Loop LE7: SemanticPruner wired into the live loop.** `ContextManager.Retrieve` phase-4 now admits episodic memories via `semanticAdmitMemories` → `SemanticPruner.Prune` (relevance-ranked under the episodic budget, **never dropping a `summary`/`commit_relevant`-class memory** — `DefaultNeverPruneClasses`, overridable via `ContextManagerConfig.NeverPruneClasses`), replacing the order-based `pruneMemories`. Rank-derived importance (`len-i`) makes the pruner's stable sort reproduce Qdrant's relevance order, so with no protected class it's byte-identical to the old greedy pass (existing `pruneMemories` tests unchanged). `pruneMemories` retained as the no-protection base case. **Tests:** 3 new (`TestSemanticAdmitMemories_*`: summary survives over budget · protected+fitting-prunable · nil-neverPrune==greedy) + `ExposedSemanticAdmitMemories`; `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/service`, full `internal/service` suite all green. *(LE7 now has BOTH halves live: retrieval-side commit-protecting admission [this] + the already-wired pairing-safe message-window `elideStaleToolResults`.)*
- [x] ✅ **B2 — Loop LE2: governed compaction now ENACTED + receipt recorded to the audit spine.** The `RetrieveContext` activity no longer discards `Plan()`'s `compactable` set: new `compactEpisodic` (pure, in `context_manager.go`) replaces the compactable **episodic** memories with one bounded extractive summary while keeping preserved (pinned / never-compact-class) memories verbatim — session turns are never compacted here (recent conversation stays intact; WriteBack summariser owns long-session). `retrieved.EpisodicMemories`/`TotalTokensUsed` are updated by the reclaimed tokens, so downstream injection is genuinely leaner. The receipt is now written via `recordCompactionReceipt` to the tenant's **HMAC-chained tamper-evident audit spine** (`auditSpine.Append`, `event_type=loop.compaction.receipt`, full receipt JSON payload) — the auditable GAL edge the plan specifies — not merely a `zap.Info` (log retained for observability; append is best-effort, never fails the task). Added `TenantID`+`TaskID` to `RetrieveContextInput` (additive; populated **only** inside the existing `loop.CompactionEnabled()` block ⇒ non-loop agents' activity input stays byte-identical, replay-safe). **Tests:** 3 new (`TestCompactEpisodic_*`: replaces-with-summary+reclaims · <2-compactable no-op · session-source ignored); `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/{service,workflow}` suites all green.

Pure decision/apply helpers extracted (`oauthNeedsRefresh`, `applyRefreshedToken`, `isInvalidGrant`) + injectable persist/mark seams for testability. **Tests:** 9 (pure helpers table-driven + `EnsureFreshToken` end-to-end via httptest token endpoint: non-oauth passthrough · still-valid-no-refresh · refresh-expired→rotated-token-persisted · invalid_grant→needs_reauth+ERR_CONNECTOR_REAUTH); `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/{connector,connectorgov,connectortool}` suites all green. **Honest scope:** the token-refresh lifecycle (the actual gap) is complete + wired + tested; the browser **authorize→callback** connect endpoint (`GET/POST /v1/connectors/oauth`) that mints the FIRST token is thin remaining UX over already-built mcphub primitives (`ExchangeCode` + `OAuthStateStore`) — connectors created with refresh material already self-refresh today.
- [x] ✅ **B8 — Connector rate-limit retry + pagination/incremental-sync cursor.** **Rate-limit + transient-failure resilience (CLAUDE.md §4.4, the named centerpiece):** `CustomRESTAdapter.Fetch` now sends through `doFetchWithRetry` — retries on **429 (honouring the provider's `Retry-After`: delta-seconds OR HTTP-date)** and **5xx** with bounded exponential backoff + **full jitter** (`maxFetchAttempts=3`, `baseRetryDelay=250ms`, `maxRetryDelay=20s`), body-less so safe to resend, context-deadline-respecting; a 4xx (≠429) returns immediately (no wasted retries). **Pagination + incremental sync:** `ConnectorResponse.NextCursor` now exposes the provider's next-page token parsed from the RFC 5988 `Link rel="next"` header (`parseNextLink`), so the agent/poller pages through results and fetches only what's new since the last cursor (caller-driven, fits the flattened-response model — full server-side list-merge would need an array response-model rework, noted). **Tests:** 7 (`parseRetryAfter` seconds/date/past/garbage · `retryBackoff` bounded+jittered · `doFetchWithRetry` retries-then-200 / exhausts-on-5xx / 4xx-immediate · `parseNextLink`); `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/connector` suite green.

- [x] ✅ **B26 — Code-Bundle B4: TS/Node harness builds implemented (the core of B4).** The `internal/build` pipeline shipped **Python-only** ("Node/TS lands in B4"); built the Node/TS leg end-to-end, mirroring the Python path's purity/determinism. **(1) Detector** (`detect.go`) — new `LanguageNode` (a TS source is `LanguageNode`; the Node harness base runs it, no separate compile Language) + 4 dep managers with lockfile-driven install recipes: `package-lock.json`→`DepNpmCI`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`→`DepPnpm`, `yarn.lock`→`DepYarn`, bare `package.json`→`DepNpmInstall`. **Precedence** is deterministic: a Python *manifest* at root wins (mixed repo builds as Python) → else `package.json`→Node → else loose `.py`→Python DepNone → else `ErrUnsupportedSource`; a nested `frontend/package.json` never hijacks a root Python build; `package.json` is the *required* Node marker (loose `.js` alone is unsupported). **(2) Dockerfile** (`dockerfile.go`) — Node branch: copies the manifest **pair** (package.json + lockfile) for a clean dep-layer cache, runs the lockfile-exact install (`npm ci --omit=dev` / `pnpm --frozen-lockfile --prod` / `yarn --frozen-lockfile --production`) with a BuildKit **cache mount** + an optional **secret mount for a private-registry .npmrc** (id=dep_index_auth, mounted at /root/.npmrc — never baked into a layer, §6), then `ENV NODE_ENV=production`. Reproducibility = lockfile-exact installs + digest-pinned base + BuildKit (the Node counterpart to Python's `--require-hashes`). **(3) Language-aware base** — `PlanInput.BaseImageNode` + config `HarnessBaseImageNode` (optional; digest-pin-validated when set) threaded through `buildflow.StoreConfig` + `main.go`; `PlanBuild` selects the base by detected language and **fails a Node upload with a clear 422 when no Node base is configured — never silently falls back to the Python base** (which would produce an unrunnable image). **Tests:** `TestDetectNode` (7 cases: each lockfile, precedence, nesting), `TestRenderDockerfile_NodeNpmCI` / `_NodePnpmYarnAndNpmInstall` / `_NodeDeterministic`, `TestPlanBuild_NodeSource` (base selection + missing-Node-base rejection); the existing Python + "unsupported" cases updated/kept; `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/build`+`config`+`buildflow` suites green. **Honest scope (separate tracks, NOT built here):** the Cloud Native Buildpacks *alternative builder tier* is a distinct Builder strategy (pack/kpack), and build-cluster **region-pinning** is an infra property of the build data plane (tied to the residency plane, [[plan-status-audit-2026-07-04]] §regionpool) — neither is needed for the harness-Dockerfile path, which is the primary and is now Node-complete. The **Node harness base image itself** (published `harness-base-node@sha256:…`) is an external artifact gate, like the Python base.
- [x] ✅ **B27 — CodeArtifact pull-through dependency mirror: build wiring + terraform module built.** The plan (§246) shipped B1 allowlisted-egress as the bootstrap and left "swap to CodeArtifact" for later; there was a `dep_index_auth` secret but **no index-URL wiring** (and the Python secret mount had no target, so pip couldn't even read the credential — a latent gap). Closed both. **(1) In-code mirror wiring** (`internal/build`) — new `DockerfilePlan.PyPIIndexURL` / `NodeRegistryURL`; when set, pip installs get `--index-url <url>` and npm/pnpm/yarn get `--registry <url>`, so builds resolve through the in-region cache instead of the raw public internet; empty = default public index (B1 bootstrap, unchanged). **Fixed the auth mount:** the `dep_index_auth` secret now mounts at `/root/.netrc` (pip) and `/root/.npmrc` (npm) so the mirror's short-lived bearer token is actually consumed — never baked into a layer (§6). Threaded through config (`build.dep_pypi_index_url` / `dep_node_registry_url`, **https-validated when set**, §5.4) → `buildflow.StoreConfig` → `PlanInput` → `PlanBuild`. **(2) Terraform module** (`infra/terraform/modules/eks/build-dependency-mirror.tf`) — an opt-in (`enable_dependency_mirror`, default false) CodeArtifact **domain** (KMS-encrypted, rotation on) with two pull-through repos (`public:pypi` + `public:npmjs` external connections), a **least-privilege read policy** (GetAuthorizationToken on the domain + sts:GetServiceBearerToken scoped to codeartifact + ReadFromRepository/GetRepositoryEndpoint on the two repos — no publish/admin), and **outputs** that hand the exact `dep_pypi_index_url` / `dep_node_registry_url` / read-policy ARN to wire into the orchestrator + build-pod IRSA. Pairs with the existing `build_allow_public_egress=false` for the no-public-internet build mode. **Tests/verify:** `TestRenderDockerfile_DependencyMirror` (pip `--index-url` + `.netrc` mount, npm `--registry`, empty→no override); `go build ./...`, `internal/build`+`config`+`buildflow` suites green; `terraform validate` (prod env, includes the new module) **Success**. **Honest gate:** `terraform apply` + minting/rotating the CodeArtifact token into the `dep_index_auth` secret at build time are live-AWS/deploy steps, per §A/§C.
- [x] ✅ **B28 — B2 OCI referrer-digest surfacing implemented.** `BuildOutcome.ProvenanceRef`/`SBOMRef` were filled with predicate-TYPE placeholder strings (`https://slsa.dev/provenance/v1`) even though `domain.Bundle` documents them as "the OCI referrer digests." Closed the gap so they carry the **concrete referrer manifest digests** (sha256:…) — verifiable pointers a consumer can pull, not just labels. **New seam** (`builder.go`): `Referrer{ArtifactType, PredicateType, Digest}`, `ReferrerResolver.Referrers(ctx, repo, digest)` (queries the pushed image digest's OCI referrers), `NoReferrerResolver` (returns none, never errors), and a pure `classifyReferrers` that picks the provenance + SBOM digests — preferring the reliable `PredicateType` discriminator (both attestations are in-toto), falling back to `ArtifactType` substring (slsa/provenance vs spdx/sbom/cyclonedx), first-match-wins, skips empty/signature referrers. **Wired** into `K8sBuilder` (new `referrers` field + `NewBuilder` param) — after the mandatory image-digest resolve, when attestations are on it queries referrers and sets `ProvenanceRef`/`SBOMRef` to the classified digests. **Fail-OPEN** (correct severity split): the image digest is the authoritative pin and the Rekor signature already gates promotion, so a referrers-API error is logged (`build.referrers.unavailable`) and leaves the digests empty — it never fails an otherwise-good build (contrast the digest resolver, which is fail-CLOSED). The digests then flow unchanged through the existing `buildflow.Store` → `domain.Bundle.ProvenanceRef/SBOMRef` → DB path. Removed the now-dead predicate-type constants. **Tests:** `TestClassifyReferrers` (predicate/artifact matching, first-wins, empty-skip, nil), `TestK8sBuilder_Build_SurfacesReferrerDigests`, `_ReferrerErrorFailsOpen`, `_NoReferrerResolverLeavesDigestsEmpty`, and the updated `_PopulatesSupplyChainEvidence`; `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/build`+`buildflow` suites green. **Honest gate:** the concrete **ECR-backed `ReferrerResolver`** (the live registry `/v2/<repo>/referrers/<digest>` client) is the external/live-registry piece — main.go wires `NoReferrerResolver{}` today (fail-open, empty digests), exactly like the digest resolver was a seam until its ECR client landed.
- [x] ✅ **B29 — Certification P4: in-repo ISMS scaffolding built; the auditor/observation-window remainder is org-not-code by design.** The plan (`Actrone_Certification_Track_Plan.md` §0/§5/§7) is emphatic that **P4 — engaging an accredited auditor, writing the real ISMS, the months-long observation window — is an ORGANISATIONAL commitment that code cannot grant** ("the code side of the certification track is complete"). So B29's honest in-repo piece is the one concrete gap that *supports* the ISMS: the `EvidenceAttested` doc-comment promised attested controls are "tracked with an owner + **cadence**," but there was **no cadence in the model** — management review (ISO 27001 §9.3) was only prose. Built it. **(1) Model** (`catalog.go`) — new `ReviewCadence` vocabulary (`annual`/`semiannual`/`quarterly`) + `cadenceInterval` mapping; `Control.ReviewCadence` field, populated on all 5 attested controls (privacy-notice, incident-response, risk-management, isms-policies-governance, vendor-management → annual). **(2) Projection** (`isms.go`) — `ReviewStatus(lastReviewed, now) → ISMSReviewStatus`: folds the *static* cadence (catalog policy) with *operational* last-reviewed dates (caller-supplied — never hardcoded, since "when was it last reviewed" is live state, not reference data) into a per-control `current`/`overdue`/`missing` verdict + NextDue + roll-up counts, deterministically sorted. Makes an overdue management review a first-class queryable signal instead of buried prose. **(3) Invariant test** — the catalog now *enforces* the promise: every attested control has a valid cadence, no auto control does. **Tests:** `TestCatalogInvariant_AttestedControlsHaveCadence`, `TestReviewStatus` (missing/overdue/current/zero-time, catalog-driven counts, sort determinism), `TestCadenceInterval`; `go build ./...`, `go vet`, `internal/compliance` suite green. **Honest boundary (explicit, per the plan):** the live ISMS *register* (the operational store of last-reviewed dates) + the endpoint that surfaces `ReviewStatus`, and above all the **accredited-auditor engagement + observation window**, are organisational/process work — they cannot and must not be fabricated in code. The projection is ready to consume the register the moment the org stands it up.

*(Honest gate notes: B19 physical EU plane, B29 external audit, and the ALB↔Envoy fronting reconciliation are partly infra/process-gated — the in-repo code portion is built + tested here, the live apply / external process is flagged, per the §A/§C distinction.)*

## Cycle 8 — SDK-strategy plans executed (Go SDK removal · Python SDK extraction · TS memory parity · loop engineering · stepwise expansion)

Implementing the five 2026-07-05 SDK-strategy/loop plan docs to prod-grade, each with tests. All uncommitted (owner commits).

- ✅ **Go SDK Removal Plan — DONE (`docs/Actrone_Go_SDK_Removal_Plan.md`).** The Go client SDK is deleted and the client-SDK matrix is now **TS + Python** (CLAUDE.md §0.1 parity rule updated 3→2). **Phase 1 (the hard blocker — CLI depended on the SDK): the CLI is now fully self-contained.** New `actrone-cli/internal/client` package carries the SDK's transport hygiene verbatim (bounded timeout, exponential-backoff-with-jitter retries on network/5xx/429, `sony/gobreaker` circuit breaker, `X-Request-Id` + `Idempotency-Key` + `X-Actrone-Env` propagation — CLAUDE.md §4.4/§5) plus every endpoint the 6 `cmd/*.go` files use (tasks submit/get + WS tail, agents list/get, deployments list/promote/rollback/canary, bundles, model-endpoints list/create/delete, env-promotion + `DiffFromError`). Dropped the `go-playground/validator` dep in favour of inline checks (leaner binary). The 6 command files + `root.go` rewired from `actrone.*` → `client.*`; `go.mod` `replace`/`require` for the SDK removed. **16 new `httptest`-driven client tests** (header propagation, env-header omitted-when-empty, idempotency-key echo, retry-on-503-then-200, no-retry-on-400, 404→IsNotFound, 409 promote-gate→`DiffFromError`, canary start/stop, DELETE 204, BYOK create validation, list unwrap, pipeline parse, APIError format). `go build ./... && go vet ./... && go test ./...` all green (race detector needs cgo — unavailable here; the CLI ships `CGO_ENABLED=0` static so it's dev-only). **Phase 1.6 — CLI packaging (was NO distribution pipeline):** `.goreleaser.yaml` (v2 schema; matrix `{linux,darwin,windows}×{amd64,arm64}`, `CGO_ENABLED=0`, `-trimpath`, version-stamp ldflags into `cmd.version`, tar.gz/zip archives, sha256 checksums, syft SBOMs, cosign keyless signing; **`homebrew_casks` not the deprecated `brews`** — confirmed via web search that `brews` is deprecated since goreleaser v2.10; + `scoops`) + `.github/workflows/release-cli.yml` (tag `v*`, `id-token` for cosign) + `scripts/install.sh` (the `get.actrone.com` script — OS/arch detect, GitHub-release download, **sha256 checksum verification**, POSIX `sh -n` clean) + `README.md` (install matrix + usage). *(goreleaser/shellcheck not installed here → `goreleaser check` + shellcheck lint are CI-gated; YAML + `sh -n` validated locally.)* **Phase 2 — deleted `actrone-go/`** (nothing else imported it: verified no other Go module requires/replaces it, orchestrator is a separate module). *(Honest note: the delete destroyed 10 untracked/unpushed files — assurance/connectors/evals/gal/systems + tests; the 24 tracked files remain in `origin/main`. The explicit repeated directive was "delete entirely / not part of the family".)* **Phase 3 — scrubbed references:** CLAUDE.md §0.1 (Go row removed + "REMOVED" callout, done-tense), shipped SDK source comments (`actrone-ts/src/errors.ts` + `types/events.ts`, `backend/src/actrone/client.py` — no longer imply a Go sibling), user-facing docs (`backend/docs/11-publishing.md` Go publish section removed, `development-plan.md` + `actrone-sysdesign-v3.md` layer/install snippets → TS). Residual mentions live only in historical ASCII architecture diagrams + `docs/*Plan*.md` build logs (descriptive/point-in-time; the authoritative CLAUDE.md governs). **Phase 4 — verified:** `grep` for `github.com/actrone/actrone-go` across all `.go/.ts/.py` returns zero; `actrone-go/` no longer exists; CLI builds/tests green. Memory ([[sdk-locations]], [[sdk-strategy-plans]], [[loop-engineering-plan]], MEMORY.md) updated to TS+Python.

- ✅ **Python SDK Repo Extraction Plan — DONE (`docs/Actrone_Python_SDK_Repo_Extraction_Plan.md`).** The Python SDK is out of the backend and now lives at top-level **`actrone-py/`** (sibling of `actrone-ts/` — closes the long-standing "[[sdk-locations]] Python SDK is NOT top-level" foot-gun). - ✅ **Stepwise Framework Expansion Plan — DONE (all 3 waves).** Wave A ✅ (6 Python drivers), Wave C ✅ (TS D3), **Wave B ✅ (B1 stepwise driver + the JS boundary spike + B2 per-framework TS shims + B3 dispatch).** `docs/Actrone_Stepwise_Framework_Expansion_Plan.md` = Wave A (6 more Python stepwise drivers) + Wave B (port the harness+stepwise to TS — the hard JS-`BaseException` problem) + Wave C (D3 tool/handoff governance in the 5 TS adapters). **Wave A DONE — all six governed model shims built** in `actrone-py/src/actrone/harness/frameworks/`, each following the proven shipped pattern (lazy framework import so the module loads without it; pure inbound converter; route each turn through `sctx.model`/`sctx.model_sync`; map the governed boundary → the framework's native result incl. tool calls): **`langchain.py`** (`BaseChatModel` `_generate`/`_agenerate` → `AIMessage.tool_calls`), **`llamaindex.py`** (`CustomLLM` `chat`/`achat`/`complete` → `ChatResponse`, tool_calls in `additional_kwargs`), **`pydantic_ai.py`** (`Model.request` → `ModelResponse` with `TextPart`+`ToolCallPart`), **`semantic_kernel.py`** (`ChatCompletionClientBase._inner_get_chat_message_contents` → `ChatMessageContent` with `FunctionCallContent`), **`dspy.py`** (sync `BaseLM.__call__` → `list[str]` via the sync boundary — no tool channel, text path like CrewAI), **`google_adk.py`** (`BaseLlm.generate_content_async` async-generator → single `LlmResponse` with `FunctionCall` parts). **Signatures R&D-confirmed via 2026 web search** (per the CrewAI-drift lesson) — caught real drift: DSPy moved to typed contracts (→ override the `BaseLM.__call__` compatibility boundary), SK uses `_inner_get_chat_message_contents`, ADK `generate_content_async` yields an async generator. Exported from `@actrone/sdk/adapters`; flipped the adapters-index + `cursor.ts` "D3 (later)" notes to "implemented"; **handoffs** are governed server-side via the existing `client.runSystem` (one governed unit + signed handoff ledger), so no separate client trust method is needed. **6 D3 govern tests** (execute routes via `callTool`; supervise authorises-then-runs-local; block path does NOT run the local tool/action; batch sanitises names) with a fake governing client; **full actrone-ts suite 91/91, typecheck + build clean.** **✅ Wave B core (B1 + B3) NOW DONE — the plan's highest-risk item, the JS boundary-propagation spike, is SOLVED + PROVEN.** New `actrone-ts/src/harness/stepwise.ts` — the TS twin of the Python stepwise driver (replay-with-memoization: checkpoint = ordered resolved-boundary log; re-run the author loop feeding memoized results to `await sctx.model(...)`/`sctx.tool`/`sctx.memory*`/`sctx.approval` until the first unresolved boundary). **The central problem — JS has no `BaseException`, so a framework's bare `catch (e)` could swallow the thrown boundary and break durability — is solved with the plan's option 1: a branded non-`Error` `BoundarySignal` + a pending-boundary LATCH.** The moment a boundary is raised the context sets `pending`; even if the framework's `catch` swallows the throw and the loop returns normally, the driver inspects `pending` and recovers the boundary (the normal return is NOT mistaken for `done`), and any subsequent boundary call re-throws immediately so no further author work runs. **B3: the harness activity now dispatches `strategy==="stepwise"` → `StepwiseDriver`** (else encapsulated); exported from `@actrone/sdk/harness`; module note flipped from "Python flagship only" to "at parity." **Tests: `test/harness-stepwise.test.ts` — 10 tests incl. boundary-surfaces, replay→done, multi-turn crash-resume (fresh driver+context per step), the DECISIVE `except`-survival test (framework swallows the throw → driver STILL returns `model_request` via the latch, and the swallow-return is not mistaken for the final answer), the short-circuit test (second boundary call after a pending one does no work), budget-exhausted wrap-up, real-error propagation, and tool/memory/approval request shapes. Full actrone-ts suite 101/101, typecheck + build clean.** **✅ B2 (per-framework TS internal-loop model shims) NOW DONE — Wave B (and Plan 5) complete.** New `actrone-ts/src/harness/frameworks/`: shared `_common.ts` (`normalizeLoopMessages` + `splitBoundary`, mirroring the Python `_common`) + `openai-agents.ts` `makeOpenAIAgentsModel(sctx)` (structural `Model.getResponse(request) → ModelResponse{usage, output}`: assistant message + `function_call` items — signature R&D-confirmed via 2026 web search) + `vercel.ts` `makeVercelModel(sctx)` (structural AI-SDK-v5 `LanguageModelV2`: `specificationVersion:"v2"`, `doGenerate` → `content` with `text` + `type:"tool-call"` parts, `supportedUrls` — v5 shape confirmed via web search) with `makeMastraModel` = the same (Mastra consumes AI-SDK models). Peer-dep-free (structural — nothing imported at runtime, builds/tests with none installed), exported from `@actrone/sdk/harness`. **Tests: `test/harness-frameworks.test.ts` — 8 tests: the pure converters (`normalizeLoopMessages`/`splitBoundary`/`agentsInputToLoop`/`vercelPromptToLoop`) + each shim's boundary→native-result mapping driven through a seeded `StepwiseContext` (text + tool-call cases). Full actrone-ts suite 109/109, typecheck + build clean.** (Real-SDK integration validation of the exact v5/Agents shapes is peer-dep-gated, mirroring the Python `importorskip` shims.)

## Cycle 7 — external-artifact & framework gates closed to prod-grade (browser pool service, requirement gate 14/14, stepwise per-framework drivers) + consolidated deploy runbook

The Cycle-6 remainders that were genuinely-external ("needs a Playwright service", "needs the SDK installed") are now built to the codebase bar. All uncommitted.

- ✅ **Browser pool service — BUILT (`backend/browserpool/`, FastAPI + Playwright).** The live headless-browsing service the orchestrator's `browser` tool leases from — closes the last external gate for the governed browser tool. Contract-bound to `internal/browser/httpmanager.go` (`POST /sessions` → `{session_id}`, `POST /sessions/{id}/navigate` → `{content,...}`, `DELETE /sessions/{id}`, bearer auth). Prod-grade: one Chromium/worker + isolated `new_context()` per session (~10ms, no disk writes, purge-on-close); bounded pool (`max_sessions`→429) + background sweeper reaping idle/expired sessions (a crashed orchestrator can't leak a live context+creds); **re-enforced egress allowlist + SSRF guard** (deny-all default, blocks private/loopback/link-local/metadata IP literals + `localhost`, scheme allowlist) as defence-in-depth behind the orchestrator's own gate; structured domain errors → deterministic HTTP (429/404/403/400/502, 5xx never leaks); `/health/live|ready` + Prometheus `/metrics`; graceful shutdown; typed `BROWSERPOOL_*` config, fail-fast. `BrowserBackend` Protocol seam (real `PlaywrightBrowserBackend` + in-memory `FakeBrowserBackend`) → **56 unit tests pass** with no browser installed (allowlist/pool/api/config); real-Chromium integration tests (`-m integration`, local-server, cookie-isolation + purge-on-close) **verified locally (3 pass)** + run in the multi-stage Dockerfile (Playwright base image). README + docker-compose. **Infra: new `infra/helm/actrone/charts/browserpool` Helm chart** (Deployment non-root `pwuser` + read-only-rootfs + memory-backed `/dev/shm`+`/tmp` for Chromium + bearer-secret ref + drain; Service; HPA on cpu+mem; PDB; ServiceMonitor; **egress NetworkPolicy** = network-layer SSRF backstop, ingress-from-orchestrator-only + egress-to-public-except-private-CIDRs; `helm lint` clean, all 6 kinds render). **CI + GitOps deploy fully wired:** `backend/.github/workflows/ci-browserpool.yml` (unit + Docker `test`-stage real-Chromium + `publish` to GHCR) now **dispatches `repository_dispatch` → infra `image-bump`** → opens a `values.images.yaml` bump PR (browserpool added to the `# svc:` sentinel block + `bump-image.sh` allowlist + `image-bump.yml` choices — bump script tested). **Chart registered in the umbrella** (`helm/actrone` dependency `condition: browserpool.enabled`, `values.yaml` + `values.images.yaml` blocks; `helm lint` clean, renders with the GitOps tag; also fixed a pre-existing lint failure by declaring the earlier `egress-proxy` chart as a condition-gated dep). **Auth secret authored** — `k8s/browserpool/10-eso-browserpool-secret.example.yaml` (ESO materialises `browserpool-auth`/`auth-token`). **Cluster prereqs already met** (vpc-cni `enableNetworkPolicy=true`; ServiceMonitor is a platform assumption). Remaining = per-env `enabled=true` flip + create the SM secret + set orchestrator config. Deploy = runbook §6.3.
- ✅ **Requirement Gate — now 14/14 kinds wired (was 11/14).** Analyzed the authoritative stores and wired the last three in `PgPresence` (parameterised `EXISTS`): **environment** → `org_environment` (status ≠ 'locked'; env-name aliases normalised; empty=any non-dev configured rung), **approver** → `org_approval_policy` (mode ≠ 'direct' = step-up chain engaged), **credential** → `custom_connectors` with a sealed `vault_ref` for the ref (name or id). *(This overturns the Cycle-6 note that these were "trivially-true / no authoritative table" — they each have one: environments carry a real locked/unlocked/active status; the approval-policy row is authoritative; the connector vault_ref is the sealed-credential source of truth.)* Interface-assertion + pure-helper unit tests (`presence_test.go`: `canonicalEnv` table + empty-ref short-circuit) pass; DSN-gated integration test extended (all 3 kinds absent for a fresh org); orchestrator builds; `go vet -tags=integration` clean.
- ✅ **Stepwise per-framework drivers — BUILT for OpenAI-Agents / AutoGen / CrewAI (closes the Cycle-6 INTEGRATION GATE).** Web-R&D'd the current (2026) exact APIs from each framework's official docs, then built the framework-native governed model shims in new `harness/frameworks/`: `make_openai_agents_model(sctx)` (subclasses `agents.Model`, maps the governed boundary → a `ResponseOutputMessage`+`ResponseFunctionToolCall` `ModelResponse`), `make_autogen_client(sctx)` (subclasses `ChatCompletionClient` incl. all required members — `create`/`create_stream`/`close`/usage/token-count/`model_info` — returns `CreateResult` with `FunctionCall`s), `make_crewai_llm(sctx)` (subclasses `BaseLLM`, **sync** `call` via the new `sctx.model_sync` boundary). **Durability-critical hardening:** `_BoundarySignal` now subclasses `BaseException` (not `Exception`) so a framework's `except Exception` retry/tracing wrapper cannot swallow a boundary — proven by two unit tests (async + sync hostile handlers). Pure converters (`_common.py` + `agents_input_to_loop` + `autogen_messages_to_loop`) are framework-free and **unit-tested (24 new tests)**; the framework-bound factories are `importorskip` integration-tested against the real SDKs (`tests/integration/test_harness_frameworks_integration.py`) and excluded from coverage/strict-mypy like the other optional-dep adapters. mypy clean on the harness package (13 files); 45 harness unit tests pass. **All THREE frameworks verified end-to-end against the real installed SDKs (OpenAI-Agents + AutoGen on py3.14; CrewAI 1.15.1 on py3.12) — each drives a real agent/Runner through the real stepwise driver and the model call surfaces as a governed boundary.** Real-SDK testing caught a genuine bug the docs-based impl would have shipped: CrewAI 1.x's `LLM.call()` passes extra kwargs (`from_task`/`from_agent`/`response_model`) beyond the documented signature → shim's `call()` now takes `**kwargs`. ## Cycle 5 — CLAUDE.md compliance fixes (self-audit found 3 real violations in my own session work)

Having CLAUDE.md in context ≠ gating on it. A §12 pre-ship pass found three concrete rule breaks I shipped and fixed:

- ✅ **Egress proxy forward path had NO outbound timeout** (§5.4 "no open-ended waits"). Fixed: bounded default transport (dial 15s / TLS 15s / response-header 60s / idle 90s) + a `forwardTimeout` (10m, generous so long governed-gateway streams aren't cut, never open-ended) via `context.WithTimeout` on the forwarded request. (A forward proxy correctly does NOT retry the client's request — that would duplicate writes.)
- ✅ **Browser `HTTPSessionManager` had no retry/backoff+jitter or circuit breaker** (§4.4). Fixed: wrapped pool calls in `sony/gobreaker` + bounded exponential-backoff-with-full-jitter retry (max 3; 5xx/network retried, 4xx not), mirroring `mediaguard/remote.go`. New tests: retries-2×503-then-200, does-not-retry-4xx.
- ✅ **Egress proxy exposed no `/metrics`** (§6.3). Fixed: `internal/egressproxy/metrics.go` (bounded-label `egressproxy_decisions_total{outcome,posture}` + `_forward_duration_seconds` + `_forward_errors_total`, promauto, mirroring `extauthz`), recorded in the handler, served on the health port (never the proxy port).
- Full build + egressproxy/browser tests green after the fixes.

**Residual-gap follow-up (fixed what was fixable now):**

- ✅ **#3 Egress logs now carry a correlation id** (§6.3) — `AuditSink.RecordEgress` gains `correlationID`; `correlationFrom(r)` extracts the W3C `traceparent` trace-id (else `X-Request-Id`); logged as `trace_id`. Test: extracts from traceparent + falls back to X-Request-Id.
- ✅ **#1 (partial) PgIdentityStore encoding verified without a DB** — extracted `encodePublicKey`/`decodePublicKey` (+ wrong-length guard) with a plain unit test (round-trip + malformed + wrong-length); statically verified the query columns match migration 00031 exactly. *(Executing the SQL against live Postgres still needs a DB — no DSN/docker in this env — so the DSN-gated integration test remains unexecuted here.)*
- ✅ **#2 `governedsystem`/`browser.Service` — RESOLVED in Cycle 6.** Both are now reachable: GovernedSystem via `POST /v1/systems/run` + concrete adapters + 3 SDKs; the governed browser tool via the Supervisor `browser` router + `browserPolicyResolver` (in-repo complete; external Playwright pool is the runtime dependency).

- ✅ **GovernedSystem — production `AgentInvoker` built (closes the audit's named gap "no production invoker"); durable runtime reclassified as Tier-C.** New `internal/governedsystem/taskinvoker.go` `TaskInvoker` — the real (non-fake) executor: resolves a system agent ref → deployed agent id (`AgentResolver` seam) and runs a governed platform task carrying prompt + upstream outputs + purpose + trace id (`TaskRunner` seam), returning the output. Composes cleanly with the Cycle-3 `Coordinator` (compile-asserted). Tests: `taskinvoker_test.go` 5/5 (resolve+run+thread, omit-empty, error propagation, unconfigured-reject, coordinator-composition). **Honest reclassification:** wiring the concrete `AgentResolver`/`TaskRunner` adapters over the real agent repo + task-submit + Temporal poll, adding a `SystemAgent → platform agent` domain link, and an HTTP/Temporal entry that makes a multi-agent system runnable — that is the durable-runtime **feature** (Tier-C), not a wiring gap. My "deployment-gated" label for GovernedSystem was wrong.
- ✅ **Inter-agent trust — verification side wired & reachable (Tier-B part done); minting reclassified as Tier-C feature.** Built `internal/trust/pgstore.go` `PgIdentityStore` (durable IdentityStore over `agent_identities`, migration 00031; DSN-gated integration test) and wired it in main: `a2aRegistry.WithDelegation(trust.NewRegistry(NewPgIdentityStore(pool), log), cfg.MCP.RequireAgentDelegation)` — so `Registry.Execute` now verifies a delegation token against the durable registry before any cross-agent dispatch. New config flag `MCP.RequireAgentDelegation` (OFF by default). Build + trust/a2a/config tests green. **Honest reclassification:** the *minting* side (agent-key Ed25519 generation + vault sealing at agent registration + per-dispatch token signing + threading into the call context) is a genuine multi-part **feature**, not wiring — it must be built before `RequireAgentDelegation` can be turned on (else all A2A calls block). That is Tier-C, tracked in "Honest remaining feature work" below. My earlier "deployment-gated" label for trust was wrong — it was unbuilt wiring (now done for the verify side) + an unbuilt feature (minting).
- ✅ **Egress proxy — runnable, containerizable binary.** Was a library referenced by no binary; now: `cmd/egressproxy/main.go` (forward-proxy server + separate `/healthz` port + graceful SIGTERM drain + structured egress-decision audit sink) reading validated `EGRESSPROXY_*` config (`internal/egressproxy/config.go` `Load`, fail-fast on missing gateway host, strict default) + `Dockerfile.egressproxy` (multi-stage, CI test-gate, distroless). `go build ./cmd/egressproxy` green; `config_test.go` + policy/proxy tests green. *(A Helm chart for sidecar injection into the customer runtime is the remaining genuine deploy config — the image now builds and runs.)*

A pre-built, turnkey per-framework driver for OpenAI-Agents/AutoGen/CrewAI (that knows each framework's exact model-injection API) is framework-specific R&D that needs those frameworks installed to validate faithfully — **Tier-C**, not wiring. Not fake-completing it.
- ✅ **Browser — Tier-B deliverable already shipped (Cycle 3); tool integration is Tier-C.** The genuine wiring gap the audit named ("only a fake `SessionManager` exists") is closed by the production `HTTPSessionManager` (Cycle 3). Constructing `browser.NewService` in main is pointless until a consumer exists — and **no governed browser tool is registered in the Supervisor, and the browser-pool service `HTTPSessionManager` calls doesn't exist as an artifact.** Building the governed browser tool + registration + the pool service is a **feature** (Tier-C), not a `_ = browserSvc` wiring line.

### Cycle 11 follow-on — Edge Connector agent binary (B15 fully closed, 2026-07-06)

The B15 "external deliverable" — the customer-run edge-agent — is now built in-repo (it IS code we ship, like the CLI; only its deployment is customer-side). **`cmd/edge-agent/`**: a standalone binary that dials OUT to `/v1/edge/connect` (Bearer API-key auth, `?connector_id=`), holds the WebSocket, and for each request frame dials the LOCAL self-hosted model and returns the response. Prod-grade: 12-factor env config validated at startup (fail-fast); reconnect loop with exponential backoff + full jitter; bounded in-flight concurrency (semaphore); ping/pong liveness; graceful shutdown on SIGINT/SIGTERM; **host-pinning SSRF guard** — only the request PATH+query is taken from the frame, the host is always the configured `ACTRONE_MODEL_URL`, so the control plane can direct which model endpoint but never make the agent reach an arbitrary internal host; optional local-model token override (else the binding's model key in the frame is forwarded). Wire frames exported from `edgeconnect` (`RequestFrame`/`ResponseFrame`) as the single-source contract. **Distributable:** multi-stage distroless-static non-root `Dockerfile`. **Tests:** host-pinning, local-dial + header/body forwarding, token override, local-failure→error-frame, config validation, and a **full end-to-end** test (real agent ↔ real server connect handler ↔ fake local model round-trip). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, suites green. B15 is now complete end to end — nothing external remains but a customer choosing to run it.

### Cycle 11 follow-on (2) — Edge tunnel STREAMING + agent CI/CD (2026-07-06)

**Streaming (removed the whole-body-buffering limitation):** rewrote the `edgeconnect` tunnel wire protocol from a single response frame to a **`head` → `chunk`* → `end`** stream. Server: per-request `stream` with its own drain goroutine feeding an `io.Pipe`; `RoundTrip` returns the `http.Response` as soon as the head arrives, its Body streaming chunk bytes as the agent forwards them — so an LLM SSE stream flows token-by-token instead of being buffered whole. The shared read loop **never blocks on a per-stream op** (chunk sends are non-blocking; a too-slow consumer's stream is dropped/errored, not head-of-line-blocking the tunnel). ctx-bounded (the timeout timer is released by the body's lifetime, not a truncating defer). Agent (`cmd/edge-agent`): `handle` now streams the local model's response body in 32 KiB chunks (head/chunk/end), size-capped, with local failures reported as an `end` frame carrying the error. Wire frames (`RequestFrame`/`ResponseFrame`) are the shared contract. **Tests:** updated all fake agents to the streaming protocol; new `TestRoundTripper_StreamsIncrementally` proves the consumer reads an early chunk **before** the agent sends the rest; e2e (real agent ↔ real server ↔ fake model) green; `go build ./...`, `go vet` clean.

**CI/CD (the agent is a shipped artifact, not deployed to our infra):** new **`publish-edge-agent.yml`** — on tag `edge-agent-v*` (or manual), tests (`-race`) then builds + publishes a **multi-arch** (amd64+arm64) image to `ghcr.io/actrone/edge-agent:<version>` + `:latest`, with provenance + SBOM; a PR only builds (verifies the Dockerfile) without pushing. Dockerfile updated to cross-compile via `$BUILDPLATFORM`/`TARGETARCH` (CGO off) so the multi-arch build skips QEMU for the Go compile. **No Helm/Terraform** — it runs on the *customer's* cluster, so nothing enters our deploy infra. (Customers pull the published image and `docker run` it with env vars — they never build the Dockerfile.)

All work UNCOMMITTED (owner commits).

### Cycle 13 follow-on — Voice campaign planner (§1.4 pure core, 2026-07-06)

The governance-native heart of §1.4 (durable outbound campaigns): `internal/voicecampaign` — a PURE, deterministic `NextBatch` planner that decides which callees to dial each tick given pacing (`CallsPerTick`), per-callee attempt/backoff state, max-attempts cap, and (the moat) each callee's **local-time calling window** — a callee whose window is currently CLOSED is DEFERRED, never dialed (mirrors the `voiceconsent` pure-engine design; the compliance behaviour Vapi/Bland can't offer). The planner does NOT dial — it returns callees for the caller to run through the `voiceoutbound.Service` (which re-checks consent+spend at dial time). Deterministic ordering (stable sort by eligibility+phone). The durable Temporal workflow shell around it is honestly deployment-gated (§1.7). Tests: pacing cap, window-defer (real TZ), retry-backoff, maxed/terminal skip, done-detection, unknown-tz, determinism. `go test` green.

**Note on remaining scope (honest):** the rest of Voice Outbound (full `voicecampaign` Temporal workflow, §2 orb, §3 SDK voice, §4 agentic-during-call + voice memory, §5 multi-channel delivery, §6 naturalness) is large + multi-cycle. The **native-SDK graph/branching authoring** and **crew-DX** items (`docs/Actrone_Native_vs_Framework_Audit.md` §"Gaps") are a wishlist in a COMPARISON doc with no design spec/phases and would require a new graph-execution capability in the Go runtime + authoring DSLs across TS+Python — that needs a design decision first, not a blind speculative build. Explainer-video is user-excluded. All work UNCOMMITTED (owner commits).

### Cycle 13 follow-on (4) — Voice Outbound §1.4 governed campaigns (2026-07-06)

Outbound at scale is a campaign, not a call. `internal/voicecampaign` now delivers the durable, governance-native campaign end to end:
- **Domain + store:** `Campaign` (+ per-callee state) with lifecycle `running|paused|completed|cancelled`; migration 00104 (`voice_campaigns` + `voice_campaign_callees`); `PgStore` (JSONB config, tx create, tenant-scoped, `ListRunning` for runner resume) + an in-memory fake for tests.
- **Service:** `Create` (E.164-validate + dedup targets, resolve each callee's TZ via the `voiceconsent` Locator), `Get/List/ListCalls`, `Pause/Resume/Cancel` (guarded transitions), and **`Advance`** — one governed tick: reconcile exhausted callees→failed, run the **pure planner** (pacing / callee-local **quiet-hours defer** / retry-backoff / max-attempts), dial each selected callee through the **origination Service** (consent + spend re-checked per dial), map outcome (refused→suppressed / placed→completed / transient→retry-backoff / cap→failed), recompute progress, auto-complete when all terminal. Idempotent per (callee, attempt).
- **Runner:** a restart-resumable background ticker (`NewRunner`, 15s, bounded concurrency) — durable state means a fresh process resumes every `running` campaign where it left off (the Temporal-workflow upgrade per §1.4 is a transparent later swap; the `Advance` tick it calls is unchanged). Wired in main (start on boot, graceful stop).
- **Endpoints (mounted with outbound):** `POST /v1/voice/campaigns` + `…/{id}/pause|resume|cancel` (agent_admin), `GET /v1/voice/campaigns`, `…/{id}` (progress), `…/{id}/calls` (per-callee outcomes). `campaignDialer` adapts the origination Service to the campaign `Dialer` seam.
- **Tests:** planner (pacing/window-defer/backoff/maxed/done/determinism), service (create-validate/dedup, advance dials+completes, retry→fail-at-cap, paused no-op, lifecycle transitions), runner (ticks running only, skips paused), handler (create validation, lifecycle, tenant-scope 404). `go build ./...`+`go vet` clean; suites green.

**Voice Outbound now: §1.2/§1.3/§1.4(full)/§1.5 + §3 SDK calls.create + §5 delivery — BUILT+wired+tested.** Remaining: SDK campaign methods (endpoints now exist), §2 `<actrone-orb>` embed, §4 agentic-during-call + §4.6 voice memory, §6 naturalness. Latest migration **00104**. UNCOMMITTED (owner commits).

### Cycle 13 follow-on (6) — Voice Outbound §2.2 governed embed backend (2026-07-06)

The governance moat of the `<actrone-orb>` embed — the ORIGIN-ALLOWLIST token mint (Voice Outbound Plan §2.2). `internal/voiceembed`:
- **`EmbedConfig` + `Store`** (migration 00105 `voice_embed_configs`): a tenant-scoped, revocable `public_key` (`pk_live_…`, NOT the API key) bound to an **allowed-origins** list + per-origin `rate_per_min` + `enabled`.
- **`Service.MintToken(publicKey, origin)`:** resolves the key → checks enabled → checks the browser-set **Origin** against the allowlist (unspoofable by page JS — the real control) → rate-limits per (key, origin) → mints a **LiveKit join token** (reusing the existing browser voice leg's issuer) + enqueues a worker assignment. Unknown/disabled key and disallowed origin both → generic 403 (a prober learns nothing). `CreateKey/ListKeys/DeleteKey` management (origins validated https-only, localhost for dev; `*` wildcard opt-in). `RedisRateLimiter` (fixed-window, cross-replica, fail-open).
- **Endpoints:** PUBLIC `POST /v1/voice/embed/token` (+ `OPTIONS` CORS preflight; on success echoes the *validated* origin as `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` so only allowlisted sites read the token; added to the auth middleware public-path allowlist) + authed `GET/POST/DELETE /v1/voice/embed/keys` (create/revoke agent_admin). Wired in main under the LiveKit+worker-dispatch block (`embedAssigner` adapts the assignment queue).
- **Tests:** service (origin allow/deny, unknown+disabled key, rate-limited, wildcard, origin normalisation) + handler (mint-allowed echoes CORS, bad-origin/unknown-key 403, missing-key 400, preflight). `go build ./...`+`go vet` clean; suites green.

The **orb JS bundle + browser voice client SDK** (§2.1/§2.3/§3.2 — framework-free custom element, Shadow DOM, bundled LiveKit client, CDN/npm) is the remaining client deliverable; it needs browser runtime testing, so it's tracked separately (same posture as the frontend de-Clerk). The *governed* backend it depends on is done. Latest migration **00105**. UNCOMMITTED (owner commits).

Bounded hops + per-turn deadline; on the last hop tools are withheld to force a spoken answer (no infinite loop); a governance-blocked tool feeds back an **error observation** so the agent recovers gracefully (§6.5) instead of going silent; memory (§4.6) injected. Opt-in `voice.agentic`; wired in main with the real `toolSupervisor` + agent-file resolver. - [x] ✅ **§6.1 streaming incremental clause-gate (`voice.ClauseGate`):** the "low-latency AND governed" reconciliation — gates the reply **clause-by-clause** as tokens arrive (terminal-punctuation boundary, run-on force-split at a word boundary, decimal-safe), Speaking each approved clause and **halting synthesis at the first blocked clause** (the remainder is never synthesized) — governance adds ~one clause of latency, not a whole-completion wait. Fail-closed on a governor error. `StreamReply(deltas)` for the token-stream path + `GateFullReply(reply)` for the current accumulate-then-speak path (`*VoiceSession` satisfies the speaker seam). Tests: clause-by-clause, halt-at-block, fail-closed, run-on-split, decimal-not-split, full-reply.
