- [x] ✅ **Media-path token-streaming for the ClauseGate (§6), fully wired into the live telephony loop.** Added a `StreamingResponder` seam (`RespondStream(ctx, claims, userText) → (<-chan string, error)`) implemented by **both** voice responders: `RouterResponder` (refactored to share a `route()` helper; streams token deltas) and `GovernedAgentResponder` (streams the FINAL answer live on the last hop, resolves tool hops silently — `streamTurn` gained an optional delta `sink`). `telephony.go` now builds a `voice.ClauseGate` over the `VoiceSession` and, in `onFinalUtterance`, feeds the responder's token stream straight into `gate.StreamReply` (clause-by-clause gate→synth→play with **halt-at-block**), falling back to `gate.GateFullReply` for a non-streaming responder — so `main.go`'s existing `Responder: voiceResponder` wiring auto-upgrades to streaming with no wiring change. The synth/sink media seam (the same one the real Cartesia/LiveKit adapters implement) is exercised by fakes; the governance + streaming logic is fully unit-tested. Tests added: router `RespondStream` (deltas/setup-error/mid-stream-error), agentic `RespondStream` (final-answer delivery + tool still runs), telephony streaming clause-by-clause + **halt-at-blocked-clause** + non-streaming clause-gated fallback. `go build`/`go vet` clean; voice + http suites green.
- [x] ✅ **Orb single-file CDN bundle (§2.3) — build + verified output + test.** `frontend/vite.orb.config.ts` (uses the repo's pinned Vite, no new dep) builds `src/orb/index.ts` into a self-registering **6.97 kB** ES-module entry `public/orb/v1.js` (`npm run build:orb`), with **LiveKit split into a lazily-loaded sibling chunk** (679 kB, loaded only when a session starts) so the core stays tiny + CSP-friendly. CDN usage updated to `<script type="module">`. Artifact gitignored (generated; CDN-uploaded in CI). **Test** (`orb.bundle.test.ts`) runs the real `build:orb` in a separate process and asserts the packaging contract: entry self-registers `<actrone-orb>` + hits the embed-token endpoint, LiveKit is NOT inlined, and the entry dynamic-imports the lazy chunk. 12/12 orb tests + typecheck clean.
- [x] ✅ **OIDC de-Clerk P2 — the client-hook layer + full login flow + middleware, wired, with the Clerk path byte-identical.** Built end-to-end (self-host OIDC now boots without Clerk keys):
  - **Pure PKCE flow** (`lib/auth/oidc-flow.ts`): `generatePkce` (S256), `discoverEndpoints`, `buildAuthorizeUrl`, `exchangeCode`, `decodeJwtClaims`, `validateNonce` (token-injection defense), `fetchUserInfo`, `safeReturnPath` (open-redirect guard). **17 unit tests.**
  - **Client session + agnostic hooks:** `OidcSessionProvider` (hydrates the normalized `AuthSession` from `/api/auth/session`) + provider-agnostic `useOrganization`/`useUser` (`hooks.ts` selects clerk-impl vs oidc-impl once at module load from `NEXT_PUBLIC_AUTH_PROVIDER` — a build-time constant, so the unused provider tree-shakes and hook identity is stable). Both impls project to a minimal `UseOrganizationResult`/`UseUserResult`. **8 tests** (pure builders + jsdom integration through the provider).
  - **Auth routes** (`app/api/auth/{login,callback,logout,session,token}`): code+PKCE login → callback (state/nonce/PKCE verified, fail-closed to `/sign-in?error=oidc`, sets httpOnly claims+token cookies) → RP-initiated logout → session JSON (feeds the client context) → token (browser bearer for cross-origin API). All OIDC-only (404 under Clerk).
  - **Provider-agnostic server token** (`server-token.ts`): Clerk `getToken()` OR the OIDC access-token cookie; `lib/api/server.ts` now uses it (no direct Clerk import).
  - **Middleware** (`proxy.ts`): extracted the shared feature-gate + CSP + marketplace-rewrite; `export default isOidc() ? oidcMiddleware : clerkAuthMiddleware`. OIDC path redirects unauthenticated protected pages to `/api/auth/login` (401 for APIs); `/api/auth/(.*)` added to the public allowlist.
  - **Providers:** `RootAuthProvider` (Clerk wrapper OR pass-through) in the root layout; `AppAuthProvider` (`ClerkTokenProvider` OR `OidcSessionProvider`+`OidcTokenProvider`) in the (app) layout.
  - **Env made provider-aware** (`lib/env.ts`): Clerk keys optional; a `providerRefine` requires exactly the Clerk trio OR the OIDC issuer+client-id — the fail-fast mirror of the backend `validateAuth` (cloud boot behaviour unchanged).
  - **Read-path consumers migrated** to `@/lib/auth/hooks`: `useCurrentUserRole`, `useScopedRoles`, `useEnabledFeatures`, `useEntitlements`, `EnvironmentProvider`, `SidebarBrand`, `DashboardHero`, `mcp`, `a2a`. Clerk-specific **management** surfaces (org/user/team CRUD via `organization.update/reload/inviteMember`, the org switcher, the auth UI, `settings/features`' metadata write, `posthog` `user.createdAt`) correctly stay on Clerk — they are administered in the IdP under self-host, not the console.
  - **Verification:** full frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; **full vitest suite 180/180 green** (incl. the real orb build + the migrated hooks exercised transitively via the Clerk-mocked OrgBar test).

Remaining (needs live infra / product decisions, not code): the streaming-gate over a real SFU audio path (the governance/streaming logic is done + tested via the synth/sink seam); the OIDC-mode replacements for the Clerk management UIs (sign-in page, org/user/team admin) under a running IdP. Native-SDK graph authoring + crew DX and the explainer video remain out of scope. All UNCOMMITTED (owner commits). Backend migration high-water **00106**; frontend adds the orb build + `src/lib/auth/*` OIDC flow/hooks/routes/middleware.

### Cycle 16 — OIDC-mode management UIs (self-host de-Clerk, the last mile) (2026-07-06)

The Clerk-specific management surfaces now have designed OIDC-mode replacements — the IdP is the system
of record under self-host, so the console delegates identity management to it rather than reimplementing
CRUD (Grafana/GitLab pattern). Clerk cloud path is **byte-identical** (build-time-constant branch; the
Clerk-coupled component never mounts under OIDC, so its Clerk hooks never run).

- [x] ✅ **`authCapabilities()` seam** (`frontend/src/lib/auth/capabilities.ts`): the single per-provider
  capability matrix — `{ provider, providerName, manageProfile/Org/Team/Features, switchOrg,
  accountConsoleUrl, adminConsoleUrl, signInPath, signOutPath }`. Clerk → all in-app management true, no
  external console; OIDC → management false + IdP deep links + login/logout routes. Pure + config-derived.
  Config added (`config.ts` + `env.ts`, all optional/public): `NEXT_PUBLIC_OIDC_PROVIDER_NAME`,
  `NEXT_PUBLIC_OIDC_ACCOUNT_URL` (defaults to `${issuer}/account`), `NEXT_PUBLIC_OIDC_ADMIN_URL`.
- [x] ✅ **Auth-action seams** (`actions.ts` selector + `actions-clerk.ts`/`actions-oidc.ts`, same
  module-init pattern as `hooks.ts`): `useAuthActions` (sign-out → `clerk.signOut` | `/api/auth/logout`;
  create-org) + `useOrgSwitcher` (Clerk org list/setActive | inert on OIDC — the token carries the org).
  OrgBar migrated to these + the agnostic hooks; no direct Clerk import remains in OrgBar.
- [x] ✅ **Sign-in / sign-up** (`(auth)/sign-in|sign-up/page.tsx` + new `OidcSignIn`): OIDC renders a
  "Continue with <IdP>" action → `/api/auth/login`, with a designed `?error=oidc` error state; Clerk keeps
  `<ClerkThemed>`. Pages branch server-side on `authCapabilities().provider`.
- [x] ✅ **settings/profile · organization · team + OrgBar switcher**: OIDC renders a designed
  "managed in your identity provider" state — new `ManagedInIdp` component reusing the shared `EmptyState`
  primitive (icon + copy + deep link to the IdP account/admin console). Each page wraps its existing
  Clerk body behind the capability branch; OrgBar's switcher shows the current org + a "Manage in <IdP>"
  link (no in-app switch/create) and sign-out routes to `/api/auth/logout`.
- [x] ✅ **settings/features → read-only under OIDC** (license-driven): new `ReadOnlyFeatures` view resolves
  tier + enabled_features from the org's provider claims (the license-populated `org_metadata`) via the
  SAME resolver the cloud uses, and renders the identical group/row layout with every control inert
  (isAdmin=false) + a "determined by your license, administered by <IdP>" banner — **never writes** Clerk
  org metadata. The Clerk editor (`ClerkFeaturesSettings`) is unchanged.

**Verification:** full frontend `tsc --noEmit` clean; **vitest 191/191 green** (added capabilities-matrix,
OIDC-surfaces render, and actions-seam tests; existing OrgBar test still green through the agnostic
seams); **ESLint clean** on every changed file (rules-of-hooks safe — the page wrappers early-return on a
build-time constant before any hook). All UNCOMMITTED (owner commits). Infra runbook §6.20 updated (the
management-UI gap is now ☑, with the three new `NEXT_PUBLIC_OIDC_*` deep-link vars documented).

### Cycle 18 — Tier-1 built-but-unwired fixes (2026-07-06)

Closing the Cycle-17 **BUILT-BUT-UNWIRED** list top-down — each fix makes already-written, already-tested code reachable, prod-grade + tests, CLAUDE.md-compliant. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **Gap 1 — Stepwise driver ↔ backend enum sync (biggest).** `HostedFrameworks` + `StepwiseFrameworks` (`internal/domain/agent.go`) now list all built harness drivers: the 6 Python stepwise drivers (langchain, llamaindex, pydantic_ai, semantic_kernel, dspy, google_adk) + the 2 TS shims (vercel, mastra), alongside the pre-existing set. Every hosted framework now ships a stepwise driver/shim, so `StepwiseFrameworks == HostedFrameworks` (encapsulated remains valid for all); stale comments claiming llamaindex/dspy are "encapsulated-only" removed. `governed_system.go`'s `isValidSystemFramework` derives from `HostedFrameworks` → auto-synced; the worker (`actrone-py harness`) dispatches via the author's own `make_*` entrypoint, not a server map, so the parser enum is the sole gate. No SDK/frontend mirror list exists. **Tests:** replaced the now-unreachable "stepwise unsupported framework" rejection case (every hosted framework is stepwise now) with two enum-drift guards — `TestRuntime_AllHostedFrameworksParse` and `TestRuntime_AllHostedFrameworksAcceptStepwise` (iterate `domain.HostedFrameworks`, assert each parses as `byof_hosted` and accepts `strategy: stepwise`) — the exact check that would have caught this disconnect. `go build ./...`, `go test ./internal/agent/... ./internal/domain/...` green.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 2 — G1 native graph DSL wired into the agent-file parser.** `internal/agentgraph` was a pure, tested DSL (nodes/edges/entry/terminals + validation + traversal) that no manifest field referenced → unreachable. Added `Spec.Graph *agentgraph.Graph` to `AgentSpec` (`omitempty`, so existing manifests + their manifest-hash are byte-identical → replay-safe) + `Spec.HasGraph()`, and `validateGraph` in the parser: a graph is valid **only** for a native agent (a BYOF agent owns its own loop) and must pass the DSL's structural invariants at load (fail fast). `domain` importing `agentgraph` is acyclic (agentgraph imports only stdlib). **Tests:** `graph_crew_test.go` — valid graph parses + surfaces + traverses; graph on a BYOF agent rejected; structurally-invalid (unreachable node) graph rejected. *(Graph→governed-activity compilation remains the package's own documented "infra follow-on"; this closes the "no parser field" reachability gap.)* `go build ./...` + agent/domain/http/macp suites green.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 9 — LE3/LE4 loop tools decoupled from `EMAOP.Enabled`.** The governed loop-tool router (memory.search + notes.*/todo.* scratchpad + doc.fetch + ledger.get) was registered only inside `if cfg.EMAOP.Enabled`, so a `spec.loop` agent with EMAOP off had compaction/reflection/effort but NO pull-based context tools. But the router depends only on `db.Memory`, `qdrantStore`, `threshold` + a fresh ledger store — all defined before the EMAOP block — and operates on the agent's OWN memory/notes/docs (not fresh enterprise data needing MAL), so it never needed EMAOP. Moved the `WithLoopToolRouter` registration + `loopLedgerStore` assignment OUT of the EMAOP block to run unconditionally (the browser tool stays EMAOP-gated — raw web content genuinely needs MAL tokenisation). `loopLedgerStore` is now always set, so the `ledger.get` recorder (`SetLoopLedgerRecorder`, `if loopLedgerStore != nil`) is always wired too. `toolSupervisor` is constructed unconditionally at main.go:539, so the move is safe. `go build ./...`, `go vet ./cmd/orchestrator/`, `internal/looptool` suite green. *(main.go wiring is validated by build+vet+the router's own component tests, consistent with how the repo verifies bootstrap wiring.)*
- [x] ✅ **Gap 8 — LE6 per-turn model-tier now ENACTED (not just logged).** `planLoopTurn` produced `plan.ModelTier` but `task_workflow.go` applied only `plan.ReasoningEffort` → a Loop Policy's per-phase cheap/strong routing never changed the model called. Now, for a `spec.loop` agent, the resolved per-phase tier is applied to `StreamLLMInput.Model` (route/reason on the main loop turns, wrap_up on the forced final call), so the router sends the turn to the policy's chosen model; `BaselineModel` is cleared on override so an explicit governance tier is **not** mis-attributed as a learned cost-route saving. **Replay-safe:** the override changes the recorded activity input, so it is `workflow.GetVersion`-gated (`applyLoopModelTier`, change id `loop-model-tier`) — replays of executions started before this shipped keep their prior tier-not-applied input; a zero Loop Policy is untouched (byte-identical). Stale "applying the decision is the follow-on" comment corrected. **Tests:** `agentic_loop_test.go` (2 new — route-phase tier reaches the activity input; zero-policy keeps the base model) via the Temporal test env capturing `StreamLLMInput`; existing `planLoopTurn` unit tests unchanged. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, full `internal/workflow` suite green.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 7 — Studio WS3 governance proposer upgraded from heuristic to model-backed + wired.** `SetGovernanceProposer` was a seam nothing called → only the deterministic `HeuristicProposer` ever ran (it can't infer reversibility / compensating action / amount field). Built `connectorimport.ModelProposer` (`model_proposer.go`): a `Completer`-backed LLM proposer that prompts for a strict-JSON governance classification and parses it defensively (tolerates prose/markdown fences). **Safety-first:** any model error or unparsable/empty output **falls back to the `HeuristicProposer`** (so it never drops below the deterministic floor — a DELETE is still irreversible), and whatever it returns is still reconciled by `ProposeGovernance` against the same `gal.WriteActionSpec` validation (an inconsistent suggestion, e.g. `money` with no amount_field, is discarded → conservative approval-forced default). Wired in `main.go`: a `governanceProposerCaller` adapter over the managed `modelRouter` (400-token JSON budget, `distill_adapters.go`) is passed to `connectorsHandler.SetGovernanceProposer(...)` when the router is present. **Tests:** `model_proposer_test.go` (7 — parses a rich proposal, extracts JSON from prose, falls back on model-error/garbage to the heuristic floor, ignores reads, and the end-to-end reconciliation-discards-inconsistent case). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/connectorimport/... ./cmd/orchestrator/`, connectorimport suite green.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 6 — Frontend `RoleGate` mounted on real admin-only controls.** `RoleGate.tsx` (+ its `useScopedRoles` hook + `lib/roles`) was fully built but had zero importers → scoped-RBAC UX gating existed nowhere. Mounted it where the least-privilege boundary is real: (1) **Rules Workbench** — both governance-promote controls ("Promote to production" + per-version "Activate/rollback") wrapped `<RoleGate scope="emaop" min="agent_admin" mode="disable">` (operators can still Test/Save-draft; only agent_admin promotes — the server `RequireScopeRole` is the authority, the gate mirrors it); (2) **Marketplace publish** page — `<RoleGate scope="marketplace" min="publisher">` around `PublishWizard` with a designed `EmptyState` fallback (non-publishers get an actionable "ask an admin" state instead of a wizard whose submit would fail). **Tests:** `src/test/RoleGate.test.tsx` (4 — allowed renders child, insufficient hides+fallback, disable-mode disables+`aria-disabled`, optimistic-before-hydration). `npm run typecheck` clean, RoleGate suite green, ESLint clean on all changed files.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 5 — Connector pagination/incremental sync now consumed end-to-end.** `custom_builder.go` produced `NextCursor` (RFC 5988 `Link rel="next"`) but nothing fed it back → page 2 was unreachable. Wired agent-driven pagination: (1) `Fetch` now consumes a reserved `CursorQueryKey` (`__actrone_cursor`) — when set, it pages against that URL instead of the action's base path, **SSRF-constrained** via new `nextPageURL` (cursor must be an absolute, https/same-scheme, SAME-HOST URL; a cross-host cursor is rejected before any request, complementing `GuardBaseURL`'s DNS pinning); the reserved key is stripped from provider query params (`applyQuery`) and from the write path (`stripControlKeys`). (2) The connector **tool** (`connectortool/router.go`) accepts a `cursor` arg (→ reserved key), surfaces `resp.NextCursor` as `next_cursor` in the tokenised result, and advertises the `cursor` property in the tool schema so the model knows to page. Each page still flows through the full MAL-tokenisation + purpose-filter + provenance boundary. **Tests:** `pagination_test.go` — follows `Link` next across two pages (last page → empty cursor, credential injected on every page) + `nextPageURL` validation (relative/http-downgrade/cross-host rejected, same-host accepted). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, connector + connectortool suites green.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 4 — Wave-C D3 tool governance wired into the per-framework TS adapters.** `_govern.ts` (`governTool`/`governTools`/`governAction`) was exported but the three model adapters (`vercel-ai.ts`, `mastra.ts`, `openai-agents.ts`) were D2-only — their docstrings said "D3 (later)… Not built in this wave," so a user had to reach into a separate module and hand-assemble the native tool shape. Added a framework-tailored one-liner to each, delegating to `_govern`: **`actroneVercelTools`** + **`actroneMastraTools`** (return the `{ [sanitisedName]: {description,parameters,execute} }` map `generateText({ tools })` / a Mastra agent's `tools` consume) and **`actroneOpenAIAgentsTools`** (returns an array of `tool()`-ready defs carrying the sanitised `name` field OpenAI Agents requires; `execute` closes over the ORIGINAL governed id so the supervisor resolves the real tool). Both `mode:"execute"` (route via `callTool`) and `mode:"supervise"` (authorise-then-run local fn, block on deny) supported. All three exported from `adapters/index.ts`; the stale "D3 later" docstrings replaced with the correct pairing note; frameworks stay optional peer deps (structural shapes, zero runtime import). **Tests:** `test/adapters-d3-tools.test.ts` (5 cases — each helper routes through a fake governing client, name sanitisation, supervise-mode deny blocks the local fn). `npm run typecheck` clean, full **vitest 123/123 green**, `npm run build` (incl. DTS) clean.
- [x] ✅ **Gap 3 — G2 crew DX wired end-to-end (parser + resolver + endpoint + contract).** `internal/domain/crew_spec.go` (declarative role-based crew: members/roles/goals/process + validation) was referenced only by its own test. Wired three ways: **(1) manifest** — `Coordination.Crew *CrewSpec` (`omitempty`) + `validateCrew` (crew authoring invariants + coordination-consistency: requires `can_spawn_agents`, worker count ≤ `max_sub_agents`); **(2) runtime resolver** — `macp.CrewFromSpec(spec, tenantID, crossTenant) *macp.Crew` lowers a validated spec into a runnable governed crew (member order preserved = sequential execution order; per-crew shared-memory namespace), the static counterpart to the dynamic `CrewAssembler.Assemble`; **(3) product surface** — `POST /v1/coordination/crew` (`CoordinationHandler.ResolveCrew`, entitlement-gated on `FeatureCoordinationGraph`, tenant from context) validates + resolves + returns the crew, mirroring `POST /v1/coordination/assemble`. OpenAPI updated (`ResolveCrewRequest`/`CrewSpec`/`ResolvedCrew` + path). **Tests:** parser (valid crew + Workers, missing-spawn rejected, over-max rejected, invalid hierarchical rejected) + `crew_from_spec_test.go` (sequential order preserved, hierarchical+cross-tenant threaded, unique IDs). `go build ./...`, agent/domain/macp/http suites green, `openapi.yaml` re-validated.

- [x] ✅ **P6 — `build_mode` now a first-class, persisted, queryable attribute.** It was stored only inside the `agent_file` JSONB, so you couldn't efficiently list/filter agents by execution locus. Handler: `GET /v1/agents?build_mode=` with allowlist validation (`domain.IsValidBuildMode`, 400 on unknown); OpenAPI updated (query param + 400). **Verification:** the exact generated-column SQL + index + filter **validated against a live PostgreSQL 16 container** (derives byof_hosted/byof_connected, legacy→native, EXPLAIN shows the filter is **Index Scan**-served) + DB-free unit tests (`buildmode_test.go` IsValidBuildMode; `agents_buildmode_test.go` scanAgent derivation via a fake row) + a build-tagged integration test (`agents_buildmode_integration_test.go`, compiles; runs in a migrated CI DB). `go build ./...`, domain/repository/handler/agent suites green.

- [x] ✅ **P2 — modelkeys per-agent routing (multi-binding).** `modelkeys.Resolver.Resolve` accepted `agentID` but ignored it — every agent resolved to the tenant's single env-default binding. Now a tenant with several bindings can pin different agents to different providers. **Migration 00108** `agent_model_bindings` (thin pointer table `(tenant,env,agent) → endpoint_id`, PK per agent×env, FK `ON DELETE CASCADE` to `model_endpoints`, `set_updated_at` trigger; the credential is never duplicated). Store: `ActiveForAgent` (JOIN; returns only a **valid** binding, so a pinned-but-unhealthy binding falls through rather than taking the agent offline), `SetAgentBinding` (ownership-guarded upsert — `INSERT…SELECT WHERE EXISTS owned` → a tenant can't pin to another tenant's/env's binding, NotFound otherwise), `ClearAgentBinding`, `GetAgentBinding`; `prefixedEndpointColumns` derives the JOIN projection from the single `endpointColumns` source (no drift). Resolver order is now **per-agent override → env-default → managed** (back-compat: no override / no agent context ⇒ unchanged). HTTP: `GET/PUT/DELETE /v1/models/agents/{agentID}/binding` (read open to members; set/clear gated on `billing:admin`, mirroring binding ownership). **Verification:** the exact SQL (guarded upsert, JOIN, valid-only fall-through, FK cascade) **validated against live PostgreSQL 16**; a full **integration test PASSES against a live DB** (`agent_binding_integration_test.go` — override wins over env-default, other agents unaffected, cross-tenant pin rejected, clear reverts) run by applying migrations 00001/00038/00048/00108 to a throwaway PG16; DB-free unit test `TestPrefixedEndpointColumns`; existing resolver back-compat tests green. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, modelkeys/handler/service suites green.

- [x] ✅ **P3 — promotion separation-of-duties (approver ≠ initiator) now server-enforced.** The approver seam checked "is the actor an authorised approver" but never that the approver DIFFERED from the initiator — an admin could self-approve their own broadening promotion (the old code comment conceded "distinct-approver-from-initiator SoD needs the initiator identity threaded"). Now, when a request store is wired, a **broadening** promotion is HELD as a persisted pending request recording the **initiator** (never trusted from the client), and a **distinct** authorised approver must approve it. **Migration 00109** `promotion_requests` (pending → approved|rejected, partial pending index, env CHECK). Repo `PromotionRequestRepository` (Create / GetByID / ListPending / **Resolve = compare-and-set on status='pending'** → guards the double-approve race). Service: `Promote` holds a broadening hop as a request (returns `NeedsApproval`+`Request`, applies nothing); `ApprovePromotion` enforces **approver≠initiator** (`ERR_GOVERNANCE_BLOCK`) + authorised-approver, **claims the request atomically before applying** (no double-apply), re-diffs the live source, then applies via the extracted `finalise`; `RejectPromotion` (initiator withdraws or approver declines); `ListPendingApprovals` (approver inbox). Non-broadening promotes stay single-step; a nil request store preserves the legacy inline check (back-compat — existing tests unchanged). HTTP: `POST /v1/promotions/{requestID}/approve|reject`, `GET /v1/promotions/pending`; the held Promote returns 202 with the request. Wired in main (`SetRequestStore(db.PromotionRequests)`; stale "documented follow-on" comment replaced). **Verification:** 6 new service unit tests (held-for-approval, initiator-cannot-self-approve, distinct-approver-applies, unauthorised-rejected, already-resolved-conflict, initiator-withdraws) all green; the migration SQL + **CAS conflict guard + pending index + env CHECK validated against live PostgreSQL 16**; a repo **integration test PASSES against a live DB** (create→list→get→resolve→double-resolve-conflict). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, promotion/repository/handler/domain suites green.

Domain `GovernedSystemRecord` (id/version/System/is_active/…). Repo `GovernedSystemRepository`: `Save` (transactional next-version + deactivate-prior + insert-active, so the one-active index can't be transiently violated), `GetActive` / `GetVersion` / `ListActive` / `ListVersions`. Handler (interface-backed `systemStore`, nil-safe): `POST /v1/systems` (save, validated, agent_admin), `GET /v1/systems` (active list), `GET /v1/systems/{name}` (active or `?version=N`), `GET /v1/systems/{name}/versions`, `POST /v1/systems/{name}/run` (runs the active version) — the ad-hoc `POST /v1/systems/run` stays. Wired in main (`db.GovernedSystems`). **Verification:** 5 new handler tests (save persists v1, invalid rejected, run-by-name runs the active version + returns its number, unknown→404) + existing ad-hoc run tests green (constructor updated); the versioning txn + one-active partial index **validated by a repo integration test PASSING against live PostgreSQL 16** (save v1 → save v2 deactivates v1 → active=v2 → old version still retrievable → list/versions → NotFound). `go build ./...`, `go vet`, handler/repository/domain/governedsystem suites green.

All structural over the shared `memoryFor` core (zero runtime import → frameworks stay optional peer deps), consumed via the `actrone-memory/adapters` subpath; all 7 (+ the previously-unexported langgraph/mastra) now also re-exported from the root index for discoverability. **Tests:** 4 new adapter cases (llamaindex getSystemPrompt+saveTurn; openai-agents prepend-order + empty-base passthrough; genkit getSystem+remember). `npm run typecheck` (strict) clean, full **vitest 24/24 green**, `npm run build` (incl. DTS) clean. *(Note: parity is matched-breadth-with-real-JS-frameworks, not the identical 7 names — the JS ecosystem's popular agent frameworks differ from Python's.)*

### Cycle 19 follow-on — migration-chain fix + full-chain validation (2026-07-07)

- [x] ✅ **Fixed the pre-existing `escalation_queue` duplicate that broke a from-scratch `goose up`.** `00030_dpe_rules.sql` created a divergent, UNUSED `escalation_queue` placeholder (columns `dpe_rule_id`/`approver_id`/`confidence_score`) while `00041_escalation_queue.sql` created the canonical one the code actually uses (`workflow_id`/`rule_id`/`resolved_by` — confirmed against `internal/repository/escalations.go`), so a fresh migrate failed at 00041 (SQLSTATE 42P07). Fix: **(a)** removed the placeholder from 00030 (kept `dpe_rules` + `rule_sandbox_results`; nothing between 30 and 41 references it), making **00041 the sole canonical definer**; **(b)** added a defensive `DROP TABLE IF EXISTS escalation_queue` at the top of 00041 so a half-migrated env carrying the stale placeholder self-heals (the placeholder never held app data). No-op in environments where 00041 is already applied (goose never re-runs applied migrations). **Verified: the FULL chain (all 110 migrations) now applies cleanly from scratch to a live PostgreSQL 16 container** ("successfully migrated database to version: 110"), `escalation_queue` ends with the canonical 00041 schema, and no other downstream conflict exists.
- [x] ✅ **Bonus — hardened 00107 build_mode column (found by running the full integration suite against the now-migratable DB).** The generated column `COALESCE(… ->> 'build_mode', 'native')` mapped only a MISSING key to `native`, not an explicit empty string — so a hand-built manifest with `runtime.build_mode == ""` yielded column `''`, disagreeing with `EffectiveBuildMode()`. Changed to `COALESCE(NULLIF(…, ''), 'native')` so absent AND empty both resolve to `native`, matching the Go semantics exactly. Also populated `BuildMode` on `AgentRepository.Create`'s return (previously only set on reads). **All four Cycle-19 repo integration tests (build_mode filter, GovernedSystem versioning, promotion-request CAS, per-agent binding resolution) now PASS against the fully-migrated live PG16** — genuine end-to-end validation, not subset-applied. `go build ./...`, `go vet`, repository/handler/domain suites green.

### Cycle 20 — Public Domain Cutover Runbook implementation (2026-07-08)

Implementing the **CODE** items of `infra/docs/Actrone_Public_Domain_Cutover_Runbook.md`, prod-grade + tested, one at a time. Scope note (honest): the runbook is a public-domain-cutover plan — many items are **[MANUAL]** infra applies (Route53/CloudFront/Fargate/ACM/DNS-registrar) or need live external accounts (Sanity), which by the runbook's own tagging are apply-gated and cannot be "run" in-repo; those will be authored + statically validated (`terraform validate`/yaml/lint) and marked **authored+validated, apply-gated**, never falsely "deployed". Fully in-repo-buildable+testable items are done + tested first. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **Phase 7 — `POST /v1/contact` backend endpoint (own governed contact form, no third-party PII).** The runbook's recommended contact path: a public endpoint that persists a durable record (source of truth) and best-effort emails the team via the existing branded pipeline, so no prospect PII flows through a third-party form processor. **Built:** migration **00112** `contact_submissions` (append-only: name/email/company/message/source/ip/user_agent/request_id/created_at + `created_at DESC` index); `repository.ContactRepository.Create` (returns server-generated id + created_at) wired into `DB` (`db.Contact`); `ContactHandler.Submit` (`internal/handler/http/contact.go`) — PUBLIC route registered **before** the auth middleware next to the channel webhooks (`r.Post("/v1/contact", …)`), with allowlist validation + rune-safe size caps (name ≤200, email via `net/mail.ParseAddress` ≤320, message ≤5000, company ≤200), a **honeypot** (`website` field → 200 but silently dropped, never persisted/emailed), `X-Forwarded-For`-aware client IP, and `X-Request-Id` capture (CLAUDE.md §5.1). **Durable-first ordering** (CLAUDE.md §6.3): persist, then notify — an email-send failure is logged but never fails the request (the record already captured the lead); a missing recipient still persists + skips only the notification. Notification renders via `email.Render(email.Notice{})` to a new optional `cfg.Email.ContactRecipient` (falls back to `FromAddress`). Rate-limiting is the Envoy edge (per-IP); CORS for `actrone.com` is applied at the gateway (both noted in code). **Tests:** `contact_test.go` — 6 handler cases incl. table-driven validation (valid→persist+email to recipient; honeypot→200-but-dropped; missing/blank/invalid email, missing/blank/oversized message, unknown-field→4xx none-persisted; persist-error→500-no-email; no-recipient→still-persists; email-failure→still-200). `contact_integration_test.go` (`//go:build integration`) — `Create` round-trips through **live PostgreSQL 16** (server-generated id + created_at, all fields verbatim), **PASSED** after applying the **full 112-migration chain from scratch** to a throwaway PG16 (`goose … successfully migrated database to version: 112`). `go build ./...`, `go vet ./internal/handler/http/ ./internal/repository/ ./cmd/orchestrator/`, and the full `internal/handler/http` + `internal/repository` + `internal/config` suites all green. *(Frontend contact-form wiring to this endpoint + the gateway CORS-allowlist add for `actrone.com` are the paired frontend/infra items, tracked separately.)*

- [x] ✅ **Phase 3 — reusable CloudFront + Fargate + ACM + WAF site module, instantiated for marketing + marketplace (authored + `terraform validate`-clean; APPLY-GATED, not apply-verified).** The runbook's chosen hosting model as one DRY module (`modules/cloudfront-fargate-site/`, ~450 lines) instantiated twice in `envs/prod/frontend.tf` (marketing `actrone.com`/`www`; marketplace `marketplace.actrone.com`), each an independently-scaled Next.js standalone container off the Envoy edge. **Contents (prod-grade patterns):** two **ACM** certs (DNS-validated in the zone) — a public cert for the CloudFront aliases + an **origin-hostname** cert (`<site>-origin.actrone.com`) for the ALB (both us-east-1, which the prod plane is); an **internet-facing ALB locked to CloudFront two ways** — SG ingress 443 only from the `com.amazonaws.global.cloudfront.origin-facing` managed prefix list, **and** an HTTPS listener whose default action is `403` with a rule that forwards only requests carrying the secret **`X-Origin-Verify`** header CloudFront injects; **ECS Fargate** service in private subnets (SG admits only the ALB SG) with least-priv exec/task roles, awslogs, container-insights, and **independent CPU target-tracking autoscaling** (`ignore_changes=[desired_count]`); an **OAC-locked S3** assets bucket (public-access-block + CloudFront-SourceArn bucket policy) for the `/_next/static/*` behavior; a **CloudFront** distribution (default behavior → ALB, caching-disabled + AllViewer origin-request so SSR/ISR/cookies pass through; `/_next/static/*` → S3, CachingOptimized; a security-headers response policy = HSTS/X-Content-Type-Options/X-Frame-Options/Referrer-Policy); a **WAFv2** (`CLOUDFRONT` scope) with the AWS common rule set + an IP rate-limit; and **Route53** ALIAS records (public aliases → CloudFront, origin hostname → ALB). - [x] ✅ **Phase 2b — CloudFront static-asset acceleration in front of `app.actrone.com` (authored + `terraform validate`-clean; apply-gated).** `envs/prod/app_cdn.tf` (new): an ACM cert for `app.actrone.com` (DNS-validated in the zone), a `CLOUDFRONT`-scope WAFv2 (AWS common rule set), a security-headers response policy (HSTS/X-Content-Type-Options/X-Frame-Options/Referrer-Policy), and a CloudFront distribution whose **single origin is the Envoy edge** (`app-origin.actrone.com`, HTTPS-only, carrying the secret `X-Origin-Verify` header). Cache behaviors enforce the invariant: **default = caching-disabled + AllViewer origin-request** (every dynamic/authenticated request is forwarded to Envoy so `ext_authz` runs on everything that matters), and **only `/_next/static/*` is cached** (CachingOptimized). The configuration is valid."** **Honest bound:** validate confirms syntax/refs/types, not runtime wiring — apply-gated, and Envoy must be configured to require the `X-Origin-Verify` header (the origin-lock half) as the runbook's [MANUAL] counterpart.

Added the canonical **`hls.js@^1.6.16`** dependency (justified for the runbook's HLS requirement; it appears in **no** security advisory — the tree's pre-existing highs are lodash-es/undici/vite, not introduced here). Additive component; moves into `apps/marketing` with the split. **Tests:** `src/test/HlsVideo.test.tsx` (2 cases — renders an accessible `<video>` with controls/poster; with native HLS unavailable it **lazily attaches hls.js** to the right manifest, verified via a hoisted class mock + `vi.waitFor`). **`vitest run` 2/2 pass; ESLint clean** on all four media files (both components + tests).

- [x] ✅ **Phase 5 (media) — MediaConvert HLS transcode pipeline (authored + `terraform validate`-clean + Lambda JS syntax-checked; apply-gated).** `modules/media-pipeline/` + `envs/prod/media.tf`: a **private ingest bucket** (PAB) → an **S3 `ObjectCreated:*.mp4` notification** invokes a **Node 20 Lambda** (`lambda/index.mjs`) that resolves the account MediaConvert endpoint (cached) and submits an **adaptive Apple-HLS job** (QVBR 1080/720/480 renditions + manifest) whose output lands in a **private delivery bucket** (PAB); a **MediaConvert service role** (read ingest / write delivery) and a least-priv **Lambda role** (scoped logs + `s3:GetObject` on ingest + `mediaconvert:CreateJob/DescribeEndpoints` + `iam:PassRole` limited to the MediaConvert role only). The delivery bucket's `bucket_regional_domain_name` is an **output** for wiring a CloudFront S3-origin (media CDN). The Lambda zip is `var.lambda_zip_path` (built by CI from `./lambda`), so validate never reads it. **Verification:** `terraform fmt` clean; **`terraform validate` → "Success! The configuration is valid."**; the Lambda source passes **`node --check`** (syntax). **Honest bound:** validate + syntax only — the MediaConvert job template + IAM + the S3→Lambda→CreateJob flow are **not apply-verified** (no live MediaConvert here), so this is a reviewable, apply-gated draft.

## WorkOS auth switch (docs/Actrone_WorkOS_Auth_Switch_Plan.md) — implementing 2026-07-08+

Clean replacement of Clerk with WorkOS (managed tier, fully-custom headless UI); generic-OIDC kept for self-host. Pre-prod ⇒ no data migration. Clerk kept working during the build (both providers coexist); final Clerk deletion = Phase 7. Each increment: prod-grade + tests green + here.

**Phase 7 status (Clerk rip-out) — COMPLETE + FULLY SCRUBBED:** ✅ 7a control-tower, ✅ 7b backends, ✅ 7c marketing, ✅ 7d marketplace FRONTEND, ✅ 7e cosmetic+SDK+docs, ✅ **7f DB schema + SDK parity + every last config/CI/Dockerfile/generated-doc**. **`clerk` now appears in ZERO places outside the historical `docs/*.md` plan/guide records + vendored deps (`.venv/winerror.py`) + build caches.** Renamed the DB columns (`clerk_user_id`→`external_user_id`, `clerk_org_id`→`external_org_id`, `clerk_role`→`external_role`, `subscriber_clerk_id`→`subscriber_external_id`) + the `"clerk_org_id"` Stripe-metadata key + all index names + the `00014_clerk_identity_mirror.sql`→`00014_identity_mirror.sql` migration file + the `00067` subprocessor seed (Clerk→WorkOS). TS SDK gained `verifyWorkosWebhook` (full Python parity, 9 tests). All builds + tests green: control-tower vitest 227, marketplace 42, marketing tsc/eslint, both Go backends build+vet+test (incl. testcontainer DB tests on the renamed schema), Python SDK mypy(49) + pytest 226, TS SDK typecheck + vitest 154.

- [x] ✅ **Cycle 22 — `internal/loopeval` "orphan" — CLARIFIED, not a defect (LOW).** The backend audit flagged loopeval as built-but-unwired (no runtime caller). By design it is an **offline** loop-quality scorer (LE0): the agentic loop emits per-turn telemetry as structured logs (`loop.turn.telemetry`, `task_workflow.go:673`) + Prometheus metrics (`loop_metrics.go`), and `ScoreRun`/`Aggregate` compute the baseline/after report **offline** from that telemetry — exactly as its own doc-comment states ("the workflow emits the TurnRecords, this package computes the report"). It has unit tests (`loopeval_test.go`, green). Wiring an inline runtime caller would contradict the explicit offline/log-only design and touch the replay-sensitive hot loop path for no benefit; the genuine follow-on (a CI eval-runner + corpus that invokes `ScoreRun`) is deferred eval infrastructure, not a code defect. No code change — documented as intentional.

- [x] ✅ **ABSENT: agent-callable cross-org fabric TOOL — DONE + tested.** The cross-org fabric had an operator HTTP surface (`POST /v1/fabric/requests`) but **no builtin in `internal/tool/`**, so an agent couldn't fire a governed cross-org action mid-loop. Built the full governed path. **Key design (governance-first):** an agent must NOT hold or choose a peer's Ed25519 key/bearer, and today nothing stores outbound-peer trust material — so added an **operator-configured outbound-peer store**: migration **`00113_fabric_outbound_peer`** (tenant+target_org PK; endpoint, **vault-sealed bearer**, peer Ed25519 pubkey, key id, and an **`allowed_actions` allowlist** = the governance seam) + `actionfabric.PgPeerStore` (`Upsert`/`Get`/`List`/`Delete`; seals via the shared `mcphub.CredentialVault`, blank-bearer-on-update preserves the existing secret, `List` masks it). **Tool:** `internal/fabrictool/Router` — resolves the peer for `fabric/{target_org}/{action}`, **fails closed** on unknown peer / non-whitelisted action, forwards only the agent's `{args, purpose}`, fires through the **same `actionfabric.Requester`** (build+sign+send+**verify the peer's signed receipt**), and returns the receipt (a governed ALLOW/DENY + proof, **no raw enterprise data** — inference-safe). **Wired:** `tool.FabricToolRouter` interface + `isFabricToolName` + supervisor **dispatch case** (before MCP, like `a2a/`/`connector/`) + **advertisement** (generic schema; peer/allowlist enforced at call time); operator **management endpoints** `GET/PUT/DELETE /v1/fabric/peers` (agent_admin scope for writes, bearer sealed); **bootstrap** builds the peer store + router when the GAL signing key **and** shared vault are present (`WithFabricRouter`). **Tests:** `fabrictool/router_test.go` — happy path (peer trust material resolved from store, agent supplies none), allowlist deny fails-closed (initiator never called), wildcard, unknown peer, store-error propagation, malformed name / bad tenant, advertise; compile-time interface assertion. **Verification:** `go build ./...` clean, `go vet` clean, gofmt clean, **`fabrictool` + `tool` + `handler/http` + `actionfabric` tests GREEN**; migration is standard `CREATE TABLE` (00110 pattern) with valid goose markers (no live PG container up this run to `goose up`, but the Go store round-trips it in tests). **⇒ Agents can now fire governed cross-org actions mid-loop against operator-whitelisted peers, keys never leaving the platform.** Uncommitted (owner commits).

## Cycle 11 — Memory Strategy & Roadmap (`docs/Actrone_Memory_Strategy_And_Roadmap.md`)

Implementing the memory roadmap end-to-end, each item prod-grade (CLAUDE.md) → tests green → marked ✅ → next. All uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Now-1: Local-first everywhere — a zero-service/in-memory default for Python (parity with TS).** `MemoryManager.create()` now runs fully in-process with **no Redis, no Qdrant, no API key** ("memory that never phones home"). **New code (`actrone-memory-py`):** (a) `in_memory.py` — `InMemoryStore` implementing **both** L1 (hot session) + L2 (cold semantic) tiers with the *same* blended `relevance_w·cos + recency_w·recency` ranking, 30-day recency window, `max_turns` cap, and an in-process SET-NX-EX summary-cooldown lock as the Redis/Qdrant backends (+ a shared `cosine_similarity` mirroring the TS helper, tolerant of length mismatch); (b) `HashingEmbedder` in `l2/embedder.py` — dependency-free, deterministic FNV-1a hashing vectorizer, L2-normalised, 256-dim default (exact parity with the TS `LocalEmbedder`), the new default `embedding_provider="hashing"`; (c) `protocols.py` — `L1Store`/`L2Store` `runtime_checkable` Protocols so the manager depends on the store **seam**, not concrete backends. **Config:** added `backend: "memory"|"redis_qdrant"` (default `memory`) + `hashing_dimensions`; **flipped defaults** to local-first (`embedding_provider` `openai`→`hashing`), documented as a conscious behavioural change in `CHANGELOG.md` (existing deploys set `ACTRONE_BACKEND=redis_qdrant` + `ACTRONE_EMBEDDING_PROVIDER=openai`). **Manager:** `create()` branches on `backend`; extracted `_build_embedder()`; Qdrant collection now sized from `embedder.dimensions` (not a fixed constant) so hashing/local embedders provision correctly; `__init__` typed to the protocols. **Exports:** `InMemoryStore`, `HashingEmbedder`, `Embedder`/`LocalEmbedder`/`OpenAIEmbedder`, `L1Store`/`L2Store`, `cosine_similarity`. **Docs:** README leads with the zero-service quickstart + a "Going to production" switch section; `pyproject` description + `examples/quickstart.py` (already `create()`-based, now genuinely offline). **Verification:** `ruff check src` clean, **`python -m mypy src/actrone_memory` strict clean (22 files)**, **full unit suite 122 passed**, coverage **89%** (gate 80%); added `tests/unit/test_in_memory.py` (25 cases — protocol conformance, L1 cap/isolation/metadata/lock TTL, L2 threshold/rank/limit/content-type/delete, + an **end-to-end zero-service `create()`** proving store→retrieve with no services/key) and 7 `HashingEmbedder` cases (determinism, L2-norm, empty-text, word-overlap ranking, batch). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Now-2: Provenance-typed facts v1 in OSS (both libs) — source + PII/sensitivity type + local erase.** Governance now *starts* in the free wedge (the graduation seed). **Shared vocabulary (language-neutral spec, kept in lockstep):** `MemorySource` (`user`/`assistant`/`tool`/`summary`/`injected`/`extracted`/`reflection`/`imported`/`unknown`, or a namespaced string like `"import:crm"`) + `Sensitivity` (`none`→`low`→`pii`→`sensitive`, mirroring the hosted DPE tiers). **Python (`actrone-memory-py`):** added `source: str="unknown"` + `sensitivity: Sensitivity="none"` to `MemoryEntry` (backwards-compatible defaults); `inject_memory()` gains `source`/`sensitivity` params; summaries tagged `source="summary"`; Qdrant `upsert`/`upsert_batch` payloads persist both + `search` reads them back; new **`MemoryManager.erase_agent_memories(agent_id, session_id=None)`** local right-to-erasure (hard L2 delete + optional L1 clear); `L2Store` protocol + `delete_agent_memories` (Qdrant/InMemory already implement); exported `MemorySource`/`Sensitivity`/`ContentType`. **TS (`actrone-memory-ts`):** same `MemorySource`/`Sensitivity` + `source`/`sensitivity` on `MemoryEntry`; `injectMemory()` params; `eraseAgentMemories()`; `L2Store.deleteAgentMemories` on `InMemoryStore` + `QdrantL2Store` (filter-delete, extended the `QdrantLike` structural `delete` to accept `{filter}`); payload persist+readback; exports updated. **Verification:** **PY** ruff `src` clean, `python -m mypy src` strict clean, **full unit suite 126 passed** (89% cov) incl. new provenance round-trip / erase / summary-source cases; **TS** `npm run typecheck` + `typecheck:examples` clean, **`vitest run` 28 passed** (new provenance-defaults/round-trip, erase, Qdrant payload persist + `deleteAgentMemories` isolation), `npm run build` (ESM+DTS) green. Both CHANGELOGs updated. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Now-3: Optional basic fact extraction (LLM-gated), to the shared spec (both libs).** OSS is now credible beyond turn storage: turns → durable atomic facts. **Shared spec (new, canonical):** `docs/memory-spec/extraction.v1.md` — versioned prompt + output JSON schema + parser rules + storage mapping; both libs expose `EXTRACTION_SPEC_VERSION="1.0"` and the identical system prompt, so "improve once = update the spec + eval." **Python:** new `extraction.py` (`FactExtractor` protocol, `OpenAIFactExtractor` via JSON mode, `ExtractedFact` pydantic model, `parse_facts()` defensive parser — envelope-or-bare-list, skips malformed, clamps 2000 chars / 20 facts, never raises); config `extract_facts: bool=False`; `MemoryManager.extract_memories()` public API + auto-extraction riding the summarise cadence (cost-bounded, lock-deduped); facts stored `content_type="fact"`, `source="extracted"`, LLM-classified `sensitivity`; `_build_extractor()` gates on opt-in + OpenAI provider; new `"fact"` content type; exports added. **TS:** mirror `extraction.ts` (`FactExtractor`, `OpenAIFactExtractor` over a structural `ChatCompleterLike` — no hard `openai` dep, `parseFacts` accepting `topic_tags`+`topicTags`); `MemoryManager.create({extractor})` + `extractMemories()`; `"fact"` content type; exports added. **Verification:** **PY** ruff+`mypy src` strict clean (23 files), **unit suite 142 passed** (90% cov) incl. 18 extraction cases (parser edge cases, provenance round-trip, summarise-ride, ConfigurationError gate, `_build_extractor` gating); **TS** typecheck + examples clean, **`vitest run` 38 passed** (parser spec cases incl. snake/camel tags + clamp/cap, extract round-trip, no-extractor throw). Both CHANGELOGs updated. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Now-4 / §5: Public benchmark + shipped eval harness (both libs) — "the only memory lib that ships its own quality eval."** Memory quality is now a measurable, **CI-gated** property, runnable fully offline (local backend, no services/keys). **Python:** new shipped package `actrone_memory.benchmark` — `dataset.py` (bundled LongMemEval-style dataset: 2 cases, 15 memories, 14 queries with known-relevant ground-truth ids + sensitivity), `harness.py` (`run_eval()` embedder/backend-agnostic — accepts any manager incl. competitor adapters; recall@k / precision@k / MRR + latency p50/p95 via nearest-rank percentile), `__main__.py` (`python -m actrone_memory.benchmark`), `README.md` (methodology + **honest** win/lose framing + how to plug Mem0/Zep/Letta). Measured **recall@5 = 0.929, MRR = 0.929** with the default hashing embedder. **CI gate:** `test_benchmark.py` asserts recall@5 ≥ 0.85 + MRR ≥ 0.80 (+ metric-unit tests for `_aggregate`/`_percentile`/`hit_rank`/`format_table`). **TS:** `src/benchmark.ts` mirrors the **same dataset** + `runEval()` + `formatTable()`; `benchmark.test.ts` gates recall@5 ≥ 0.85 / MRR ≥ 0.80. **Verification:** **PY** ruff+`mypy src` clean (27 files), **unit suite 150 passed** (90% cov); **TS** typecheck clean, **`vitest run` 41 passed**. This is the shared eval anchoring the "one spec + one eval, two implementations" coherence decision (§1). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Now-5 / §4b: `create-actrone-app` (greenfield) + `actrone-memory add` snippet-printer (existing-project).** The §4b "clarity, not cleverness" onboarding — selection not detection, non-destructive. **Recipe registry (`actrone-memory-ts/src/recipes.ts`):** one identical-shape recipe (`install → paste → run`, ending with the hosted-upgrade seed) per framework — core, Vercel AI, LangChain, LangGraph, Mastra, LlamaIndex, OpenAI Agents, Genkit — driven by the **real** adapter API in `adapters.ts`; single source of truth for CLI + docs + examples; exported (`RECIPES`/`getRecipe`/`renderRecipe`/`renderStandaloneFile`/`listFrameworks`). **CLI (`src/cli.ts`, new `bin: actrone-memory`):** `add <framework> [--write <file>]` (prints recipe, or writes **ONE new self-contained file** — refuses to overwrite, never reads/edits existing code) + `list`; pure `runCli(args, io)` with injected IO for testability; shebang preserved through tsup (new `cli.ts` build entry); guarded `main` (won't run on import). **`create-actrone-app/` (new top-level, zero-dependency plain-ESM package):** `lib/scaffold.mjs` (pure `scaffold()`→file map: package.json + tsconfig + src/agent.ts local-first starter + README + .gitignore; `validateProjectName`; framework-aware README hint to `npx actrone-memory add`), `bin/create-actrone-app.mjs` (shebang, arg parse, refuses non-empty dir, writes tree, prints next steps), README. **Verification:** **TS** typecheck clean, **`vitest run` 51 passed** (recipe-registry invariants + CLI: print/list/unknown-fw/`--write` new-file/overwrite-refusal/no-path/usage), **`npm run build` green with shebang preserved** (`node dist/cli.js add langgraph` verified). **create-actrone-app** `node --test` **6 passed**; **bin smoke-tested end-to-end** (scaffolds 5 files, refuses non-empty dir). Verified the current (2026) interface shapes via web search, then added real, structural (no-hard-dep) conformances in `actrone-memory-ts/src/adapters.ts`: (1) **`loadMessages()`** — structured role-tagged `ChatMessage[]` (system memory block + turns), the raw material every memory interface needs; (2) **`langchainChatHistory()`** — a genuine `BaseListChatMessageHistory` (`getMessages`/`addMessage`/`addMessages`/`clear`) that pairs human+AI messages into one governed turn and, given `messageClasses`, emits **real `HumanMessage`/`AIMessage` instances** (not stand-ins); (3) **`llamaindexChatMemory()`** — a genuine `BaseMemory` (`get`/`getAll`/`put`/`reset`) with buffer-then-flush mirroring the Python LlamaIndex adapter. The remaining TS frameworks (Vercel/Mastra/OpenAI-Agents/Genkit/LangGraph) have no formal memory class — their system-string/graph-state helpers already ARE the idiomatic integration. **Verification:** typecheck + `typecheck:examples` clean, **`vitest run` 55 passed** (structured messages, LC `BaseChatMessageHistory` contract incl. real-instance emission, LI `BaseMemory` get/put/reset), **`npm run build` (ESM+DTS) green**. Exports + CHANGELOG updated. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Near-term-1: Closed the `memorydepth` retrieval loop — `Retrieve` + wired into context assembly + exposed via the hosted memory API.** The structured-memory engine was a sophisticated *write-only* store (Extract→Ingest→consolidate, Erase, **no read path**); now the loop is closed. **Core (`internal/memorydepth`):** `Service.Retrieve(tenant, agent, RetrieveQuery)` — **temporal-aware** (active facts only, `valid_to IS NULL`), **provenance-carrying** (each `Fact` keeps `SourceTurnID`), optional scope + free-text filter, ranked most-confident-then-recent, bounded by `DefaultRetrieveLimit` (32); new `FactRepo.RetrieveFacts` (Postgres `RetrieveFacts` on `memory_facts` — partial-index-served, ILIKE substring across subject/predicate/object) + pure `FormatFact` renderer + `RetrieveQuery` type. **Context assembly (`RetrieveContext` activity):** new `retrieveMemoryFacts` hook injects the agent's active facts into episodic context (best-effort, no-op unless `EnableMemoryDepth` wired); `RetrieveContextInput.TenantID` now **always populated** (was loop-agent-only) so the read is tenant-scoped for every agent. **Hosted API:** `GET /v1/agents/{agentID}/memory/facts?query=&scope=&limit=` → `MemoryHandler.Facts` (nil-safe: returns `enabled:false` + empty when memory-depth off; carries id/subject/predicate/object/confidence/scope/**source_turn_id**/valid_from); wired `memDepthSvc` (hoisted) into `NewMemoryHandler`; route registered; OpenAPI contract added (`retrieveMemoryFacts` op + `MemoryFact`/`MemoryFactsResult` schemas). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, **memorydepth tests green** (Retrieve defaults-limit/forwards-query/propagates-error, FormatFact), **http Facts handler tests green** (provenance exposed + query forwarded + disabled→empty), service/workflow suites green, OpenAPI YAML validates. **⇒ Facts extracted+consolidated on the write path now flow back into agent context and are queryable via the API.** Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Near-term-2: Turned `memorydepth` ON by default (retired the off-by-default gate now that the loop is closed).** Flipped `cost_opt.memory_depth` viper default `false`→`true` so a default hosted deployment gets genuine extraction + temporal + governed structured memory (not a write-only store). **Prudent + honest:** kept it fully reversible (`cost_opt.memory_depth=false` restores old behaviour); extraction stays **best-effort and off the served answer** (one cheap judge-model call per final turn, non-blocking, never fails a task) and the read path is a plain DB query — so default-on degrades gracefully (missing judge model / empty fact set ⇒ no facts, warnings only). Verified `migrations/00027_memory_facts.sql` exists (table present). Updated the `CostOptConfig` header comment to record MemoryDepth as the deliberate exception to the "all levers OFF ⇒ byte-identical serving path" invariant, with the stated rationale (loop closed), and expanded the field doc (extraction + retrieval + API, cost, disable switch). **Verification:** `go build ./...` clean, config + service + workflow + http tests green; no test locks the old default (safe flip). *(Note: platform-wide live-eval + per-tenant cost shadow-measurement via the savings ledger remains the ops rollout gate — the code default is now on, reversibly.)* Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Moat-A: Outcome-gated self-improving retrieval — the flagship.** Memory now learns which facts actually improved task outcomes and re-ranks by it — a signal only Actrone's governed loop produces. **Mechanism (`internal/memorydepth`):** migration **00118** adds `helpful_count`/`unhelpful_count` to `memory_facts`; `Fact.Utility()` = Laplace-smoothed success rate (neutral 0.5 prior until evidence); pure `OutcomeRank(facts, RankWeights, limit)` orders by `0.6·confidence + 0.4·utility` (stable, recency tie-break); `Service.Retrieve` now **over-fetches 4× by confidence then outcome-re-ranks** to the limit; new `Service.RecordRetrievalOutcome` + `FactRepo.RecordFactOutcome` (tenant-scoped `UPDATE … helpful_count+1`). **End-to-end wiring (the loop closes):** `retrieveMemoryFacts` returns the injected fact **IDs**; `RetrieveContextResult.StructuredFactIDs` threads them through `StreamLLMInput.Context`; on a **grounded final answer** `RecordMemoryOutcome(helpful=true)` rewards those facts so they rank higher next turn (`ActivityService.RecordMemoryOutcome` — best-effort, parses/guards ids). **Honest scope:** this is the positive-feedback variant (facts present during good outcomes get reinforced); **negative attribution on an eval-gate failure is the documented refinement** (the eval pass/fail isn't yet threaded to the attribution point). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, **memorydepth tests green** (Utility prior + helpful>0.8, OutcomeRank surfaces the proven fact first + limit truncation, RecordRetrievalOutcome forward + empty-noop, over-fetch limit), service/workflow/http suites green; migration is standard `ALTER TABLE` (00110 pattern). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Moat-B: Verifiable memory (memory ⨝ the tamper-evident audit spine).** Every structured-memory retrieval that drives a decision is now recorded on the tenant's **HMAC-chained audit spine** — the agent can prove what it knew, when, why it believed it, with provenance. **Impl (`internal/service/memorydepth_hook.go`):** `auditMemoryRetrieval` appends a `memory.retrieval` `audit.AuditEvent` (via the existing `*audit.Spine`, same idiom as `recordCompactionReceipt`) whenever facts are injected into context — recording, per fact, `{id, source_turn_id, content_sha256, confidence, valid_from}`. **Privacy-preserving + verifiable:** stores a **content hash**, not the raw (already-governed) fact content — so the log proves *which* memories drove the decision and that they weren't altered, without re-duplicating PII into the audit trail. Best-effort + nil-safe (no spine / append failure never breaks retrieval). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean; new `memorydepth_audit_test.go` (2 tests) asserts the appended event is **HMAC-signed** (tamper-evident), carries provenance + content hash, does **not** leak raw content, and that nil-spine / empty-facts are no-ops; full service + memorydepth suites green. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Moat-F: Provable selective forgetting — "the only memory that can prove it forgot."** MemoryAgentBench shows most systems FAIL selective forgetting; ours is now **policy-driven AND cryptographically provable**. **Policy-driven (`internal/memorydepth`):** `ForgetPolicy{Scope, MaxAge, MinConfidence}` — tombstones (closes `valid_to`, so no longer retrieved) active facts that are stale (older than MaxAge) OR low-value (confidence below floor), tenant+agent+scope scoped; `Service.ApplyForgetPolicy` → repo `ForgetByPolicy` (`UPDATE … SET valid_to=now … RETURNING id`); returns a `ForgetReceipt{scope, policy, forgotten_ids, count, at}`. **Safety guard:** an inactive (empty) policy is a deliberate no-op — a misconfiguration can never forget the whole memory. **Cryptographically provable (`internal/service`):** `ActivityService.ForgetMemory` runs the policy then `auditMemoryForget` appends a **HMAC-signed `memory.forget` proof** to the tamper-evident spine — recording *which* facts were forgotten, under which policy, and when (**ids + policy only, never the forgotten content** — proves the forgetting without retaining what was forgotten). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, **tests green** — `ForgetPolicy.Active` logic, `ApplyForgetPolicy` forwards + receipt captures policy, inactive-policy no-op guard, and the forget-proof is HMAC-signed + carries forgotten ids/count; memorydepth + service + http suites pass. *(Scope note: age/confidence decay covered; sensitivity-based forgetting would need a sensitivity column on the Go `memory_facts` — a schema extension.)* Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **§4 Moat-D/E/C: Belief-revision + confidence · Reflection · Governed federated org memory.** **D — belief-revision + calibrated confidence:** `BeliefRevision` + pure `DeriveRevisions(existing, consolidation, now)` pairs each superseded fact with its same-key replacement (old→new object + old→new confidence), wired into `Ingest` so every "change of mind" is logged auditably; `Fact.Uncertain(threshold)` is the hook for uncertainty-aware retrieval. **E — reflective insight memory:** `Reflector` seam + pure `GroupBySubject` pre-aggregation + `Service.Reflect` (loads active facts → reflector synthesises higher-level insights with provenance → persists them through the governed write path); `SetReflector` opt-in (LLM-backed in prod, off the hot path). **C — governed federated org memory:** `RetrieveQuery.IncludeShared` + `Repository.SharedOrgFacts` (tenant's `ScopeOrg` facts, no agent filter) folded into `Service.Retrieve` (union + `dedupeByID`, then outcome-ranked) and **wired live** in the retrieval hook — so an agent recalls institutional knowledge its siblings wrote, within the org's trust boundary (no cross-boundary leak; access control = tenant scoping). **Honest scope:** intra-org sharing shipped; cross-*org* federation across trust boundaries is the extension (rides the existing A2A/fabric spine). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean, **tests green** — `DeriveRevisions`/`Uncertain`, `GroupBySubject`, `Reflect` (no-op without reflector, reasons over active set, persists insight via consolidation), `Retrieve` IncludeShared merges org facts + doesn't leak them without the flag; memorydepth + service + http + workflow suites pass. Uncommitted (owner commits). **⇒ Verifiable Cognitive Memory pillars A/B/C/D/E/F all landed.**

## Cycle 12 — Memory-roadmap deep wiring audit (3 agents) + gap closure

Spawned 3 deep-audit agents (Python lib / TS lib+scaffolder / Go backend end-to-end). They confirmed the retrieval loop + Moat A are **truly wired end-to-end** (verified call graph: retrieve → `RetrieveContextResult.StructuredFactIDs` → `StreamLLMInput.Context` → `RecordMemoryOutcome` on a grounded final answer; hosted `/memory/facts`), and flagged real gaps in B/C/E/F + GDPR-erase being **built-but-unwired orphans**. Closed them prod-grade:

- [x] ✅ **Moat F (forgetting) + GDPR erase — wired to real callers.** Were orphans (no HTTP/scheduler/DSR caller). Added agent-admin endpoints: **`POST /v1/agents/{id}/memory/forget`** (`ForgetFacts` → `ActivityService.ForgetMemory`, rejects an empty policy) and **`POST …/memory/erase`** (`EraseFacts` → new `ActivityService.EraseMemory` → `memorydepth.Erase` + **provable `memory.erasure` audit proof**, subject hashed never retained). Principal from `middleware.PrincipalFromContext`.
- [x] ✅ **Moat E (reflection) — concrete engine + caller.** Was interface-only (no impl, no caller, `s.reflector` always nil). Added concrete LLM `memoryReflector` (router-backed, groups facts → synthesises insights → `ParseFacts`), **wired via `svc.SetReflector` in `EnableMemoryDepth`** (no longer a permanent no-op), triggered by **`POST …/memory/reflect`** (`ReflectMemory`).
- [x] ✅ **Moat C (federated org memory) — writer added.** Read path was live but **nothing wrote `ScopeOrg` facts** (always empty). Added **`POST …/memory/facts`** (`WriteFact` → `ActivityService.IngestStructuredFact`) to seed org-scoped institutional knowledge through the governed write path → `SharedOrgFacts` now returns data.
- [x] ✅ **Moat B (verifiable memory) — explicit coupling.** The audit spine only exists under `emaop.enabled=true` (governance layer), so proofs silently no-op'd with `memory_depth` on + EMAOP off. Added a **startup `Warn` (`cost_opt.memory_depth.audit_disabled`)** making the coupling explicit instead of silent. (Verifiable memory/forgetting proofs require the governance spine — by design; now surfaced.)
- **Wiring:** `NewMemoryHandler` widened with a `memoryAdmin` interface (satisfied by `*ActivityService`); 4 routes registered agent-admin-only in `main.go`; OpenAPI contract added for all 4 (`forgetMemoryFacts`/`eraseMemorySubject`/`reflectMemory`/`writeMemoryFact`, YAML validates). **Tests:** `memory_test.go` +5 (forget guard+forward, erase subject-required+forward, reflect invoked, org-scope write forward, nil-admin error). `go build ./...`+`go vet` clean; memorydepth/service/http/workflow/config suites green.

(3) Documented the `langchainChatHistory` cast for `RunnableWithMessageHistory`. typecheck + 55 vitest green.

**Honest remaining (flagged, NOT closed — genuinely larger work):** per-framework **CI-tested example dirs** (`/examples/<fw>/`, §4b.3 trust pillar); **semantic/vector retrieval** for structured facts (current is ILIKE+confidence, not embeddings); the **knowledge-graph tier** (`memory_graph_edges` table exists, zero Go code); a **public comparative benchmark vs Mem0/Zep/Letta** (§5 — bundled eval is our own regression floor, honest but not comparative); a **scheduled decay job** for autonomous forgetting (currently endpoint-triggered); richer **reflection provenance** (source-fact ids) + a reflection **scheduler**; and **eval-fail negative attribution** for Moat A. ## Cycle 13 — Closing the "honestly remaining" memory gaps (Cycle-12 audit follow-through)

- [x] ✅ **GAP: Semantic/vector retrieval for structured facts (was ILIKE + confidence only).** memorydepth now ranks facts by **meaning**. Migration **00119** adds `embedding double precision[]` to `memory_facts` (nullable — old/embedderless facts fall back). `Fact.Embedding`; pure `cosine` + `SemanticRank(facts, queryEmbedding, weights, limit)` (blends `0.5·semantic + 0.25·confidence + 0.25·utility`, stable recency tie-break). New `memorydepth.Embedder` seam + `SetEmbedder`; **write path** embeds each new fact's `subject predicate object` text on `Ingest` (best-effort — an embed failure stores the fact without a vector, never blocks the write); **read path** `Retrieve` enters semantic mode when an embedder + query are present, **drops the ILIKE substring filter** (so it ranks by meaning over top-confidence candidates, not brittle substring matching) and blends the three signals, else falls back to `OutcomeRank`. Repo writes/reads the embedding column in `ApplyConsolidation`/`RetrieveFacts`/`SharedOrgFacts`. **Wiring:** `ActivityService` injects a `memoryEmbedder` (wraps its OpenAI `embed()`) via `EnableMemoryDepth`, **guarded by an API key present** (no always-failing calls otherwise); the task input is threaded as the query into `retrieveMemoryFacts`. **Verification:** `go build ./...`+`go vet` clean; memorydepth tests green incl. `cosine` edges, `SemanticRank` orders query-similar first + keeps embeddingless facts, and `Retrieve` semantic mode reranks by meaning + clears the ILIKE filter; service/http suites green. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **GAP: Knowledge-graph tier (table existed, zero Go code).** The `memory_graph_edges` relationship tier is now live. Pure `DeriveEdges(facts) []GraphEdge` (subject —predicate→ object, weight=confidence, provenance to `source_fact_id`; skips empty subject/object). `Ingest` **derives + upserts edges** for each new fact (best-effort — a graph write never fails the fact ingest). Repo `UpsertEdges` (idempotent `ON CONFLICT (tenant,agent,from,to,relation) DO UPDATE` via `pgx.Batch`) + `GraphNeighbors` (outgoing edges from a node, case-insensitive, most-weighted first); `Service.Neighbors`. **Hosted API:** `GET /v1/agents/{id}/memory/graph?node=&limit=` → `MemoryHandler.Graph` (nil-safe enabled:false; edges as from/to/relation/weight/source_fact_id) + OpenAPI (`memoryGraphNeighbors`). **Verification:** `go build ./...`+`go vet` clean, YAML validates; tests green — `DeriveEdges` (derivation + empty-object skip + provenance), `Ingest` upserts the edge, `Service.Neighbors` forwards, handler returns neighbours + requires `node`. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **GAP: Richer reflection provenance (insights were tagged only "reflection").** Reflected insights now trace back to the source facts they were synthesised from. Pure `ReflectionProvenance(sourceFacts, maxIDs)` → `"reflection:<id1,id2,…>"` (capped, default 10; `"reflection"` when none); the concrete `memoryReflector` sets it as each insight's `SourceTurnID` via `ParseFacts`. **Verification:** build+vet clean; test covers empty → `"reflection"`, ids present, and the cap. memorydepth + service suites green. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **GAP: Eval-fail negative attribution for Moat A (was reward-only).** Outcome-gated memory now learns from the **real** governed signal in **both** directions. Removed the blind `helpful=true` at the extract site; moved attribution into `runGroundednessCheck` (the eval gate), which now calls `RecordMemoryOutcome(…, StructuredFactIDs, dec.Allowed)` on the floors-aware gate path and `…, passed` on the legacy verifier path — **rewarding** injected facts when the answer passes the eval floor and **penalising** them when it fails. This makes the utility signal genuinely bidirectional (facts present during failures are down-weighted), sourced from the eval/groundedness gate — the governed signal only Actrone has. **Verification:** build+vet clean, service + memorydepth suites green (added a penalty-path assertion to `RecordRetrievalOutcome`). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **GAP: Autonomous memory-hygiene scheduler (forget/reflect were endpoint-only).** New `service.MemoryHygiene` runs a periodic governed pass over every agent with active memory: **decay-forgetting** (Moat F — each forget records its provable-forgetting audit proof via `ActivityService.ForgetMemory`) + **optional reflection** (Moat E, LLM-gated). `RunOnce` enumerates agents (new `memorydepth.Service.ActiveAgents` → repo `SELECT DISTINCT tenant_id, agent_id … WHERE valid_to IS NULL`), applies per-agent (best-effort — a per-agent error never aborts the pass, honors `ctx.Done()` between agents for prompt shutdown-drain); `Start(ctx, interval)` runs once immediately then on each tick, stopping on cancellation (matches the cost-optimizer refresh pattern). **Config (all OFF by default):** `cost_opt.memory_hygiene_interval` (0=disabled), `memory_decay_max_age_days`, `memory_decay_min_confidence`, `memory_reflect_enabled`; wired in `main.go` inside the memory-depth block. **Verification:** build+vet clean; service/memorydepth/config suites green — `RunOnce` applies decay per active agent, and nil-service / disabled / interval-0 are safe no-ops. New `actrone_memory.benchmark.compare` — `MemorySystem` protocol (two async methods: `inject_memory` + `search_memories`), `run_comparison({name: system}, cases, k)` runs the **same** ground-truth dataset against every system → per-system `EvalReport`, and `format_comparison` renders the head-to-head table. ## Cycle 16 — Framework version-compatibility verification system (SSOT + drift gate + interface canaries + Docker matrix)

Closes the honest limitation "we don't matrix-test every framework version × combination; declared ranges reflect what's tested" — with a layered system across all 4 SDK/OSS packages.

- [x] ✅ **Single source of truth — `compatibility.{yaml,json}` per package.** Every framework's `floor`/`cap`, primary pip/npm package, tested `matrix` versions (floor/current/next), README label, and stable `contract` symbols live in ONE file per package (`actrone-memory-py`/`actrone-py` YAML; `actrone-memory-ts`/`actrone-ts` JSON). Previously the declared range (pyproject/package.json), the documented range (README), and the tested version (CI) were three hand-maintained copies that could drift.

- [x] ✅ **Drift gate — `scripts/check_compat.py` / `check-compat.mjs` (fails CI on divergence).** Asserts the pyproject **extras** (Python) / optional **peerDependencies** (TS) + the README compatibility matrix agree with the SSOT: exact ranges, subset rule for extras that bundle supporting deps (openai/litellm), `bundled:false` for the deliberate MAF pre-1.0 deferral, `non_framework_peers` allowlist (@ai-sdk/openai, @temporalio/*, …). Framework-free/no-network. **Wired into all 4 package CIs** + `npm run check-compat`. Unit-tested (Python `test_compat.py`, TS `compat.test.ts`) proving it catches wrong-range / missing-README-row / undocumented-extra / non-optional-peer, and that **the real repo is in sync** (16 PY frameworks each; 11 TS each).

- [x] ✅ **Interface canaries — `test_interface_canaries.py` (manifest-driven, matrix-only).** For each framework's `contract` symbols (incl. the stepwise shims' overridden Model interfaces — `smolagents.models:Model.generate`, `agno.models.base:Model`, `strands.models:Model.stream`), assert they still exist against the REAL installed SDK. Gated behind `COMPAT_MATRIX=1` (skip in every normal run — they assert a specific pinned version). **fail-not-skip**: if the framework is installed but our target module/symbol is gone, it FAILS (not skips) — the machine version of the drivers' "verified against crewai 1.15.1" note.

- [x] ✅ **Version matrix + Dockerized runner — `ci/compat-matrix/{matrix.py,run.sh}`.** Tests each framework at THREE range boundaries (floor `==x` / current `>=x,<cap` / next `>=x --pre`) in ISOLATED environments (frameworks are mutually incompatible — e.g. agent-framework vs openai — so they never share a venv). `matrix.py emit` expands a package's manifest into 48 jobs (16×3) as GitHub-Actions matrix JSON; `run.sh` installs the package + one pinned framework + runs that framework's canary. **PROVEN END-TO-END VIA DOCKER:** memory-py langchain **current (0.3.30)** → canary PASS (exit 0); **next (1.x)** → canary FAIL with precise message (exit 1, `allow_fail` early-warning).

- [x] ✅ **GitHub Actions `.github/workflows/compat-matrix.yml`.** Layer 1 (every PR): the drift gate. Layer 2 (scheduled Mon 06:00 + dispatch): the version matrix — `emit-matrix` → dynamic `fromJson` fan-out, `continue-on-error: matrix.job.allow_fail` so `next` (pre/next-major) only warns. New framework releases caught within a week (Renovate-friendly).

- [x] ✅ **REAL FINDING acted on — the matrix caught an over-promise.** The Docker canary proved our memory-py langchain range `<2` was **wrong**: the `ActroneMemory` adapter binds to `langchain_core.memory.BaseMemory`, which LangChain **removed in 1.x**. Narrowed the SSOT to `<1` (manifest cap + pyproject extra + README, gate re-green); `next:"1.0"` matrix job is now the tracked early-warning for a langchain-1.x adapter. This is the whole system delivering value on day one.

- [x] ✅ **Latent H5 bug fixed (found by the matrix).** `actrone-memory-py/tests/conftest.py` hard-imported `redis_store`/`qdrant_store` at collection — so ANY test run without the now-optional redis/qdrant extras (the local-first path; the matrix) failed to collect. Removed the dead imports (they were unused). Added `pyyaml` to both Python dev extras (compat tooling dep).

**Verification (aggregate, all green):** memory-py gate in-sync (16) + **185 passed / 10 skipped** (canaries skip) + ruff/mypy clean; actrone-py gate in-sync (16) + **248 passed / 16 skipped** + ruff/mypy clean (incl. stepwise drivers + canaries); memory-ts gate in-sync (11) + **67 tests**; actrone-ts gate in-sync (11) + **170 tests** + typecheck OK; all 5 workflow YAMLs valid; Go `internal/domain` green; **Docker end-to-end proof green (current pass / next fail)**. Uncommitted (owner commits).

## Cycle 17 — Parity plan final audit sweep (3 parallel agents) + fixes

Spawned 3 agents to independently deep-audit the parity implementation (Python surfaces / TS surfaces / cross-surface enum+compat). **Headline: NO wiring gaps and NO orphans** — every adapter/driver is exported and reachable; enum ↔ harness-driver ↔ BYOF ↔ SSOT chains are internally consistent; all 4 drift gates exit 0; matrix.py emits 48+48 well-formed jobs. The findings were polish/consistency, now addressed:

- [x] ✅ **Contract tests added for the 3 legacy OSS adapters that lacked them** (§5 gap): `tests/contract/test_{langchain,langgraph,crewai}.py` (importorskip + mock-manager delegation + import-guard), mirroring the existing pattern. memory-py contract suite now **13 passed / 13 skipped**.
- [x] ✅ **Interface-canary coverage completed** — added the missing `contract:` symbols in `actrone-py/compatibility.yaml` for the stepwise shims that had `contract: []`: `semantic_kernel` (`ChatCompletionClientBase` + `ChatHistory`), `autogen` (`ChatCompletionClient`), `pydantic_ai` (`Model`), `llamaindex` (`CustomLLM`). Canary count 16→21.
- [x] ✅ **CANARY BUG the audit surfaced, fixed** — `google` is a shared PEP-420 namespace (google-genai/google-adk), so `importorskip("google")` wrongly succeeded when only google-genai was present, making the `google_adk` canary FAIL instead of SKIP. Fixed root-detection in BOTH canary files to use the first TWO components for `google.*`. Verified: canaries now correctly skip google_adk when google-adk absent.
- [x] ✅ **3 stale comments/docstrings corrected**: `agent.go` HostedFrameworks header ("every value has a stepwise driver" → accurate "stepwise subset is StepwiseFrameworks; claude_agent_sdk is the sole encapsulated-only"); `actrone-py/harness/frameworks/__init__.py` docstring (listed 3 frameworks → all 13); `actrone-memory-ts/adapters.ts` docstring (added the 4 Cycle-15 frameworks).
- [x] ✅ **Pre-existing contract-test lint debt cleaned** (surfaced while touching the dir): SIM117 nested-`with` + E501 + F401 across test_autogen/dspy/haystack/llamaindex; added a `tests/contract/*` E402 per-file-ignore (importorskip-before-imports is idiomatic). `ruff check src/ tests/contract/ scripts/` clean.

**Documented residuals (conscious, not defects — reported honestly) — ✅ ALL THREE CLOSED in Cycle 20 (2026-07-11), see below:**
1. **§2.1 Tier-1 on the 7 legacy OSS-py adapters** (langchain/langgraph/crewai/autogen/llamaindex/dspy/haystack) — they ship Tier-2 native conformance only, no per-framework framework-free string helper. The framework-agnostic Tier-1 path exists at the library level (`MemoryManager.retrieve_context` / the `core` recipe); retrofitting the shared `BaseActroneMemory` onto 7 battle-tested adapters is deliberately NOT done in a verification sweep (regression risk > value).
