## Cycle 21 — Memory Depth Competitive Plan: Axis A (dense default recall) — OSS Python (2026-07-12)

Implementing `docs/Actrone_Memory_Depth_Competitive_Plan.md`. Axis A ("kill the lexical default") landed prod-grade in the Python OSS lib (`actrone-memory-py`), grounded in a 2026 web sweep (fastembed 0.8.0 / bge-small-en-v1.5 384-dim ONNX; fastembed-js 2.1.0; ms-marco-MiniLM cross-encoder). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **A1 — In-process ONNX embedder (`FastEmbedEmbedder`).** `fastembed` (onnxruntime, **no torch/GPU**), default model `BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5` (384-dim). Lazy model download + cache, offline thereafter; dimension resolved from fastembed's catalogue with a probe fallback; CPU encode offloaded to a thread pool. New `[onnx]` extra (`fastembed>=0.7,<1`), added to `all` + `non_framework_extras` + mypy overrides.
- [x] ✅ **A2 — Graceful local chain + default flip.** New `build_local_embedder()` degrades **ONNX → sentence-transformers → hashing**, catching both `ImportError` (extra absent) and model-fetch failure (air-gapped first run) — so `embedding_provider="local"` never fails and never makes an unavoidable network call. Default provider flipped `hashing` → **`local`**; base install (no extras) still resolves to hashing, so the "no services, no API key" guarantee holds. Wired in `MemoryManager._build_embedder`.
- [x] ✅ **A3 — Hybrid retrieval via Reciprocal Rank Fusion.** New `retrieval.py` (pure, dependency-free): `bm25_scores` (Okapi BM25, no external index), `reciprocal_rank_fusion` (weighted RRF), `fuse_channels`, `hybrid_rank`. Fusion re-ranks **within the dense-threshold-admitted set** (dense cosine + BM25 lexical + recency), so the "only relevant returned" contract is unchanged while an exact-keyword match the embedder under-ranks can surface. Wired into **both** backends — `InMemoryStore.search` (recomputed cosine) and `QdrantStore.search` (server cosine) share `fuse_channels`, so they rank identically. Config `hybrid_retrieval=True`; `query_text` threaded through the `L2Store` protocol + both call sites.
- [x] ✅ **A4 — Optional cross-encoder rerank (opt-in).** New `rerank.py`: `CrossEncoderReranker` (fastembed ONNX `TextCrossEncoder`, default `Xenova/ms-marco-MiniLM-L-6-v2`) reorders the top-K of an over-fetched set (precision lift, not recall — honest constraint documented). `build_reranker` degrades to `None` (logged) if the extra is missing, so `create()` stays robust. Config `rerank_enabled=False` / `rerank_model` / `rerank_top_k=20`; wired into `retrieve_context` (Phase 3b) + `search_memories` (over-fetch → rerank → truncate) via `_maybe_rerank`.

**Verification (all green):** `ruff check src tests` clean · `mypy src/actrone_memory` clean (43 files) · **212 passed / 13 skipped** (was 190; +22 new across `test_embedder_local`, `test_retrieval`, `test_rerank`; **zero existing-test fallout from the default flip or hybrid reordering**) · `check_compat` in sync (16). fastembed/onnxruntime not installed in the base venv, so the ONNX embedder + reranker are covered by stubbed-`fastembed` unit tests + the graceful-fallback path the base venv actually exercises (a real-model end-to-end runs under Gate E / the compat-matrix, per the repo's importorskip pattern). **Still open on Axis A: A5 (hosted Go reranker stage) + TS parity (A1–A4 in `actrone-memory-ts`).**

- [x] ✅ **A1/A2 (TS) — `FastEmbedEmbedder` + `buildLocalEmbedder`.** In-process ONNX via the `fastembed` (fastembed-js 2.x, onnxruntime) optional peer, default `bge-small-en-v1.5`, dimension resolved by a probe. `buildLocalEmbedder()` degrades **ONNX → hashing `LocalEmbedder`** on import/model-fetch failure, so it never throws and needs no API key. `fastembed` added as an optional `peerDependency` + `peerDependenciesMeta` + `non_framework_peers` (compat gate green). ### Cycle 21 (cont.) — A5 hosted Go reranker stage → Axis A COMPLETE

- [x] ✅ **A5 (hosted Go) — reranker stage in `memorydepth.Retrieve`.** New optional `Reranker` interface (`Rerank(ctx, query, facts, topK) → []Fact`) + `reranker` field + `SetReranker` (mirrors the optional `Embedder` seam). Inserted **between the over-fetch and `SemanticRank`** in the semantic branch: when wired, it narrows the over-fetched pool to the most relevant `DefaultRerankTopK`=20 candidates (precision), then `SemanticRank` produces the final order **keeping the outcome-gated term** (cosine × confidence × learned utility — Moat A). A rerank error is **non-fatal** — falls back to the un-reranked pool so retrieval never fails on the reranker. Nil by default → existing behaviour unchanged. The concrete cross-encoder is injected at construction (like the embedder), so `internal/memorydepth` stays model-dependency-free. **Verification:** `go build` + `go vet` + `gofmt` clean; 2 new tests (`RerankNarrowsPoolBeforeSemanticRank`, `RerankFailureFallsBackToCandidates`) + full `internal/memorydepth` suite **green**.

**⇒ Axis A (dense default recall) is COMPLETE across all three surfaces:** OSS Python (A1–A4), OSS TypeScript (A1–A4), hosted Go (A5). Every claim is measured under Gate E before any "beats X" marketing (E2 not yet run — see Gate E).

### Cycle 21 (cont.) — Axis B: bitemporal knowledge graph (hosted Go) — COMPLETE

Match Graphiti's bitemporal edges, layered on the audit spine so each invalidation is provable (Moat B/F). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **B1 — migration `00120_memory_graph_bitemporal.sql`.** Adds `valid_from`/`valid_to` (valid time) + `ingested_at`/`invalid_at` (transaction time) to `memory_graph_edges`, all nullable + backfilled (`ingested_at=valid_from=created_at`). Replaces the plain `UNIQUE(tenant,agent,from,to,relation)` with a **PARTIAL unique index over active edges** (`WHERE invalid_at IS NULL`) so a superseded edge + its replacement coexist as history; adds the as-of/provenance index `(tenant,agent,from_node,relation,invalid_at)`. Robust constraint-drop via a `pg_constraint` lookup (Postgres-generated name). **Validated against a real Postgres 16 container** (`b1_validate.sql`): backfill correct, partial-unique *allows* an invalidated+active pair yet *rejects* a second active edge (`unique_violation`), Down rolls back — "B1 VALIDATION PASSED".
- [x] ✅ **B2 — temporal invalidation.** `GraphEdge` gained `ValidFrom/ValidTo/InvalidAt/IngestedAt`; `DeriveEdges` stamps `ValidFrom` from the fact. `Repository.UpsertEdges` now inserts the temporal columns + `ON CONFLICT … WHERE invalid_at IS NULL` (active-index inference). New `Repository.InvalidateEdgesBySourceFacts` closes (`invalid_at=now`) the edges of superseded facts. `Service.Ingest` calls it for `res.Supersede` **before** upserting the new edges — a contradiction closes the old edge instead of overwriting.
- [x] ✅ **B3 — "as-of" filtering.** `Repository.GraphNeighbors` + `Service.Neighbors` take an `asOf *time.Time`: nil ⇒ currently-believed edges (`invalid_at IS NULL`); set ⇒ edges true **and** believed at that instant (`valid_from ≤ T AND (valid_to IS NULL OR valid_to>T) AND (invalid_at IS NULL OR invalid_at>T)`) — "what did the agent know about X on date T?". Interface + fakes updated.
- [x] ✅ **B4 — HTTP surface.** `GET …/memory/graph?node=…&as_of=<RFC3339>` parses `as_of` (400 on malformed), threads it to `Neighbors`, and the `edgeView` response now carries `valid_from`/`valid_to`/`invalid_at`/`ingested_at` (omitempty) so callers see the provenance/validity of every edge.

**Verification:** `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt` clean across `internal/memorydepth` + `internal/handler/http`; **migration applied+reverted on live PG16**; new unit tests green — `IngestClosesSupersededEdges`, `NeighborsPassesAsOfThrough`, handler `Graph_AsOfPassedThrough` + `Graph_RejectsBadAsOf`; full `memorydepth` + `handler/http` suites pass. **Axis B COMPLETE.**

Migration `00121_memory_blocks.sql` (one row per tenant/agent/session/label, `version` bump per edit for audit + optimistic concurrency). Repository `GetBlocks`/`GetBlock`/`SaveBlock` (upsert bumps version).

Wired in `cmd/orchestrator/main.go`.

### Cycle 21 (cont.) — Axis D: Cognee-style ECL + policy-bearing ontology (hosted Go)

Same pipeline shape as Cognee (Extract → Cognify → Load), but the ontology is **governed** — typed *and* policy-bearing — so the graph knows what it's allowed to remember. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **D1 — semantic chunking + cross-doc coreference** (`ecl.go`, pure + tested). `SemanticChunks` packs paragraphs→sentences into coherence-preserving chunks ≤ maxChars (no fixed-size splits that straddle a fact); `CanonicalizeEntities` rewrites alias mentions ("she"/"the CEO"→"Jane") to one canonical node **before** extraction (cross-document coreference). These are the framework-free Cognify building blocks the document-extraction path composes.
- [x] ✅ **D2 — typed ontology constraining extraction** (`ontology.go` + wired into `Ingest`). `Ontology` (entity types + relation types with required subject/object types) + `ValidateFact` (strict when a relation is modelled; permissive for unmodelled predicates so partial ontologies don't drop facts). **Wired:** `Service.SetOntology` + `Ingest` drops facts whose subject/object type violates a modelled relation (logged `ontology.rejected`).
- [x] ✅ **D3 — structured-source → graph** (`structured.go`, pure + tested). `DeriveFKEdges(TableSchema, rows)` turns relational rows into graph edges via foreign keys (node `"<table>:<pk>"`, one edge per non-null FK) — "a database becomes part of the graph". The dlt/connector transport that *pulls* the rows is the external, swappable piece (Cognee uses dlt); the FK→edge derivation is ours.
- [x] ✅ **D4 — policy-bearing ontology** (`ontology.go`). Each `EntityType` carries `Sensitivity` (none/low/pii/sensitive) + `RetentionDays` + allowed `Purposes`. `PolicyFor` inherits the **most-restrictive** sensitivity + **shortest** retention across a fact's entities and **refuses a purpose-violating extraction** (purpose-binding). This is the enterprise wedge — memory over business data that's provenance-typed + policy-bound.

**Verification:** `go build ./...` clean; `vet`/`gofmt` clean; new `ecl_ontology_test.go` green — `ValidateFact`, `PolicyFor` (sensitivity/retention inheritance + purpose refusal), `Ingest_OntologyDropsTypeMismatch`, `SemanticChunks` (+empty/small), `CanonicalizeEntities`, `DeriveFKEdges` (FK edges, null-FK + missing-PK skipped); full `memorydepth` suite passes. **Honest scope:** the ECL primitives (D1 chunking/coreference, D3 FK-derivation) + governed ontology (D2 wired, D4 computed+purpose-enforced) are built and tested; the standalone **document-ingestion endpoint** (chunk→extract→load over raw docs), the **dlt structured-source connector** (external Python transport), and **persisting** sensitivity/retention onto facts for retention-driven forgetting are the remaining wiring on top of these primitives.

### Cycle 21 (cont.) — Gate E: measured-not-asserted benchmark harness

The acceptance gate — no "beats Zep/Mem0" claim ships until it's benchmarked. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **E1 — real competitor adapters** (`benchmark/competitors.py`). `Mem0Adapter` (2026 `MemoryClient` — `add`/`search` by `user_id`), `ZepAdapter` (`zep_cloud` — `graph.add`/`graph.search` on the user graph), `LettaAdapter` (`letta_client` — archival `passages.create`/`search`). Each maps the two-call `MemorySystem` surface (`inject_memory`/`search_memories`) onto the vendor API, **lazy-imported + key-gated** (heavy deps + API keys stay out of the offline CI gate) and **stub-testable** via an injected `client`. A defensive `_as_list`/`_first_id` normalise the vendors' differing response shapes (list / `{results}` / `{edges}` / bare object).
- [x] ✅ **E2 — external-dataset loader** (`benchmark/dataset.py::load_cases`). Loads a documented JSON case format (`memories` + `queries` with ground-truth `relevant_ids`) so **LOCOMO / LongMemEval** — converted into that shape — drive the *same* recall@k / precision@k / MRR harness, keeping the metric identical across datasets (the reproducibility Gate E needs). Validates + raises on malformed input.

## Cycle 22 — Postgres Write-Scaling Plan (2026-07-12)

Implementing `docs/Actrone_Postgres_Write_Scaling_Plan.md` — a **staged, trigger-gated** ladder (climb a rung only when a metric threshold is hit). Most rungs are `[OBS]` (observability), `[MANUAL]` (apply/console/load-test), or reuse existing patterns; the one substantive new **[CODE]** artefact — the Tier-2 horizontal-write lever — is built + tested. Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **Tier 2 [CODE] — tenant-shard dimension in `regionpool` (the decisive horizontal-write lever).** New `internal/regionpool/shard.go`: a **consistent-hash** `ConsistentHashResolver` (tenant→shard via a virtual-node hash ring + **explicit per-tenant overrides** for pinned/rebalanced tenants) and a generic `ShardRegistry[P]` that routes a tenant to one of several Aurora clusters *within* a plane — mirroring the region `Registry` pattern exactly: **inert-by-default** (no shards ⇒ every tenant uses the primary pool, identical to a single cluster), **fail-safe** (a resolver error returns the default, never a wrong shard), generic/unit-testable. Fixed a real consistent-hashing bug in the process: FNV-1a avalanches poorly on the near-identical virtual-node keys, so a **splitmix64 finalizer** was added — without it, scaling 3→4 shards reshuffled 51% of tenants; with it, **<40%** (≈the ~1/N minimum). **Verification:** `go build`/`vet`/`gofmt` clean; 6 tests green — deterministic assignment, even distribution (4 shards ±40%), **minimal reshuffle on scale-out**, explicit-override wins, no-shards→default, `ShardRegistry` route+fallback+fail-safe. This reuses the existing tenant-keyed routing (no cross-tenant joins) so it is an *extension*, not a re-architecture; the region-cutover `Migrator` already provides the tenant-move primitive for shard rebalancing.

**Status of the remaining rungs (honestly — this is a trigger-gated infra ladder, not all-code):**
- **Tier 0 `[OBS]`** — Aurora writer/contention/connection/replica-lag/SLO signals: the Prometheus/Grafana/Alertmanager stack + multi-window burn-rate rules already exist ([[observability-db-replicas]]); the specific Aurora CloudWatch/PI panels are dashboard config, apply-gated.
- **Tier 1** — **already-present foundations the plan builds on, CONFIRMED:** reader/writer pool split (`repository.WithReader`), the **outbox** pattern (`00063_billing_reconciliation`), and declarative **RANGE partitioning** (`00010_access_audit_log`). RDS Proxy (DSN/config), vertical headroom, and per-table partitioning are `[MANUAL]`/triggered — and in-place partitioning of the idempotency-keyed append tables (`usage_events`, `savings_events`) is deliberately **not** shipped as a naive migration: their unique-key would have to include the partition key, weakening dedup, so it's a design-sensitive maintenance-window step (create-partitioned + backfill + swap), correctly trigger-gated.
- **Tier 3 `[MANUAL]`** — Aurora Limitless (native) / Citus (portable) / distributed-SQL: evaluation-gated, only if Tier 2 is outrun.
- **Orthogonal `[CODE]`** — reader-pool offload (`WithReader`, present) + cache-aside w/ TTL+single-flight (`CLAUDE.md §3.3`); CQRS derived read models are a larger async-projection build, triggered by read-pressure.

**⇒ The Postgres plan's one substantive new [CODE] lever (Tier-2 sharding) is implemented + tested; the rest is a trigger-gated ladder of apply/console/load-test steps + existing patterns, authored to this environment's ceiling (no live Aurora fleet here to apply against).**

### Cycle 22 (cont.) — ShardRegistry wired into repository.DB (live-but-single-shard by default)

- [x] ✅ **Tier-2 shard routing wired end-to-end (dormant until shard DSNs are set).** `PoolRouter` gained a second, independent routing dimension: `SetShardRegistry`/`ShardActive` + `ForTenant` now composes **region × shard** — region resolves the base pool, then (when a shard registry is active) the tenant's shard cluster refines it; a nil shard pool falls through to the region base, so it's fail-safe and inert by default. `repository.DB` gained `WithShardPools(shard→DSN)` (opens+pings a pool per shard, fail-fast, no-leak), `ShardPools()`, `SetShardRegistry`, and `Close()` cleanup. `DatabaseConfig.ShardDSNs` + `database.shard_dsns` JSON-env parse + fail-fast validation + viper default. `main.go` bootstrap opens the shard pools and activates a `ConsistentHashResolver`-backed `ShardRegistry` **only when `database.shard_dsns` is configured** — so a single-cluster deployment is byte-for-byte unchanged, and horizontal write scaling "lights up" purely by adding shard DSNs (no code change). **Verification:** `go build ./...` + `vet` + `gofmt` clean; new `TestPoolRouter_ShardDimension` (region×shard composition, unsharded fall-through, resolver-error fail-safe) + full `repository`/`regionpool`/`config` suites green.

### Cycle 22 (cont.) — Postgres Write-Scaling: remaining [CODE] items built + wired

All self-contained code items across the tiers, so the write-scaling ladder is code-complete and only infra/apply remains (documented in the infra runbook). Uncommitted (owner commits).

- [x] ✅ **Tier 2 — explicit shard-assignment persistence (completes the sharding lever).** Migration `00122_tenant_shard_assignments` (tenant→shard pins with reason/assigned_by/timestamps + reverse index) + `ShardAssignmentRepository` (`LoadAll`→`map[tenant]shard`, `Assign` upsert, `Unassign`). Wired into `repository.DB` + **main.go bootstrap loads the pins as consistent-hash `overrides`** (a load failure is non-fatal → pure hash), so a hot tenant can be pinned / a rebalance move recorded authoritatively, winning over the hash.
- [x] ✅ **Tier 1 — partition-maintenance helper** (`internal/partition`). Pre-creates upcoming month partitions for RANGE-partitioned write-heavy tables (idempotent `CREATE … PARTITION OF … IF NOT EXISTS`) so inserts never fall into the catch-all DEFAULT partition; optional retention-drop (detach-then-drop, off by default). Interpolated identifiers are regex-guarded (§5.1). **Wired** in main.go: a daily `Maintainer` for `access_audit_log` (3 months ahead). 5 pure unit tests (month bounds, Dec→next-year, unsafe-identifier reject, sequence count).
- [x] ✅ **Orthogonal — cache-aside helper** (`internal/cacheaside`). Generic `Get[T](cache, singleflight.Group, key, ttl, jitter, loader)`: cache hit serves cheaply; a miss loads through the backing store **exactly once under a stampede** (single-flight) and caches with a **jittered TTL** (thundering-herd guard, §3.3); cache errors degrade to loading (cache is pure optimisation). `Cache` interface = any Redis/in-memory store. 4 tests incl. 50-goroutine single-flight collapse → 1 load. (Reader/writer split `WithReader` already present; routing more lag-tolerant reads to it is a per-repo audit noted for the runbook.)

**Verification:** `go build ./...` + `vet` + `gofmt` clean; `repository` (+shard-assignment), `partition`, `cacheaside`, `regionpool`, `config` suites all green. **⇒ Every self-contained [CODE] item of the Postgres Write-Scaling Plan is now built + wired + tested; the residual is provisioning/apply/load-test — see the infra runbook.**

Two read-only audit agents swept both plans. **Postgres: clean** (every CODE item implemented, wired onto the live tenant query route, tested; only CQRS had no artifact). **Memory Depth: found Axis D + A5 were built + tested but NOT activated in production** (inert seams with no caller), and Cycle-21's "Axes A,B,C,D … wired" over-stated D. Fixes below. Uncommitted (owner commits).

**Postgres gap closed:**
- [x] ✅ **CQRS async-projection primitive** (`internal/projection`) — `Projector` (checkpointed, resumable, idempotent: apply-in-order, save-progress-on-error so a retry resumes with no gap) + `Source`/`Handler`/`Checkpoints` seams + `MemoryCheckpoints` (in-mem) + durable `repository.ProjectionCheckpointStore` (migration `00123_projection_checkpoints`, exposed on `DB`). 5 tests (apply+checkpoint, resume, stop-on-error saves progress, batch limit). The reusable read-model building block (concrete projections attach on a read-pressure trigger — same reusable-primitive status as `cacheaside`/`partition`). Also added a `database.shard_dsns` parse unit test (sweep note #4).

**Memory Depth — Axis D now ACTIVATED in production (was inert seams):**
- [x] ✅ **D2 activated** — `EnableMemoryDepth` now calls `svc.SetOntology(DefaultOntology())`, so `ValidateFact` runs on every ingest. `DefaultOntology()` classifies PII (email/phone → sensitivity `pii`, 365-day retention) and is **permissive** (no strict relations / purposes) so it never drops a legitimate fact.
- [x] ✅ **D4 enforced + persisted** — `Ingest` now runs `PolicyFor` on each fact **before redaction** (so PII values still match), stamps the inherited sensitivity class, and refuses purpose violations (fires when a purpose-bound ontology + a threaded purpose are present). New `Fact.Sensitivity` + migration `00124_memory_fact_sensitivity` (metadata-only ADD COLUMN, transactional) + `00125_memory_fact_sensitivity_index` (partial index built **CONCURRENTLY** under `-- +goose NO TRANSACTION`, so a large live `memory_facts` is not write-locked during the build) + repo persistence (INSERT + all 3 SELECTs). **Both migrations validated on PG16** (column adds metadata-only, index builds `indisvalid=t` + partial; negative control confirms CONCURRENTLY requires NO TRANSACTION; both Downs clean). Tested: `Ingest_DefaultOntologyStampsPIISensitivity`.
- [x] ✅ **D3 wired** — `Service.IngestStructured` (DeriveFKEdges → UpsertEdges) + `ActivityService.IngestStructuredMemory` + **`POST …/memory/structured`** endpoint (RequireRole AgentAdmin). Tested: service `IngestStructured_DerivesAndUpsertsEdges` + handler `IngestStructured`.
- [x] ✅ **D1 wired** — `ActivityService.IngestDocument` composes `SemanticChunks` → per-chunk extraction → governed `Ingest` + **`POST …/memory/documents`** endpoint. Tested: handler `IngestDocument`.

**Honest status of the two remaining sweep items (genuinely not code-fixable here):**
- **A5 hosted reranker — NOW BUILT + WIRED (2026-07-12), inert-by-default.** A provider-neutral, Cohere-shape cross-encoder client (`internal/service/rerank.go` — `memoryReranker` + `RerankConfig`) is wired into `EnableMemoryDepth` (`SetReranker` gated on `RerankConfig.Enabled()`, mirroring the embedder). Config: `ORCHESTRATOR_MODEL_RERANK_BASE_URL` / `_RERANK_MODEL_ID` (default `bge-reranker-v2-m3`) / `_RERANK_API_KEY`. Blank BaseURL ⇒ OFF (embedding-blend ranking only, byte-identical to before); a rerank error degrades to the un-reranked pool (never fails retrieval). The one wire protocol (`POST {base}/v2/rerank` → `{results:[{index,relevance_score}]}`) covers self-hosted **Infinity** + managed **Cohere/Jina/Pinecone**. 7 client tests (reorder+truncate, auth header on/off, non-200→error, empty→passthrough, bad/dup index guard, empty-input noop) + the pre-existing memorydepth insertion/fallback tests. **What remains is deploy-only:** stand up a rerank model service (runbook **§6.21** — recommends self-hosted Infinity + `bge-reranker-v2-m3` in-VPC so query+facts never leave the tenant boundary) and set the env var. Native-TEI/Voyage wire variants are a documented additive follow-up.

### D2 — EMAOP chat strictness (SHIPPED, corrects the earlier "deferred" note)

- [x] ✅ **D2 — chat strictness wired end-to-end.** Solved the "chat shares the `/v1/tasks` autonomous path" problem with a safe, **additive `interactive` marker** threaded exactly like the existing `Preview` flag: `AgentChat` → `tasksApi.submit(…, interactive=true)` → `POST /v1/tasks {interactive}` → handler `req.Interactive` → `AgentTaskInput.Interactive` → the workflow's new `systemPromptForRun()` appends `domain.WithChatStrictness()` to the system prompt **ONLY when interactive**. The directive (guarantee #3 topic-guard + soft #2): stay within the agent's purpose, politely decline + redirect off-topic requests, and never *offer* actions the user isn't authorised for. **Default false ⇒ byte-identical for every autonomous/scheduled run**, and determinism-safe (old histories replay with `Interactive=false` = base prompt; the hard directive only appears on new chat tasks). Guarantee #1 (single-agent) is already routing-bound (`/agents/[id]/chat`); the HARD execution RBAC (approvals/promotions) remains `RequireRole`-gated server-side — the directive stops the model from *offering* disallowed actions, the server still *blocks* them. Tests: 3 domain (`WithChatStrictness`) + 1 workflow (`systemPromptForRun` interactive vs not) + handler tests green; control-tower `tsc` clean. (Full hard RBAC-aware *tool-set* scoping — filtering the advertised tools by role, not just directing the model — is a further refinement noted for later; the directive + server enforcement covers the practical case.)

### D1 — two-way channel chat (SHIPPED, corrects the earlier "deferred" note)

- [x] ✅ **D1 — two-way channel chat wired end-to-end.** A plain inbound Telegram/WhatsApp message now routes to the channel's default agent as a **governed interactive turn**, and the answer is delivered back to the sender.
  - **Inbound detection**: `InboundAction.MessageText` + Telegram & WhatsApp `ParseInbound` surface a plain (non-command, non-approval) message as a chat action (kept distinct from link-handshakes + approval taps).
  - **Binding**: new `channel_configs.default_agent_id` column (migration `00127`, metadata-only nullable ADD COLUMN) + `InboundConfig.DefaultAgentID` + `ResolveInbound`. NULL ⇒ the channel ignores conversational messages (approvals/handshakes unaffected). Set/clear via `Store.SetDefaultAgent` + **`PUT /v1/channels/{provider}/default-agent`** (agent-admin) + `channelsApi.setDefaultAgent`.
  - **Governed bridge**: `Service.HandleInbound` gains a chat branch → `handleChatMessage` runs **asynchronously** (webhook returns immediately; the turn takes seconds): maps the sender to a **linked Actrone identity** (unlinked ⇒ ignored — never trust a raw chat id), then calls the `ChatResponder` seam. The concrete `ChannelChatResponder` submits a real **interactive task** (D2 `Interactive:true` ⇒ chat-strictness directive applies) and **awaits the Temporal workflow result** — so the FULL governed pipeline (MAL → policy → audit) runs, the linked user's permissions apply, and the answer comes back. **Idempotent**: the inbound event's dedupe key is the task idempotency key, so a replayed webhook returns the same answer (no double-run/double-bill).
  - **Delivery**: the answer is sent back to the sender via `NoticeSender.SendNotice` (reusing the per-channel send creds, same path as `NotifySummary`).
  - **Tests**: Telegram + WhatsApp plain-message parse, `handleChatMessage` no-op when unconfigured, responder output-extraction + idempotent-replay (5 new) + all channels/handler/domain/workflow suites green; control-tower `tsc` clean. BYOF-pod agents run their own loop (the directive is native-agent scoped — same boundary as D2); a Control Tower dropdown to pick the default agent is thin UI polish on top of the shipped API.

### D2 tool-set scoping — follow-ups pass (2026-07-14)

- [x] ✅ **Channel-chat RBAC scoping wired.** The D1 two-way channel chat now scopes its advertised tool set to the linked user, closing the earlier "ActingRole left empty" gap. `channels.Service.resolveActingRole` maps the **persisted** org role (the fine `actrone_role` is a JWT claim, absent off the HTTP path) to an EMAOP role via the existing `RoleChecker.IsOrgAdminBySubject`: org admin ⇒ `super_admin` (every tool), any other member ⇒ `agent_operator` (least privilege — a fail-closed bias right for a chat surface). Threaded through a new `actingRole` param on the `ChatResponder.Respond` interface → `ChannelChatResponder` sets `AgentTaskInput.ActingRole`, so the same wired menu-filter + execution-gate apply as the playground path. Fails **open** to "" (unfiltered) when no checker is wired or the lookup errors — a role read never breaks a chat. `RoleChecker` is already `db.Memberships` in `main.go`, so no bootstrap change. New `TestResolveActingRole_MapsOrgRole` (admin/member/error/no-checker) + updated `ChatResponder` doubles; channels + handler-http suites green, build + gofmt clean.
- [ ] ⚠️ **No-code Studio role-scoping — blocked by a foundational gap, NOT built (would be an unenforced control).** Investigated building per-capability role-scoping in the Agent Studio. **Finding:** the only *wired + tested* tool-role enforcement is `ToolSpec.AllowedRoles` (menu filter + execution gate, operating on `spec.tools`), but **nothing in the no-code product produces `spec.tools`**: `CompileStudioManifest` maps built-in capability IDs → `AgentCapabilities` flags and **drops** `integrations` and `custom_capabilities` entirely (no ToolSpec emission). And the capability gate that WOULD enforce the flag path — `dpe.EvaluateTier1` / `RequiredCapabilityForTool` — has **no production call site** (only tests). So a Studio "restrict capability to roles" control would compile to a field that nothing enforces (security theater — forbidden by the secure-by-construction bar). Delivering it correctly requires first implementing **Studio → governed `spec.tools` provisioning** (custom-capability bindings + integrations → real ToolSpecs, with their scopes/DPE-rules/tier-gates), which is a real, behaviour-affecting project — not a follow-up. `allowed_roles` remains the wired **power-user** path (Agent-File/SDK), consistent with its siblings (`allowed_domains`/`rate_limit`/`daily_budget_usd`, which likewise have no Studio/SDK/OpenAPI surface). Recommend scoping the provisioning work separately before adding the no-code UI.

Honest per the codebase reality I verified: the built-in tool registry is **empty**, so the *only* capability-backed built-in loop tool is the governed **`browser`** tool (all 4 browser tiers → `browsertool.ToolName`); connector-backed caps (email/tickets/docs/db/messaging/api/schedule) get their tool from the author's **connection** pick (decision §9.1), not the flag; voice + meeting bots + reports provision **no loop tool by design** (dedicated subsystem / inline output). Added `NeedsWiring` flag on the two genuinely-missing loop tools (**web_search** default + **execute_code** sandbox — see the new follow-on below). Added `domain.CapabilityNames()` (authoritative capability enumeration). Tests (`capability_tools_test.go`, 4): catalog covers EXACTLY `domain.CapabilityNames()` (no missing/stale/dupe — the FE↔BE drift guard), builtin-ToolName invariant, browser-tier mapping drift-locked to `browsertool.ToolName`, known/unknown lookup. **Also fixed a pre-existing stale test** (`runtime_test.go :: TestRuntime_AllHostedFrameworksAcceptStepwise`) that wrongly asserted `StepwiseFrameworks == HostedFrameworks` — `claude_agent_sdk` is intentionally encapsulated-only (parser.go:265 rejects `strategy: stepwise` for it), so rewrote it bidirectionally (`TestRuntime_StepwiseAcceptanceMatchesEnum`: stepwise frameworks accept, encapsulated-only reject). Full `internal/agent` + `internal/domain` suites green; gofmt clean. **Contract note:** the `StudioAgentManifest` OpenAPI schema is deferred to P1 (the fields it documents — `integrations`/`custom_capabilities`/`tool_role_access` — aren't read by the compiler until P1; documenting them now would describe unwired fields).
- [x] ✅ **NEW follow-on — wired the two genuinely-missing built-in loop tools (web_search + execute_code).** Both as **provider-neutral seams** (mirroring the OpenAI-compat model seam), registered ONLY when a provider is configured — so an unconfigured deployment omits them gracefully (the capability can still be met via a connected MCP), and there's no SSRF surface (endpoints are operator-configured, not model-controlled). R&D (web-searched, 2026): search via Brave/Tavily APIs; code sandboxes E2B/Modal/Blaxel/Daytona/Northflank all expose an HTTP execute surface.
  - **web_search** (`internal/tool/web_search.go`): `WebSearchProvider` iface + `BraveSearchProvider` (Brave Search API, clean JSON, no scraping) + governed `web_search` handler + schema. (Corrected the P0 note: web_search was already MCP-backed via the `brave-search` catalog server; this adds the managed built-in default.)
  - **execute_code** (`internal/tool/code_executor.go`): `CodeExecutor` iface + `HTTPCodeExecutor` (POST {language,code} → {stdout,stderr,exit_code}) + governed `execute_code` handler + schema. (Corrected the P0 note: the wasm sandbox runs pre-compiled WASM PLUGINS, not arbitrary code — arbitrary execution is delegated to a configured sandbox service where the microVM isolation lives.)
  - **Config** `ToolsConfig` (`ORCHESTRATOR_TOOLS_WEB_SEARCH_*` / `_CODE_SANDBOX_*`); **conditional registration** in `main.go`. **Catalog** updated: both → `BindingBuiltin` (drift-locked to `tool.WebSearchToolName`/`ExecuteCodeToolName` by a test); removed the now-vestigial `NeedsWiring` flag. Tests: httptest provider + handler (parse, default-language, missing-arg reject) + the drift test. Full build + tool/agent/config suites green; gofmt clean. **Deploy note:** the actual search-API key + sandbox service are operator/deployment config (external services), not repo code.
- [x] ✅ **P6 — Teams primitive (migration + repository + API + audit).** The platform-wide Teams foundation (§13), org-scoped like `org_memberships`.
  - **Migration `00129_teams.sql`** (§13.2): `teams` (nested via `parent_team_id` + self-parent CHECK, `visibility`, `external_group_id` for IdP-synced teams, `UNIQUE(org_id, slug)`), `team_memberships` (member|maintainer + `expires_at`), `team_role_grants` (team→EMAOP-role by scope), `team_join_requests` (partial-unique one-pending), append-only `team_audit` (FK-free `team_id`). **PG16-validated Up + Down**.
  - **`repository.TeamRepository`**: CRUD, membership, role grants, append-only audit, a SQL **cycle guard** (`wouldCycle` recursive CTE — self + descendant loops rejected as `ErrTeamCycle`), and the **downward-cascade closure** the PrincipalResolver consumes — `TeamIDsForUser` / `RoleGrantsForUser` return a user's direct (non-expired) teams **+ all descendants** (GitHub semantics: a parent member inherits the child's access/grants). Wired onto `DB.Teams`; added `UserRepository.GetByInternalID`. **6 integration tests green on real PG16** (CRUD/nesting, cycle guard, descendant cascade + inherited grants, expiry exclusion, grants/members, audit-outlives-delete).
  - **`/v1/teams` API** (`handler/http/teams.go`): teams CRUD + members + role-grants, **org-admin-gated** mutations (role grants **super_admin-gated** — privilege assignment), reads open to any member, RBAC vocabulary allowlisted (fail-closed), every mutation **audited**. Routes wired in `main.go`. Full orchestrator build + handler/repository suites green; gofmt clean. (Join-request endpoints + maintainer-delegated membership are a thin follow-up; the table + repo audit support them.)
- [x] ✅ **P9 — org-admin-configurable channel authorization + notification-delivery filtering (§13.8).** Closes the "approvals are org-admin-only" + "notifications aren't role-checked" gaps.
  - **Migration `00130_channel_authorization.sql`**: `channel_authorization_policies` (tenant + nullable agent_id override, `approvers`/`alert_audience` JSONB principal-sets, `delivery_mode` legacy|authorized, partial-unique default-vs-override). **PG16-validated** Up + Down + the precedence query (per-agent override beats org default; `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` upsert).
  - **Model + predicate:** `Principals{roles,teams}` + `AuthorizationPolicy` + a pure `authzPrincipal.matches(allow)` (super_admin — direct or team-granted — always; else a held role or team membership in the allow-set). `DefaultAuthorizationPolicy` = empty approvers + legacy delivery ⇒ **byte-identical to today**. 2 pure tests.
  - **Enforcement (one predicate, both points):** `applyApproval` gates the tap on `policy.Approvers` — empty approvers ⇒ only super_admin (org admin, via the coarse role map) passes = today's org-admin-only behaviour; a configured list widens it. `NotifyEscalation`/`NotifySummary` filter delivery to the `alert_audience` when `delivery_mode=authorized` (else all verified accounts), resolving each recipient's principal; broadcast channels post only when the audience is non-empty. Fail-open to legacy — a policy read never drops an alert. Team-based approvers/audiences via `channels.Service.WithPrincipalResolver` (the P7 resolver, structurally satisfied).
  - **Store + API:** `Store.Get/UpsertAuthorizationPolicy` (precedence + transactional delete-insert upsert) + `ListVerifiedTargetsWithUser`; `GET /v1/channels/authorization` (any member) + `PUT` (**super_admin-gated**, role-allowlist-validated). Routes + resolver wired in `main.go`; test double updated. Full build + channels/handler suites green; gofmt clean. (Per-agent approver override is stored+supported; the tap uses the org-default — a thin follow-up can pass the escalation's agent.)
- [x] ✅ **P7 — Teams enforcement generalisation (`PrincipalResolver` + `AllowedTeams`, principal-aware).** Made Teams *bite* on tool access, generalising the shipped role-only scoping to an acting **principal** = {direct role, team-inherited roles, team ids}.
  - **Predicate + field:** new `ToolSpec.AllowedTeams`; `toolAllowedForRole` → `toolAllowedForPrincipal(p, spec)` (OR-composition: an allowed role — direct or team-granted — OR membership of an allowed team; super_admin always; empty principal = autonomous = unrestricted). One predicate at BOTH points: `Supervisor.ToolDefinitionsForPrincipal` (menu filter) + `authorize` Step 2.5 (execution gate). `ActingPrincipal` threaded via context (`WithActingPrincipal`); `WithActingRole`/`ToolDefinitionsForRole` kept as back-compat shims.
  - **Threading:** `ActingTeamIDs` + `ActingTeamRoles` added to `AgentTaskInput` → `StreamLLMInput` / `ExecuteGovernedToolInput` (additive/optional ⇒ role-only default byte-identical + determinism-safe). `activity_service` uses `ToolDefinitionsForPrincipal` + `WithActingPrincipal(execCtx, …)`.
  - **Sourcing (snapshotted at submit for replay determinism):** new `PrincipalResolver` (`ResolveTeams` by org-external-id, `ResolveTeamsByTenant` for the channel path via a new `OrganizationRepository.GetByTenantID`) resolves a user → team ids (P6 closure) + roles granted to those teams in the platform/emaop scopes (fail-open). Wired into the **playground submit** (`TaskHandler.WithPrincipalResolver`) AND **channel-chat** (`ChannelChatResponder.WithPrincipalResolver`) — closing decision §9.4.
  - **Tests:** 2 new team-scoping tests (menu filter + execution gate: team membership grants access, team-granted role satisfies a role-restricted tool, non-member blocked with `ERR_TOOL_NOT_PERMITTED`, super_admin-via-team, autonomous unrestricted). Full build + tool/workflow/service/handler-http/repository suites green; gofmt clean.
- [x] ✅ **P11 — IdP directory-group → team sync (WorkOS Directory Sync / SCIM).** Mirrors an IdP directory group to an org-scoped, UI-immutable team so membership tracks the source of truth (§13.1).
  - **Repo:** `TeamRepository.UpsertSyncedTeam` (idempotent, keyed by (org, `external_group_id`), slug derived from the group id), `DeleteSyncedTeamByGroup`, `TeamIDByExternalGroup`. (No new migration — the `external_group_id` column shipped in P6's `00129`.)
  - **Service:** `IdentityService.WithTeams` + `OnDirectoryGroup{Upserted,Deleted,UserAdded,UserRemoved}` — resolve org (+ user) via the existing IdP mirror and upsert/delete the team + add/remove members. All **nil-safe** (no resolver ⇒ no-op) and **fail-skip** (an event for an unmirrored org/user/group is accepted + ignored, never fails the webhook).
  - **Webhook:** extended the `identitySyncer` interface + `WorkOSWebhookHandler` switch with `dsync.group.created/updated/deleted` + `dsync.group.user_added/user_removed`, tolerating both WorkOS payload shapes (nested `{user,group}` and flat `directory_*_id`). Wired `identitySvc.WithTeams(db.Teams)` in `main.go`.
  - Tests: 4 webhook-dispatch cases (upsert/delete/add/remove, both payload shapes) + a **PG16 integration test** (upsert idempotency, resolve-by-group, delete). Full build + handler suite green; gofmt clean. **Note:** directory-user→auth-user linking is best-effort by subject (WorkOS-config-dependent); the group sync — the primary value — is solid + tested. SCIM/Directory sync is the last backend phase; **all backend work in the plan is now complete.**

Errors clearly when provisioning is off or the agent has no stored manifest (SDK/YAML or pre-column ⇒ re-author in the Studio). Endpoint `POST /v1/agents/{agentID}/reprovision` (**agent_admin** gated), wired in `main.go`.
  - **Rollout:** the `EMAOP.StudioToolProvisioning` flag (P1) is the per-env lever (dev→staging→prod); the tier gate (P2) rides `Billing.Enforced` — both dormant/default-off so nothing changes until an operator opts in per environment. Test: Reprovision-disabled guard. Full build + agent/repository/handler suites green; gofmt clean.
- [x] ✅ **P2 — custom-capability governance completeness (dpe_rules + gate).** Honoured the custom-capability metadata P1 provisions but previously dropped, honestly per what each field actually is:
  - **`dpe_rules` → governance guidance:** `governanceGuidanceFromCustomCaps` collects the enabled custom capabilities' dpe_rules (deduped, blanks dropped) and `CompileStudioManifest` appends them to the synthesised system prompt as an explicit "Governance rules you must follow" section. (They are DESCRIPTIVE strings, not executable Tier-2 rules — those come from the tenant rule set — so surfacing them to the model is the honest, meaningful use; forcing them into `Governance.SyncRules`, which holds rule *ids*, would break the engine.)
  - **`gate` (min tier) → server-authoritative entitlement:** new `RequiredFeatureForCapability` (backend mirror of the FE `gate`, voice today) + `Service.guardCapabilityTiers` reject registration of an agent enabling a capability its tenant's plan lacks (via `billing.Entitlements.Can` + `OrganizationRepository.PlanTier`), with `ERR_ENTITLEMENT_REQUIRED`. Applied on ALL three register paths. **Dormant by default** — wired in `main.go` only when `cfg.Billing.Enforced`, and fails OPEN on a tier-lookup error, so a free-tier tenant's existing agents are never retroactively blocked.
  - **`scopes`:** enforced at connector CONNECT time (the OAuth scope allowlist) — a no-op at compile, documented in-code.
  - Tests: resolver (voice gated / others ungated), guidance (dedupe + empty), tier gate (dormant / free-blocked / scale-allowed / ungated-allowed), dpe_rules-in-prompt. Full build + `agent` suite green; gofmt clean.

  - **Team join-flow (§13.4) — was schema-only, now fully built.** Repo (`teams.go`): `TeamJoinRequest`, `MemberRole`, `CreateJoinRequest` (idempotent — repeat-while-pending returns the same row; `ErrAlreadyMember`), `ListJoinRequests`, `DecideJoinRequest` (**atomic** approve→membership in one tx; `ErrJoinRequestNotPending`). FE: `JoinRequestQueue.tsx` (rendered in `TeamDetail`, hidden for synced teams / non-managers via 403→null) + `teamsApi` join methods. **PG16 integration test** `TestTeamRepository_JoinFlow` (idempotent pending, approve→member atomic, deny→no member, guards) — green.
  - **Maintainer-delegated membership (§13.9) — now enabled.** `AddMember`/`RemoveMember` moved off coarse `RequireOrgAdmin` route middleware to the in-handler `canManageTeam` check (team-specific permission a route can't express), so a team maintainer manages their own team's membership; org-admins unaffected.
  - **Per-agent channel-authz override at the tap (§13.8) — mechanism existed, now enforced.** `ApprovalCard`/`Notice` carry an optional `AgentID`; `NotifyEscalation`/`NotifySummary` pass it to `GetAuthorizationPolicy` (per-agent policy overrides the org default via the store's precedence). At the approval **tap**, `applyApproval` resolves the escalation's agent via a new **optional** `escalationAgentLookup` capability (`*service.EscalationResolver.AgentForEscalation` → `EscalationRepository.GetByID`, best-effort → falls back to org default). Additive/non-breaking (type-assertion + fail-open). Test `TestAgentForEscalation` + updated `fakeEscStore`.
  - **Agent-ownership write-path parity (audit finding) — closed.** `PrincipalResolver.ValidateOrgTeams` rejects an `owner_team_id`/`visibility_team_ids` outside the caller's org; `SetOwnership` now fail-closes on it (matching the marketplace attribution check — the one place the ownership write path lacked own-org validation).
  - **Nits:** fixed the stale Tier-1 comment in `studio.go` (P4 deleted that block) + corrected the "00129–00132"→"00129–00133" migration count; TeamDetail members fetch now surfaces an `ErrorState`+retry instead of swallowing the error.
  - **Re-verification:** full `go build ./...` + `go vet` clean; repository/handler/channels/service/agent suites green; **all 10 PG16 integration tests green** (incl. join-flow); FE `npm run typecheck` green + provisioning vitest 8/8; gofmt clean. No new migrations (join-flow uses the shipped `team_join_requests` table). Nothing committed.

Migrations 00129–00133 PG16-validated; TS+Python parity unaffected (no client-facing endpoint contract change beyond the additive Studio manifest + agent-ownership surfaces, documented in the authoritative `openapi.yaml`). Remaining follow-ons are the standing published-openapi copy sync + the optional marketplace publisher-teams surface (explicitly incremental in §13.5). **Nothing committed — the tree is uncommitted per standing instruction.**

## Super Admin Dashboard (SAD) — implementation (2026-07-15)

Implementing `docs/Actrone_Super_Admin_Dashboard_Implementation_Plan.md` phase by phase. The SAD is the internal, system-wide operations control plane for Actrone staff — a NEW isolated app + `cmd/admin-api` service + isolated admin DB, entirely separate from the customer-facing Control Tower and its org-scoped EMAOP `super_admin` role. Reuse-first per the plan's decisions (audit spine, `oidc` verifier, region router). Each phase additive, prod-grade, tested; nothing committed.

- [x] ✅ **P0 — identity isolation & skeleton.** The authenticated, deny-by-default `cmd/admin-api` foundation. Exit criterion met: an authorised operator reaches `/v1/me`; everyone else gets a generic **404** (never 401/403, so the surface isn't confirmable — sysdesign §3.1).
  - **P0.1 — RBAC permission registry** (`internal/admin/rbac`): the 7 platform-operator roles (owner, super_admin, infra_admin, security_admin, billing_admin, support_engineer, read_only) × `resource:action` permissions, as **immutable-via-deploy code** (plan D5) — distinct from the org-scoped EMAOP roles. `Can(role, perm)` with wildcard support (`*:*` Owner, `resource:*`), deny-by-default, `RequiresDualApproval` (kill_switch/data_purge). Test **pins the entire sysdesign §6.2 matrix** + Owner-superset + separation-of-duties (only Owner writes RBAC; Security Admin reads audit but can't execute destructive ops) + dual-approval + validation (wildcards are grant-side only). 6 tests green.
  - **P0.2 — admin schema migrations** (`internal/admin/migrations`, a SEPARATE goose chain from the orchestrator's): `0001_admin_identity.sql` — `admin_users` (WorkOS-mirror, status/mfa CHECKs) + `admin_role_assignments` (one active role/user, role-vocab CHECK = defence-in-depth Level 3). Embedded FS; applied by admin-api at startup against `ADMIN_DATABASE_DSN`. **PG16-validated** (Up/Down + CHECK constraints) and **applied end-to-end via goose** in the integration test.
  - **P0.3 — config** (`internal/admin/config`): fail-fast typed config, `ADMIN_`-prefixed; prod **requires** a non-empty IP allowlist + a ≥32-byte audit master key. 3 tests (missing-required fails, dev defaults, prod hardening).
  - **P0.4 — JWT verifier** (`internal/admin/auth`): thin adapter over the shared `internal/oidc` JWKS verifier against the **isolated admin WorkOS project** (RS*/ES* only — rejects HS* alg-confusion; validates iss/aud/exp). Exposes **identity only** (sub+email); the role is resolved from our DB (D5), never a token claim.
  - **P0.5 — service skeleton** (`cmd/admin-api`): chi router, unauth infra endpoints (`/health/{live,ready}`, `/metrics`), private bind, conservative pool limits, security headers (`no-store`, HSTS, `X-Frame-Options: DENY`, nosniff), graceful SIGTERM/SIGINT drain, goose-migrate at startup.
  - **P0.6 — security middleware** (`internal/admin/httpapi`): app-layer **IP allowlist** (CIDR, 404 on miss, empty=dev-allow-all), **Authenticate** (verify→resolve operator+role, **404 on every failure**: bad token / unknown / suspended / no-role), **RequirePermission** (deny-by-default 404). Store dependency is an interface → fully unit-testable; **the whole 404-on-fail matrix + IP allowlist + permission gating is covered** (8+3+ cases green).
  - **P0.7 — repository + wiring** (`internal/admin/repository`): `admin_users` + role resolution (`GetUserByWorkOSID`, `RoleForUser` excluding expired, `UpsertUser`, `AssignRole` fail-closed on unknown role). Wired in `cmd/admin-api/main.go`; `/v1/me` + `/v1/rbac/roles` (gated on `rbac:read`) prove the stack. **PG16 integration test** (embedded-migration apply + identity/role/expiry/suspension/unknown-role) green. `.env.example` added (no secrets). Full `go build ./...` clean; gofmt clean; all admin unit + integration tests green.
- [x] ✅ **P1 — admin audit chain + gate (the foundation every write depends on).** A single platform-wide, tamper-evident, append-only action log, reusing the shipped Audit Spine's HMAC hash-chain pattern (plan D1). Exit criterion met: **every `/v1` request is provably logged before its response is delivered, and viewing the audit log is itself audited.**
  - **P1.1 — migration** `0002_admin_audit_log.sql`: `admin_audit_log` (full sysdesign §8.2 schema + HMAC chain columns) with **DB-enforced append-only** — rewrite RULEs (`ON UPDATE/DELETE DO INSTEAD NOTHING`) + `REVOKE UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE FROM PUBLIC` — so no one, including the table owner, can mutate a row; plus `admin_audit_anchors` for external checkpoints. **PG16-validated** (a raw UPDATE/DELETE is a proven no-op; row unchanged, `DELETE 0`).
  - **P1.2 — chain** (`internal/admin/audit`): HMAC-SHA256 hash-chain (each event's signature covers the prev event's id; NUL-delimited fields per sysdesign §8.3), `Append` (UUIDv7, prev-linkage, microsecond-truncated timestamps so DB round-trip matches), `Verify` (recompute + linkage → detects **modification** and **deletion**), `MerkleRoot` for anchoring. `PgStore` (append-only, inet-safe scan) + `MemStore`. 6 unit tests incl. tamper/deletion detection + Merkle tamper-evidence.
  - **P1.3 — audit gate** (`httpapi.AuditGate`, sysdesign §3.1 Layer 7): buffers the response, writes the event with the true outcome (`success`/`failure`/`blocked`), and **blocks the response on a log-write failure** — the invariant "no response leaves the SAD without a durable log". Sits after Authenticate + before RequirePermission so **denied attempts are recorded as `blocked`**. Handlers enrich via `SetAuditAction/Resource/Change`. 3 tests: delivers+logs, **block-on-fail hides the body**, records blocked.
  - **P1.4 — anchoring + nightly verify** (plan D2): `RootAnchor` interface + `NoopAnchor` (S3 Object-Lock WORM is the deploy-time impl behind the seam — Hedera avoided); `Anchorer.Checkpoint` (Merkle root every N events → `admin_audit_anchors`); `PeriodicVerify` ticker job (verify + checkpoint + alert-on-break, sysdesign §15). Test: checkpoints exactly every N.
  - **P1.5 — wired**: `AuditGate` on all `/v1`; `GET /v1/audit` (viewer, gated `audit:read`, **self-audited**) + `GET /v1/audit/verify` (Merkle-proof backing); chain + anchorer + nightly `PeriodicVerify` started in `cmd/admin-api/main.go` (key domain-separated from `ADMIN_AUDIT_MASTER_KEY`). **PG16 integration test** proves end-to-end: PgStore round-trip, chain verifies, **raw UPDATE/DELETE are no-ops and the chain still verifies**, anchoring round-trips. Full `go build ./...` + all admin unit tests green; gofmt clean.
- [x] ✅ **P3 (backend) — cross-tenant read foundation.** The read-only aggregation the dashboard's System Overview + Organisations modules consume (sequenced before the P2 frontend since the FE depends on it). Reuses the plan §11.1 model: the admin-api holds an OPTIONAL read-only pool to the customer/orchestrator DB (a read replica in prod).
  - **Config**: `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_DATABASE_DSN` (optional; unset ⇒ cross-tenant modules return 503, the SAD still runs on identity+audit).
  - **`internal/admin/platform.Reader`**: `ListOrgs` (newest-first, name/slug filter, per-org agent + member counts, tier, suspended-from-`tenants.deleted_at`), `GetOrg`, `Overview` (orgs/active/agents/users + tier breakdown). Nil-safe (`Configured()`), parameterised queries only.
  - **Endpoints** (gated + audited): `GET /v1/system/overview` (`services:read`), `GET /v1/orgs` (`orgs:read`), `GET /v1/orgs/{id}` (`orgs:read`) — each sets its audit action, 503 when unconfigured, 404 on missing org. Handler depends on a `PlatformReader` interface → unit-tested with a fake (503/200/404/400). Wired in `main.go` (customer pool built only when the DSN is set).
  - **PG16 integration test** (`reader_integration_test.go`): applies the **full orchestrator migration chain**, seeds an org (scale tier, 2 agents, 1 member) + a soft-deleted/suspended org, and verifies counts, tier breakdown, name-filter, suspended detection, and NotFound — **green**. Full admin unit suite + `go build ./...` green; gofmt clean; `.env.example` updated.
- [x] ✅ **P5 — platform kill switches + dual approval (destructive-ops governance).** The 10 named switches (sysdesign §9) as immutable-via-deploy code, a durable admin-DB state + proposal lifecycle, and a two-phase, self-approval-proof dual-approval machine (sysdesign §11.2).
  - **Migration** `0003_kill_switches.sql`: `kill_switch_state` (`(switch_id, scope_ref)` PK) + `kill_switch_proposals` (status CHECK, 15-min window) with a **partial-unique index = at most one open proposal per switch**. PG16-validated + integration-tested.
  - **Registry** (`internal/admin/killswitch`): the 10 switches with effect/impact/scope + `ValidTarget` (`halt_org` requires a target; platform switches must not). 
  - **Store**: `CreateProposal` (409 on an existing open proposal), `GetProposal`, `Reject`, **`ApproveAndApply`** (single tx: mark approved + upsert state — atomic), `ExpirePending`. 
  - **Service** (dual-approval): `Propose` (validates switch/target, requires a reason code + a typed confirmation phrase = the switch id), **`Approve`** — a **DIFFERENT** operator only (`ErrSelfApproval` → the handler writes a distinct `kill_switch:suspicious_self_approval` audit + alerts), rejects expired/decided; flips state then propagates via an **`Enforcer` seam** (`NoopEnforcer` default; the orchestrator hot-path enforcement is the deploy-time impl). 
  - **Endpoints** (gated `kill_switch:execute` = Owner + Super Admin only, audited): `GET /v1/kill-switches`, `POST /propose` (409/400 mapped), `POST /{id}/approve` (self-approval→403+alert, expired/decided→409), `POST /{id}/reject`. Wired in `main.go`.
  - **Tests**: 6 service/registry unit tests (self-approval rejected, expiry, confirmation/reason/target validation, happy-path flip) via a store interface + **PG16 store integration test** (one-open constraint, atomic approve+apply, double-approve loses, expiry). Full admin build + suites green; gofmt clean.
- [x] ✅ **P4 (partial) — platform feature-flag overrides.** The SAD's platform-wide flag override (distinct from the customer product's per-tenant PostHog evaluation): an operator can force a flag on/off across the whole platform (e.g. emergency disable). Migration `0004_platform_flags.sql` (`platform_flag_overrides`), `internal/admin/flags.Store` (List/Get/Set-upsert/Delete, key allowlist-validated), endpoints `GET /v1/flags` (`flags:read`) + `PUT`/`DELETE /v1/flags/{key}` (`flags:write`) — audited, wired. PG16 integration test (CRUD, upsert-flip, invalid-key, NotFound) green; the authoritative override lives in the admin DB with a deploy-time propagation seam. *(P4's billing/marketplace-moderation write surfaces remain.)*

  - **Typed BFF**: server-only `admin-api.ts` client attaches the WorkOS access token as a bearer to the private `ADMIN_API_URL`, `cache: 'no-store'`, typed responses; `AdminApiError` carries upstream status for UI mapping (503→"not configured").

Dashboard shell with nav + operator identity/role + sign-out.

- [x] ✅ **P4 (org write surfaces) — controlled customer writes.** `platform.Writer` (org **suspend/restore** via `tenants.deleted_at` soft-delete — reversible; **tier change** with tier allowlist). **Opt-in + fail-safe**: writes are disabled unless `ADMIN_CUSTOMER_WRITES_ENABLED=true` AND the pool has grants — otherwise every write returns `ErrWritesDisabled` and the endpoints 503, so a misconfigured deploy can never mutate customer data. Endpoints `POST /v1/orgs/{id}/suspend` + `PUT /v1/orgs/{id}/tier` (gated `orgs:write`, **typed confirmation = the org id required**, audited). Handler unit tests (503-when-disabled, confirm-mismatch→400, valid→200) + the platform PG16 integration test extended (suspend→deleted_at, restore, tier change + invalid-tier-rejected + NotFound + disabled-writer-refuses) — green. *(Remaining P4: marketplace unlist + Stripe refund/subscription writes — same controlled-write pattern.)*
- [x] ✅ **P7 (offboarding) — operator offboarding.** `repository.SuspendUser` (atomic tx: mark `admin_users.status='suspended'` + **DELETE the role assignment** — row retained for audit history, sysdesign §13.3). Endpoint `POST /v1/rbac/users/{workosUserID}/offboard` (**`rbac:write` = Owner only**, audited); the WorkOS account-suspend + session-revoke is a documented deploy-time seam. Repo PG16 integration test extended (offboard → suspended + no role + NotFound) — green. Full build + admin suites green.
- [x] ✅ **P8 (deployable zero-trust infra) — isolated `actrone-admin` Helm chart + Dockerfiles.** The SAD's separate-infrastructure deployment (sysdesign §12).
  - **Helm chart** `infra/helm/actrone-admin` (**`helm lint` clean, `helm template` renders** 3 NetworkPolicies + 2 Services + 2 Deployments + 1 Ingress): `admin-api` (private **ClusterIP**, non-root, read-only rootfs, all caps dropped, liveness/readiness probes, secrets via `envFrom` Secret) + `super-admin` (private, points at the in-cluster admin-api Service); **NetworkPolicy** = default-deny + allow ONLY (VPN CIDR → web) and (web → admin-api) — the admin-api is unreachable from anywhere else; **private ingress** (source-IP allowlisted, `internal-nginx`, never a public LB); Secret seam documented (Vault via External Secrets). Values carry the allowlist CIDR + prod fail-fast note.
  - **Dockerfiles**: `backend/orchestrator/Dockerfile.admin-api` (multi-stage Go → **distroless nonroot**, test stage runs `./internal/admin/...`, private 8090) + `frontend/apps/super-admin/Dockerfile` (Next standalone, test stage = typecheck). Both mirror the shipped extauthz/marketing patterns.
  - *(Remaining P8 = live-cloud infra config: the WireGuard VPN gateway + private Route53 DNS + Cloudflare WAF (L1/L2/L4 network edges) are Terraform against a live AWS account, and the admin-app CI security gates (npm audit/Trivy/Semgrep) are workflow YAML — both are deploy-environment work. The in-cluster + app-layer enforcement (NetworkPolicy, private ingress, IP allowlist, 404-on-fail) is delivered and verified.)*
