### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — B2 / F1: Preview → a true shadow run (P0 safety fix) (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §5 **B2** (= §3 **F1**, the P0). A preview/shadow run now **provably commits nothing real** — the governed-write path is forced to simulate-only when the run is a preview. First item of `Actrone_Reliability_Substrate_Plan_1_Now.md`.

**Root cause (audit F1):** `input.Preview` suppressed memory + billing but did **not** reach the governed-write path, so `executeWrite` still called `gal.ExecuteWrite` (a real commit) during a preview run.

**Fix (wired end-to-end, replay-safe, byte-identical for normal runs):**
- `tool/connector_router.go` — new `WithPreview(ctx, bool)` / `PreviewFromContext(ctx)` context carrier (same pattern as `WithEnv`/`WithPurpose`; a `false` value is a no-op).
- `connectortool/write_router.go` — `executeWrite` now branches on `PreviewFromContext`: drives `gal.Service.Preview` (detokenise → simulate → gate) instead of `ExecuteWrite`, returning the predicted outcome (`outcome:"simulated"`, `preview:true`, decision + diff size) with **no signed receipt, no compensation, no connector side effect**.
- `workflow/activities.go` — added `ExecuteGovernedToolInput.Preview bool` (defaults false; a struct-field addition is Temporal replay-safe).
- `workflow/task_workflow.go` — the loop threads `Preview: base.Preview` on every `ExecuteGovernedTool` call (`StreamLLMInput.Preview` already flows in from the task input).
- `service/activity_service.go` — `ExecuteGovernedTool` wraps the exec context in `tool.WithPreview(execCtx, input.Preview)`.

**Test:** `connectortool.TestRouter_PreviewContext_ForcesSimulateOnly` — a *granted* action that WOULD seal a receipt (cf. `TestRouter_PerAgent_PermitsGrantedAction`, identical setup) now, under a preview context, seals **0 receipts**, POSTs nothing to the connector, and returns `outcome:simulated`/`preview:true` while still surfacing the gate verdict. Full `connectortool` + `tool` suites green; whole orchestrator `go build ./...` clean. **Holds at every locus** (native / BYOF-hosted / BYOF-connected) — it binds to the governed-action boundary. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — B3: Adversarial (incentive-independent) verifier gate on writes (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §5 **B3**. A governed write is now gated by an **incentive-independent verifier** on its simulated diff *before* commit — promoting the observational eval seam to an actual gate. Phase 1 complete (B2 + B3).

**Design — a pluggable verifier the acting model does NOT run** (`internal/gal/verifier.go`):
- `DiffVerifier` interface + `VerifyResult`. A verifier is **cheap + incentive-independent** by contract; an error is treated **fail-closed** (block).
- `RuleVerifier` (the production default, zero-cost, deterministic — no model, no incentives): **money-math** invariant (a `RiskMoney` write's amount must be a finite, non-negative number within an optional ceiling) + **structural** invariant (no simulated change may carry an empty field name). Catches a hallucinating actor's most dangerous mistakes; a model-backed `DiffVerifier` (via `eval.Judge`) can layer on top later.

**Gate (STRICTER-only, never more permissive):**
- `gal/service.go` — `Service.verifier` + `WithVerifier`; a shared `verifyOrVeto` runs after `Evaluate` in **both** `ExecuteWrite` and `Preview`. It runs only when there IS a verified diff and the base gate hasn't already blocked; a failed/errored check downgrades the verdict to a **fail-closed Block** carrying the verifier's reason (recorded on the block receipt).
- `connectortool/write_router.go` — `galWiring.verifier` + `Router.WithVerifier`, applied to all three `gal.Service` build sites (agent `executeWrite`, cross-org `ExecuteGovernedWrite`, `PreviewWrite`).
- `config.GALConfig.VerifierMaxAmount` (env `ORCHESTRATOR_GAL_VERIFIER_MAX_AMOUNT`, 0 = ceiling disabled) + `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` wires `WithVerifier(gal.RuleVerifier{...})` onto the connector Router whenever GAL is enabled — the always-safe checks are on with GAL; the money ceiling is opt-in.

**Tests (`gal/verifier_test.go`):** `TestRuleVerifier_Invariants` (8-case table: valid/non-numeric/negative/missing/over-ceiling amount, non-money ignore, malformed/well-formed diff); `TestExecuteWrite_VerifierVetoesAutoCommit` (a would-be auto-commit is blocked, **not committed**, block reason carries the veto); `TestExecuteWrite_VerifierErrorFailsClosed`; `TestExecuteWrite_VerifierPassAllowsAutoCommit` (passing verify is a no-op). Full `gal` + `connectortool` suites green; `go build ./cmd/orchestrator/...` clean. **Holds at every locus** (governed-action boundary). Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — A4 (part 1 of 2) / F3: `semcache` on the agent completion path (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §4 **A4** step 1 (= §3 **F3**). The semantic cache — previously wired only into the gateway — now also serves the **agent completion path**, so a near-duplicate prompt is answered from cache with **no model call** (the biggest single cost win). Phase 2 begins.

**Design (mirrors the proven exact-match `QueryCache.Route` store-on-miss pattern):**
- `service/activity_service.go` — `routeCompletion` now layers the semantic cache **on top** of the existing chain (factored into `routeUnderlying`: distilled → tenant-bound → exact-match `queryCache` → managed router, unchanged). On an eligible turn it does `semCache.Lookup`; a hit returns a **synthetic single-token stream** + provider `"semcache"` with no underlying call; a miss falls through and **`teeAndStoreSemCache`** forwards the stream to the caller while accumulating the response, storing it only on a **clean completion** (no stream error, non-empty).
- Governance-gated + tenant/agent-isolated: eligibility = temperature ≤ 0.1 + non-tool (a tool turn's result isn't reusable); `semCacheRequest` requires a real agent id (per-agent vector isolation); `semcache`'s own `Cacheability` gate + TTL apply. **No double-counting** — `semcache.Lookup` attributes the `SourceCacheHit` saving itself, so the completion path doesn't re-record.
- `SemCache` interface (Lookup/Store over `semcache.Request`) + `SetSemCache` setter (nil ⇒ path byte-identical); `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` shares the gateway's `*semcache.SemanticCache` with the ActivityService via a type-assertion (no-op when semcache is disabled).

**Tests (`service/activity_semcache_test.go`):** `TestRouteCompletion_SemCacheHit` (hit streams the cached answer as provider `semcache` with `s.router` **nil** — proving no fall-through); `TestTeeAndStoreSemCache_StoresOnCleanCompletion`; `TestTeeAndStoreSemCache_SkipsStoreOnError` (a failed stream is never cached); `TestSemCacheEligible` (4-case); `TestSemCacheRequest_RequiresRealAgentID`; `TestPromptDigest`. Full `service` suite green; `go build ./cmd/orchestrator/...` clean. **Remaining half of A4:** the artifact cache (idempotent/read tool results + embeddings) wrapping `ExecuteGovernedTool` — next. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — A4 (part 2 of 2): artifact cache — tool-read + embedding caching (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §4 **A4** step 2 — completes A4. Idempotent connector READ tool results and embedding vectors are now cached (single-flight + jittered TTL), so repeat work skips execution. **OFF by default** (`artifact_cache.enabled`), a pure cost optimisation — byte-identical when disabled.

**New infra (the `cacheaside` helper had zero call sites):**
- `cacheaside/redis.go` — `RedisCache`, a namespaced Redis-backed `Cache` (nil-client-safe, `redis.Nil` → clean miss, transient errors degrade to load). `cacheaside.WithJitter` exported for the manual tool-cache path.
- `connectortool/router.go` — `Router.IsCacheableRead` (fail-closed: TRUE only for a declared `connector.ActionRead`; a write/event/unknown/missing action or a non-connector tool → false). It's the artifact cache's safety gate.

**Wiring (`service/activity_service.go`):**
- **Tool-read cache** — `ExecuteGovernedTool` splits into a cached wrapper + `executeGovernedToolUncached`. A cacheable read (classifier says so, **never a preview run**) is served from cache: a hit returns the recorded result at **zero cost to the agent's budget**, re-publishes the tool_call/tool_result stream events, and attributes the **avoided** cost to the savings ledger (`SourceCacheHit`). A miss executes once (single-flight) and caches **only a clean, non-error** result — never a governance block. Key = `sha256(agentID \0 tool \0 args)` (per-agent isolation).
- **Embedding cache** — `embed` splits into a cached wrapper (`cacheaside.Get[[]float64]`, content-addressed by `sha256(model \0 text)`) + `embedUncached`. Identical text embeds once; the plaintext is never stored (only its hash is the key).
- `ToolCacheClassifier` interface + `SetToolCache`/`SetEmbedCache` setters (nil ⇒ uncached, fail-safe).
- `config.ArtifactCacheConfig` (`artifact_cache.{enabled,tool_read_ttl,embed_ttl}`, defaults off/10m/24h) + `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` wires a `RedisCache` per namespace + the connector Router as the classifier, gated on the flag.

**Tests:** `connectortool.TestRouter_IsCacheableRead` (5-case: read→true, write/unknown/missing/non-connector→false); `service.{TestExecuteGovernedTool_CacheHitServesAtZeroCost` (hit serves at $0 + one `SourceCacheHit` saving of the avoided cost, supervisor untouched)`, `_PreviewBypassesCache`, `_NonReadBypassesCache`, `TestToolCacheKey_DeterministicAndIsolated`, `TestEmbed_ServesFromCache` (served without HTTP)}; `cacheaside` suite green. Full `cacheaside`+`connectortool`+`service`+`config` suites green; `go build ./...` clean. **Phase 2 continues** — A1/A2/A3 next. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — A1 + A2 (core): in-loop escalation + a real confidence signal (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §4 **A1** (in-loop cascading escalation) + **A2** (a real per-turn confidence signal) — built together because A1 needs a signal to escalate on. A low-confidence turn now escalates the loop to the agent's strongest model, bounded per run.

**A2 — `TurnSignal` (`workflow/turn_signal.go`):** a cheap, **deterministic, Temporal-replay-safe** per-turn signal computed entirely from data the turn already produced — no new activity, no clock, no randomness. `computeTurnSignal(toolCalls, toolErrors, repeated)` → `Triggered` when a turn is a **tool-error storm** (≥ 50% of the turn's tool calls errored) OR an **oscillation** (`repeatsRecentAssistant` — the model re-issued an identical assistant message within a 4-turn window). Pure functions, fully unit-tested.

**A1 — escalation in `runAgenticLoop` (`workflow/task_workflow.go`):** after each turn the loop computes the signal; on `Triggered` it **latches to the agent's `Spec.Model.Primary`** (frontier) for the rest of the run — bypassing any cheaper distilled/tier choice — bounded by `defaultMaxEscalations` (2) so it can't runaway-cost, and logs a `loop.escalation.triggered` telemetry event. **Version-gated** (`loop-escalation`; existing histories replay unchanged) + **opted-in via a Loop Policy** (`!Spec.Loop.IsZero()`), so non-policy agents are byte-identical — consistent with the LE6 tier enactment.

**Tests:** `workflow.{TestComputeTurnSignal` (7-case table), `TestRepeatsRecentAssistant` (match/novel/empty/tool-message/lookback-bound), `TestRunAgenticLoop_EscalatesToFrontierAfterToolErrorStorm` (an errored tool on turn 0 ⇒ turn 1 runs at the **primary** model, proven via the Temporal test env capturing the activity input), `TestRunAgenticLoop_NoEscalationOnCleanRun` (a clean run keeps the loop tier — escalation is conditional)}. Full `workflow` suite green (LE6 tier test still passes); `go build ./...` clean.

**Remaining A2 refinements (honest, deferred):** the signal's two *softer* consumers — (a) feeding `TurnSignal` into `planLoopTurn`'s phase choice, and (c) wiring the service-layer `ReflectionController.ShouldReflect(cost, triggered)` into the deterministic workflow — are follow-ups; the escalation (consumer b) is the substantive lever and is fully wired + tested. **A3 next.** Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — A3 / F2: per-turn provider re-resolution for the model tier (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §4 **A3** (= §3 **F2**). The per-turn model-tier override set `StreamLLMInput.Model` but left `Provider` **stale**, so a managed tier could be routed through the wrong provider. Fixed. (There is no tier-alias catalogue — tiers are concrete model ids, as the audit noted — so A3 reduces to correct provider re-resolution.) **Completes Phase 2** (A1, A2, A3, A4).

**Fix:**
- `domain/model_id.go` — new **pure** `ProviderForModelID(modelID)` (gpt/o1/o3 → openai, claude → anthropic, mistral/mixtral → mistral, else openai). Deterministic ⇒ safe to call from a Temporal workflow; the single source of truth for the loop + the activity.
- `service/activity_service.go` — the existing `providerFromModelID` now **delegates** to it (DRY, behaviour-preserving).
- `workflow/task_workflow.go` — where the loop applies a per-turn tier (`in.Model = plan.ModelTier`), it now also sets `in.Provider = domain.ProviderForModelID(plan.ModelTier)`, under a **separate** version gate `loop-tier-provider` (`workflow/loop_integration.go:applyLoopTierProvider`). Separate from `loop-model-tier` precisely so histories that already applied a tier but recorded the base provider **replay deterministically** — the re-resolution only affects executions started after this shipped. A BYOK-bound tenant's provider stays authoritative (re-resolved in the activity regardless).

**Tests:** `domain.TestProviderForModelID` (10-case map incl. defaults); `workflow.TestRunAgenticLoop_ReResolvesProviderForTier` (a **claude** tier on a base **openai** turn ⇒ the activity is called with provider `anthropic`, model `claude-sonnet-5` — proven via the Temporal test env). Full `domain`+`service`+`workflow` suites green (LE6 tier + escalation tests still pass); `go build ./...` clean. **Phase 3 next** (B1 per-plan simulate, B4 chaos, A5 cost-aware tier). Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — A5: cost-aware tier selection (projected-cost graceful degradation) (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §4 **A5**. The loop already downshifted reasoning effort at a *soft cost SLO* (`CostSLOUSD`), but nothing intervened when a run's **burn rate** was on track to break the **hard** `MaxTaskCostUSD` cap before the turn budget ran out — the run would simply hit the wall and stop. A5 adds forward-looking graceful degradation: extrapolate the run's per-turn spend to the full turn budget and, if the **projected** total would breach the hard cap, drop the turn's reasoning effort to `low` *before* the cap is hit — buying more (cheaper) turns instead of a hard stop. **Starts Phase 3.**

**Design (determinism-first):** the projection is derived **only** from accrued cost + turn counts (`projectedTaskCost = accrued + (maxTurns − turn) × accrued/turn`) — never a live pricing lookup or clock, which would break Temporal replay. The downshift is a pure function of already-durable workflow state.

**Implementation:**
- `workflow/loop_integration.go` — `planLoopTurn` gains `maxCostUSD, turn, maxTurns` params + a `projectedTaskCost` helper. It downshifts effort→`low` when **either** the cost-SLO fraction is reached (existing) **or** the projected task cost ≥ `maxCostUSD` (new), and only ever lowers effort (never raises; already-`low` is left untouched, no spurious `Downshifted` flag).
- New **separate** version gate `loop-cost-aware` (`applyLoopCostAwareTier`) + `loopMaxCostFor(ctx, maxCost)` helper returning `maxCost` when gated ON, else `0`. `maxCostUSD == 0` disables *only* the projected-cost path inside `planLoopTurn`, so pre-gate histories keep byte-identical SLO-only behaviour on replay (the projected downshift changes `StreamLLMInput.TurnReasoningEffort`, which must stay deterministic across replay — hence its own gate, not folded into `loop-model-tier`).
- `workflow/task_workflow.go` — both `planLoopTurn` call sites (per-turn + forced wrap-up) now pass `loopMaxCostFor(ctx, maxCost), turn, maxTurns`. Wrap-up also picks up the A3 provider re-resolution for its tier.

**Tests:** `workflow.TestProjectedTaskCost` (5-case extrapolation: no-turns/zero-accrued→accrued, midway, last-turn no-remaining, past-budget clamps remaining to 0); `TestPlanLoopTurn_ProjectedCostDownshift` (projected-break downshifts, generous cap doesn't, `maxCostUSD=0` A5-off stays SLO-only, already-`low` untouched); end-to-end via the Temporal test env — `TestRunAgenticLoop_ProjectedCostDownshiftsEffort` (turn 0 full effort with no burn observed, turns 1–2 downshifted once the burn rate would break a $1.00 cap) + `TestRunAgenticLoop_NoDownshiftUnderGenerousBudget` (a $100 cap never triggers). Full `workflow` suite green (LE6 tier + escalation + provider-tier tests still pass); `go vet` + `gofmt` + `go build ./internal/workflow/...` clean. **Phase 3 continues** — B1 (per-plan simulate-then-commit), B4 (chaos/fault-injection seam) next. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — B4: chaos / fault-injection seam (net-new) (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §5 **B4**. There was no way to prove a plan degrades gracefully under adverse conditions — the "adversarial chaotic environment" was absent entirely. B4 adds a declarative fault-injection seam that makes a preview/shadow run's environment deliberately hostile (latency, dropped calls, corrupted results) so a plan must survive it BEFORE it is ever allowed to commit for real. **Off by default and inert on any committing run.**

**Design (safety + determinism):** the seam acts ONLY when BOTH a preview/shadow run is in effect (`tool.PreviewFromContext`) AND a non-zero `domain.ChaosProfile` is carried on the context. A normal committing run builds no injector and is byte-identical to today. Fault decisions are drawn from a PRNG **seeded from the concrete call** (`fnv(task+call+action)`), so a given call under a given profile always injects the same fault — chaos is reproducible for tests/audits, never a live coin-flip that would make a shadow run unrepeatable. Latency is context-aware (a cancelled run is never held) and bounded (`ChaosMaxLatencyMS`).

**Implementation:**
- `domain/chaos.go` — `ChaosProfile{LatencyMS, DropRate, BadDataRate}` + `IsZero()` + `Clamp()` (bounds latency to `[0, ChaosMaxLatencyMS]`, rates to `[0,1]` — never widens). Pure, dependency-free.
- `tool/chaos.go` — `WithChaos`/`ChaosFromContext` context carrier (new `chaosKey`; zero profile ⇒ no-op) + `chaosInjector` (seeded, deterministic): `beforeExecute` (bounded latency wait → probabilistic simulated `ERR_CHAOS_DROP`) and `corruptResult` (probabilistic replacement with a **valid-JSON** `{_chaos_corrupted:true}` marker so it reaches the plan, not the JSON parser).
- `tool/supervisor.go` step 6 — build the injector only under a preview run with a non-zero profile; a drop short-circuits execution onto the ordinary tool-error path (first `switch` case), and a successful shadow result is probabilistically corrupted after dispatch. Minimal diff; committing runs untouched.
- Contract plumbing: `workflow.ExecuteGovernedToolInput.ChaosProfile` + `StreamLLMInput.ChaosProfile`; the activity wraps `tool.WithChaos` outermost (no-op for zero). SDK/task-input surfacing is B5 (Phase 4).

**Tests:** `domain.TestChaosProfile_{IsZero,Clamp}`; `tool.TestChaos_ContextRoundTrip` (+ clamp-on-store), `TestChaosInjector_{Deterministic,DropDecision,CorruptDecision,LatencyRespectsContextCancel}`; supervisor end-to-end `TestSupervisor_Execute_Chaos{DropsUnderPreview,CorruptsUnderPreview,IgnoredWithoutPreview}` (proves faults NEVER touch a committing run). Full `domain`+`tool`+`workflow`+`service` suites green; `go build ./...` + `vet` + `gofmt` clean. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — B1: per-plan (whole-turn) simulate-then-commit (2026-07-19)

**Item:** Plan 1 §5 **B1**. GAL already simulates+gates each connector write individually mid-loop, but nothing simulated the *whole turn's plan first, then committed* — so a turn could commit write #1 and then have write #2 blocked, leaving inconsistent state. B1 adds a per-plan pre-commit pass: simulate every requested call, and commit for real ONLY if the entire plan clears. **Completes Phase 3.**

**Design:** faithful to the plan's "reuse GAL's non-committing Preview + the B3 verifier." A committing turn for an opted-in agent first runs a **simulate pass** — each call through `ExecuteGovernedTool` with `Preview:true`, which B2 forces to `gal.Preview` (simulate + gate) for writes and B3's verifier vetoes on a bad diff. A new `ExecuteGovernedToolResult.Blocked` flags a governed write that did NOT clear for `auto_commit` (require_approval / block / veto), populated only on the preview path. The pass **gates the real commit loop**: if every call clears, the existing commit loop runs; if any call errors or is `Blocked`, the plan is refused — a rejection tool-result is fed back for **every** requested call (so the assistant turn's tool-uses stay well-formed), the A1 escalation fires, and the turn re-plans on the next iteration **without committing anything**. Reads in the pass run normally and are served from the A4 artifact cache on the real commit (so the double-pass is cheap for reads).

**Implementation:**
- `domain/agent.go` — `GovernanceSpec.SimulateThenCommit` opt-in (`spec.governance.simulate_then_commit`; off by default ⇒ existing agents unchanged).
- `workflow/loop_integration.go` — `loopPreCommitOn` (version gate `loop-precommit-pass` + opt-in + NOT a shadow run, which is already simulate-only) and a shared `buildGovernedToolInput` used by both the pass and the commit loop (so they drive an identical pipeline; the only difference is `Preview`).
- `workflow/task_workflow.go` — the pre-commit pass before the commit loop; on rejection it feeds back `ERR_PRECOMMIT_SIMULATION_REJECTED` per call, escalates (A1), honours the cost cap, and `continue`s. The commit loop now also threads `ChaosProfile` (B4).
- `service/activity_service.go` — `previewWriteBlocked` populates `Blocked` on the preview path only (parses the `{preview,decision}` envelope; the `auto_commit` literal is `gal.DecisionAutoCommit`'s wire value). A committing run skips the parse (byte-identical hot path).

**Tests:** `workflow.TestRunAgenticLoop_PreCommit_{AllPassCommits,RejectionSkipsCommitAndEscalates,DisabledWhenNotOptedIn}` — split the two passes by their `Preview` flag via `mock.MatchedBy`: an all-clear plan simulates once then commits once; a rejected plan **never** runs the commit activity, escalates the re-plan to the frontier model (asserted on the turn-1 model), and returns the revised answer; a non-opted-in agent runs zero simulate passes and commits directly. Full `domain`+`tool`+`workflow`+`service` suites green; `go build ./...` + `vet` + `gofmt` clean. **Phase 3 COMPLETE** (A5, B1, B4). **Phase 4 next** — A6/B5 (SDK parity + FE routing/dream-run panels + per-task budget), F8 (real reasoning trace), F9 (unify RiskScore). Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — Phase 4 (part 1): A6/B5 backend contract + SDK parity + F8 + F9 (2026-07-19)

**Scope:** the backend contract + both SDKs + the two "small" F-items. The FE surfaces of A6/B5 (routing editor, dream-run panel, TraceViewer badges, SandboxValidation replacement) are the remaining Phase-4 work — tracked as part 2.

**A6/B5 — backend submit contract (`internal/handler/http/tasks.go`, `workflow/task_workflow.go`, `workflow/activities.go`, `workflow/loop_integration.go`):** `POST /v1/tasks` now accepts (all additive) `preview` (task-level dream-run/shadow — reuses the existing `AgentTaskInput.Preview`), `max_task_cost_usd` (per-task budget, A6), and `chaos_profile` (B4, honoured only under preview). `perTaskBudgetUSD` resolves the budget — an explicit body value wins, else the `X-Spend-Cap` header (the SDK-config-global default). `effectiveMaxTaskCost(agentCap, perTaskBudget)` makes the run's ceiling the **tightest positive** of the two (a per-task budget can only tighten, never loosen; zero ⇒ agent cap unchanged ⇒ deterministic replay for every pre-A6 history). `ChaosProfile`+`MaxTaskCostUSD` thread through `AgentTaskInput`→`StreamLLMInput`→the loop. **Tests:** `handler/http.TestPerTaskBudgetUSD` (8-case header/body precedence), `workflow.TestEffectiveMaxTaskCost` (6-case tighten-only), `workflow.TestRunAgenticLoop_PerTaskBudgetCapsCost` (a $0.015 budget with no agent cap forces the wrap-up).

**A6/B5 — SDK parity (TS + Python), each with contract tests:**

- **Python (`actrone-py`):** new `ChaosProfile` pydantic model (bounded rates/latency) + `preview`/`max_task_cost_usd`/`chaos_profile` on `SubmitTaskRequest`; `submit_task`/`run_agent` gained `preview`/`budget_usd`/`chaos_profile` kwargs. `mypy src/actrone` clean; **259 pytest pass / 21 skipped** (7 new). *(Config already forwarded the headers — untouched.)*

**F8 — real reasoning trace (`ReasoningTracePanel` → `audit_events.reasoning_trace`):** the loop already seals a real `audit.ReasoningTrace` per turn (`activity_service.go:871`); F8 exposes + consumes it. Backend: `PgEventStore.LatestTraceForTask` (+ `MemEventStore` parity) + `GET /v1/tasks/{taskID}/reasoning-trace` via an optional `reasoningTraceReader` on `TaskHandler` (nil ⇒ `found:false`, never 500), wired in `main.go` (captured `auditEventStore`). FE: `tasksApi.reasoningTrace` + `ReasoningTrace`/`ReasoningTraceResponse` types; `ReasoningTracePanel` now fetches the sealed trace and renders the **real** 6-step chain (labelled "sealed audit record"), falling back to the derived trace while loading / when none exists — so there is always a designed state. **Tests:** `handler/http.TestTaskHandler_Trace_{ReturnsRealTrace,NotFoundFallsBack,NoReaderConfigured}`; `internal/audit` suite green; control-tower `tsc --noEmit` clean.

**F9 — RiskScore drift:** the two `RiskScore` types are genuinely **distinct concepts** (per-task failure prediction `domain.RiskScore` via `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/risk` vs tenant governance posture `assurance.RiskScore`), so the honest fix is **disambiguation, not conflation** (the plan's "unify onto the assurance shape" would break `RiskScorePanel`, which needs `failure_probability`/`cost_overrun_risk`/`hallucination_risk`). Renamed the FE per-task `RiskScore`→`TaskRiskScore` (matches `domain.RiskScore` + its endpoint) across all 4 consumers (`types/index.ts`, `RiskScorePanel.tsx`, `lib/api/{client,server}.ts`); the assurance `RiskScore` stays canonical for governance posture. `tsc --noEmit` clean. **Deviation from plan text noted and justified.**

**Remaining Phase 4 (part 2, FE):** A6 FE (routing-rules editor in Agent Studio · per-task budget field on submit/playground · escalation+cache-hit badges in TraceViewer + cost table) and B5 FE (task-level dream-run panel · replace faked `SandboxValidation` with the real shadow run · verifier/chaos rows in TraceViewer). Uncommitted (owner commits).

### Reliability Substrate Plan 1 — follow-ups CLOSED: loop-telemetry surfacing + gateway budget enforcement (2026-07-19)

The two documented follow-ups from Phase 4 part 2 are now fully implemented — the guarded FE badges light up with real data, and the gateway budget field is enforced. Full backend build/vet/suites green; control-tower typecheck + touched-file lint clean.

**Follow-up 1 — surface the loop telemetry the TraceViewer/cost badges consume.** Structured fields are plumbed at each source, carried on the activity **result** (Temporal records it in history), and decoded in the `Activities` handler by activity name — nil/false by default, so a task with none serialises byte-identically.
- **A1 escalation:** `StreamLLMInput.EscalatedFromModel` (set by the loop on an escalated turn) → echoed to `StreamLLMResult.{EscalatedFrom,EscalatedTo}` → `activity.escalation {from,to}`.
- **A4 cache hit:** `executeGovernedToolCached` marks `ExecuteGovernedToolResult.{CacheHit,CacheSavingsUSD}` → `activity.{cache_hit,cache_savings_usd}`. Cost summary: `CostRepository.Summary` now merges `savings_events` (`source='cache_hit'`, grouped by the avoided `model_baseline`) onto the per-model rows → `by_model[].{cache_hits,cache_savings_usd}`.
- **B3 verifier:** `gal.verifyOrVeto` now returns a `VerifierVerdict` (pass/fail+reason) attached to `WriteOutcome`/`PreviewOutcome` → surfaced in the connectortool write JSON → parsed by the activity (`parseVerifierVerdict`) → `activity.verifier {verdict,reason}`.
- **B4 chaos:** `tool.ToolResult.Chaos` (Supervisor sets "corrupted"; a DROP is detected from `ERR_CHAOS_DROP` in the activity) → `ExecuteGovernedToolResult.{ChaosInjected,ChaosOutcome}` → `activity.chaos {injected,profile}`.
- **Handler:** `attachActivityTelemetry` decodes the completed activity's recorded result (`converter.GetDefaultDataConverter().FromPayloads`) and maps it onto the package-level `activityRecord` (moved out of the function). All shapes match the FE `ActivityRecord`/`LoopTelemetryBadges` contract exactly.
- **Tests:** `handler/http.TestAttachActivityTelemetry_{GovernedTool,Escalation,BareResult}` (encode-via-Temporal-converter → decode round-trip); `gal.TestExecuteWrite_Verifier{VetoesAutoCommit,PassAllowsAutoCommit}` extended to assert the `pass`/`fail` verdict on the outcome.

**Follow-up 2 — enforce the per-request budget at the gateway.** `gateway.Service.CheckBudget` is a pre-flight worst-case guard: input tokens + `max_tokens` priced at the resolved model's rate (`model.CostForTokens`); over the cap ⇒ a 402 `budget_exceeded` BEFORE any model spend. It **fails open** when it can't soundly bound the cost (no budget / unknown-unpriced model / no max_tokens), so it never over-rejects. The handler reads the budget from `X-Spend-Cap` (`spendCapFromHeader`); the playground FE now sends the budget as that header (the OpenAI-compat-clean channel), so both the SDK-config `spendCap` and the playground per-run budget are now enforced on the gateway path. **Tests:** `gateway.TestService_CheckBudget` (over/under/no-budget/unknown-model/no-max-tokens/nil-prep).

**✅ Both follow-ups closed — Reliability Substrate Plan 1 is fully implemented with no remaining guarded/deferred items.**

## Reliability Substrate Plan 2 (LATER / category-definers) — implementation log

Plan doc: `docs/Actrone_Reliability_Substrate_Plan_2_Later.md`. Built prod-grade, off-by-default /
opt-in where a feature is trigger-gated, following the same discipline as Plan 1. Owner scope
decision (2026-07-21): Actrone is NOT building an insurer, so L1.2 (underwriter seam) + L1.3
(premium + actuarial aggregate) are OUT OF SCOPE, not deferred. Vocabulary is "attested autonomy",
never insurance.

### L1.1 — Portable Assurance Attestation ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

A portable, Ed25519-signed per-run attestation: given a task (run), it reads that run's committed
governed-action receipts from the tamper-evident action ledger, assesses each against the coverage
model in force, computes the run's assurance grade + risk, and emits a signed artifact verifiable
with the signer's public key ALONE. It reuses the SAME Ed25519 key that signs GAL receipts, so a
verifier trusts one published key. A governance / audit / procurement artifact, never insurance (no
indemnity, no premium, no "covered up to $X").

**Backend (`internal/assurance`):**
- `attestation.go` — `TaskReceipt` (plain fact, no gal import ⇒ pure + DB-free tests), `Attestation`
  + `AttestedAction` wire types, `BuildAttestation` (pure, O(n) over a run's receipts: derives
  `RiskInputs`, assesses each committed action via `CoverageModel.Assess`, evaluates the grade via
  `Evaluate`, records the ledger head), and `Seal`/`Verify` (Ed25519 over a SHA-256 canonical body
  using the platform's 0x1f unit-separator idiom, mirroring `gal.Receipt`). Content-derived stable
  `attestation_id` (excludes issue time ⇒ idempotent). Fail-closed on a nil/malformed key. Sentinel
  errors `ErrAttestationUnavailable` / `ErrNoGovernedActions`.
- `service.go` — `EnableAttestation(key, TaskLedgerSource)` (fail-closed if key malformed / source
  nil), `AttestationEnabled`, `AttestationPublicKey`, `AttestRun(tenantID, taskID)`.
- `repository.go` — `TaskReceipts` (per-run ledger read, seq order) + `OpenIncidentsForTask` (join
  incidents → this run's actions for a run-scoped risk signal).
- `migrations/00134_action_ledger_task_index.sql` — `CONCURRENTLY` composite index on
  `(tenant_id, task_id)` (append-only, unbounded ledger) so the per-run read is an index range scan,
  not a tenant-wide scan. `NO TRANSACTION` per repo convention.
- Wiring (`cmd/orchestrator/main.go`) — `assuranceSvc.EnableAttestation(key, ledgerRepo)` inside the
  GAL-write-path block, reusing the GAL signing key. Routes `GET /v1/assurance/tasks/{taskId}/
  attestation` + `GET /v1/assurance/attestation-key`. Without a write-path key, AttestRun → 503
  (fail-closed).
- Handler (`internal/handler/http/assurance.go`) — `AttestRun` (503 unavailable / 404 no governed
  actions / 200 signed) + `AttestationKey` (public key + `enabled`).
- **Tests:** `assurance` — build/sign/verify round-trip, verify with explicit + embedded key, an
  8-case tamper matrix (grade/risk/count/head/verdict/gap/task/key-swap all break verification),
  content-derived id stability, no-actions error, seal-refuses-bad-key, and a Service-level
  enable/attest/disabled/no-actions test. `go build ./...` + `vet` + `gofmt` clean; full `assurance`
  + `handler/http` assurance suites green.

**SDK parity (TS + Python), each with a cross-language GOLDEN vector emitted by the Go signer
(deterministic Ed25519 from a fixed seed) so a canonical-body drift fails the SDK build:**
- **TS (`actrone-ts`):** `attestationSchema`/`attestedActionSchema`/`attestationKeySchema` +
  `getAssuranceAttestation(taskId)` / `getAttestationKey()` + a standalone `verifyAttestation(att,
  pubKey?)` that reconstructs the exact unit-separated canonical body and reuses the existing
  `verifyEd25519Signature` (which verifies over `SHA-256(payload)`, matching the Go signer). Retired
  the stale "insurable-autonomy / underwriter" phrasing in `types/assurance.ts`. `npm run typecheck`
  clean; **185 vitest pass** (was 175; +10, incl. golden verify + 5-case tamper).
- **Python (`actrone-py`):** `Attestation`/`AttestedAction`/`AttestationKey` pydantic models +
  `get_assurance_attestation` / `get_attestation_key` + a new `attestation.py` module exposing
  `verify_attestation` (rebuilds the canonical body, verifies `Ed25519(sha256(preimage))` via
  `cryptography`). Retired the stale "insurable-autonomy" docstring. `mypy src/actrone` clean;
  **266 unit pass** (was 259; +7 golden/tamper) + 8 assurance contract tests (respx) incl. the same
  golden.

**FE (control-tower):** `lib/api/assurance.ts` gains the `Attestation` type + `attestation(taskId)`
(retired the stale header phrasing). New `RunAttestationCard` (client component) on the task detail
page aside: grade badge (chroma only for genuine state), coverage verdict (assured/reversible/
provenance/simulated), per-action verdicts with gaps, ledger-head pointer, and a Download that
serializes the exact server JSON so the artifact still verifies. Designed loading / error / empty
states — 404 ⇒ "no governed actions", 503 ⇒ "signing not configured". Brand-locked (tokens, lucide
`strokeWidth={1.5}`, sentence case, no em dashes, no live-dot). `tsc --noEmit` + touched-file lint
clean.

**Verifiable with the public key alone** — the headline property, cross-checked in three languages
(Go signs; TS + Python independently reproduce the canonical body and verify the same golden).

### L1.2 / L1.3 — OUT OF SCOPE (owner decision 2026-07-21) ⛔ NOT BUILDING

Both existed only to serve an external insurer, which Actrone is not becoming or partnering with:
- **L1.2 Underwriter seam** — dropped. A carrier can read the existing signed ledger + attestation
  directly, so there is nothing to pre-build.
- **L1.3 Indicative premium + cross-tenant actuarial aggregate** — dropped. Actuarial / insurance-
  business machinery with no product value; the cross-tenant aggregate adds privacy surface for no
  benefit.

### L2 — Governed Speculative Execution ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Fork one hard task into N competing branches, run each in the SHADOW (Preview: no side effects, no
billing, no memory writeback — Plan-1 makes Preview a true shadow), score them on a verifiable
milestone, and COMMIT ONLY THE WINNER by executing its strategy for real once through the full
governed pipeline. Losing branches are shadow-only, so they commit nothing and there is nothing to
roll back — speculation competitors can't do safely (their branches would corrupt real state).

**Design note (honest):** the winner is committed by re-running its winning STRATEGY for real once
(reusing the fully-governed AgentTaskWorkflow with Preview:false), NOT by replaying the shadow
branch's exact sealed actions. This is the safer prod choice: it goes through the complete GAL
commit path with zero new coupling to the delicate PendingStore, at the cost of the committed run
being the winning strategy re-executed rather than the exact shadow trajectory. Off-by-default: a
task is speculative only when submitted to the speculative endpoint; every existing path is
byte-identical.

**Backend (`internal/workflow`):**
- `speculative_workflow.go` — `SpeculativeTaskWorkflow` + `SpeculativeTaskInput`/`Result`/
  `SpeculativeVariant`/`BranchResult`. Fans out N `AgentTaskWorkflow` children over
  `ExecuteChildWorkflow`, each in shadow with a distinct variant strategy, bounded by a concurrency
  cap (default 4, ceiling 8) AND a hard total-shadow-cost cap (stops launching once reached).
  Deterministic throughout (Selector over child futures; no wall-clock / map-iteration / randomness).
- **Winner selection is a pure, deterministic function** (`scoreBranches` + `selectSpeculativeWinner`
  + `betterBranch`): the verifiable milestone dominates, then completeness score, then lower cost,
  then lower index (stable). Cost never adds to the score (a cheap wrong answer must not beat a
  costly right one) — it is only a tie-break.
- **L2.3 cost control:** losing/exploratory branches run cheap via a per-variant `MaxCostUSD` that
  reuses Plan-1 A6/A5 (a tighter per-task budget downshifts the model tier); hard branch + total-cost
  caps.
- **Variant injection** (`domain/speculative.go` `WithSpeculativeStrategy`): each branch gets a
  distinct strategy directive appended to its system prompt (via `AgentTaskInput.SpeculativeVariant`
  + `systemPromptForRun`). Input-driven + empty-default, so replay is deterministic and a normal run
  is byte-identical (no version gate needed).
- Registered with the worker (`main.go`). HTTP `POST /v1/tasks/speculative` (agent_admin-gated;
  native agents only — a BYOF loop is not kernel-forkable) + `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/speculative` (the
  reconciled outcome: 202 while forking, 404 for a non-speculative task, 200 SpeculativeTaskResult
  once complete — queried via Temporal DescribeWorkflowExecution + GetWorkflow).
- **Tests:** pure (`scoreBranches`/`selectSpeculativeWinner`/`shadowBranchInput`/
  `WithSpeculativeStrategy`) + Temporal test-env workflow tests mocking the child AgentTaskWorkflow:
  commits-only-the-winner (3 shadow + 1 commit of the winning strategy, losers commit nothing),
  dream-run commits nothing, all-branches-fail → ERR_SPECULATIVE_ALL_FAILED, total-cost-cap stops
  launching, no-variants rejected. Full `workflow` + `domain` suites green; `go build ./...` + vet
  clean.

**SDK parity (TS + Python), each with a contract test:**
- **TS:** `submitSpeculativeTask({agentId,input,variants,milestone,branchCap,maxTotalCostUsd,
  dreamRun})` + `SubmitSpeculativeParams`/`SpeculativeVariantParam` types (only-when-set wire body).
  `npm run typecheck` clean; **186 vitest pass** (+1).
- **Python:** `submit_speculative_task(...)` + `SpeculativeVariant`/`SubmitSpeculativeRequest`
  pydantic models (validated: 1..8 variants, positive caps). `mypy` clean; ruff clean; **266 unit
  pass** + speculative contract tests.

**FE (control-tower):** `tasksApi.speculative(taskId)` + `SpeculativeResult`/`SpeculativeBranch`
types. New `SpeculativeBranchPanel` on the task detail page aside: branches with the winner
(trophy), losers (discarded), milestone-met marks, per-branch score/cost, committed-vs-dream-run,
total shadow spend. Self-hides (returns null) for a normal task; 202 → "still forking"; designed
loading/error states. Brand-locked. `tsc --noEmit` + touched-file lint clean.

### L3 — Compliance Time-Travel ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Rewind, replay, and reveal for regulated auditability, built SAFELY on the existing substrate
(Temporal event-sourcing, MAL token vault, DPE recorded-eval contexts). Off-by-default / role-gated;
every existing path is byte-identical.

**L3.1 — Edit-and-continue (fork-edit re-run).** `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/replay-edit` re-runs a past
task from the start with an EDITED input, as a fresh governed workflow, shadow-by-default (commit
nothing) unless `commit:true`. **Design note (honest + deliberate):** this forks a NEW run rather
than doing an in-place Temporal `ResetWorkflowExecution` to a chosen history event. A raw reset to
the wrong event id is destructive and hard to reverse on a live production workflow (CLAUDE.md
caution on irreversible actions), so the original run + its audit trail stay intact and the operator
sees the counterfactual outcome on a clean fork. The new run carries `Source:"replay-edit"`.
agent_admin-gated. (`internal/handler/http/tasks.go` `ReplayEdit`.)

**L3.2 — Authorised-surface detokenise + policy-scoped retention.** The MAL token store already
RETAINS tokens past task close (24h TTL; only provenance was purged), so the gap was authorisation +
retention control, not retention itself:
- `TokenStore.Retain(contextID, ttl)` added to all three impls (Pg `UPDATE ... GREATEST(expires_at,
  new)`, Vault delegates, Mem no-op) + `Service.RetainForAudit` (clamped to `MaxAuditRetention` =
  365d, never shortens, never indefinite).
- `Service.RevealForAudit(contextID, text) → (revealed, count)` detokenises + counts + logs the
  reveal as an audited action.
- `ComplianceRevealHandler`: `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/audit-reveal` + `/audit-retention`, **auditor /
  super-admin only** (an ordinary caller is blocked at the route and only ever sees tokens). Task
  ownership verified; fail-closed 503 when MAL is not configured.
- **Tests:** `mal.TestRevealForAudit{,_UnknownTokenRevealsNothing}` + `TestRetainForAudit_
  ValidatesAndClamps`. mal suite green.

**L3.3 — Whole-task counterfactual.** `dpe_eval_contexts` already records per-task DPE evaluations
and `ReplayRuleSet` already replays a candidate policy over them, so this is a clean, deterministic,
read-only extension:
- `PgEvalContextStore.ListForTask(tenant, taskID)` (per-task, chronological).
- `DPEHandler.Counterfactual`: `POST /v1/tasks/{id}/counterfactual` re-runs the task's recorded
  evaluations under a candidate policy (ad-hoc `rules`, a saved `policy_version`, else active) and
  reports every decision that would change vs the recorded baseline. agent_admin-gated. (Model
  counterfactuals need re-execution, which is L3.1's territory, and are explicitly out of scope of
  the deterministic replay.)
- **Tests:** `handler/http.TestDPE_Counterfactual_{ReportsChangedDecisions,NoRecordedEvals404,
  BadTaskID}`. dpe + handler suites green.

**SDK parity (TS + Python), each with contract tests:**
- **TS:** `replayEditTask`, `taskCounterfactual`, `revealTaskPII` + `CounterfactualResult`/
  `AuditReveal` schemas. `npm run typecheck` clean; **189 vitest pass** (+3).
- **Python:** `replay_edit_task`, `task_counterfactual`, `reveal_task_pii` + `CounterfactualResult`/
  `CounterfactualOutcome`/`CounterfactualChange`/`AuditReveal` models. `mypy` clean; ruff clean;
  **266 unit pass** + L3 contract tests.

**FE (control-tower):** `tasksApi.{counterfactual,replayEdit,auditReveal}` + `CounterfactualResult`
type. New `CounterfactualPanel` on the task detail page aside: run-on-demand whole-task
counterfactual under the active policy, rendering every changed decision (from → to, colour-coded by
verdict) with designed idle / loading / empty / no-evaluations / error states. Brand-locked. `tsc`
+ lint clean.

## ✅ Reliability Substrate Plan 2 — COMPLETE (2026-07-22)

L1.1 (attested autonomy), L2 (governed speculative execution), L3 (compliance time-travel) all
built prod-grade, tested, and wired end-to-end (backend + both SDKs + control-tower FE). L1.2 + L1.3
(insurer seam + premium) OUT OF SCOPE per owner decision. Full backend `go build ./...` + `vet` +
touched-package suites green; TS 189 / Python 266 unit; control-tower typecheck + touched-file lint
clean. Uncommitted (owner commits).

### L3 FE gaps closed — edit-and-continue UI + gated reveal toggle (2026-07-22)

The two L3 FE surfaces that shipped as API+SDK-only are now wired into the task detail page.

**Prerequisite — surface the token-context id.** The reveal needs the run's MAL token-context id,
which was not exposed anywhere. Threaded it end-to-end: `AuditAppendInput.TokenContextID` (set from
the workflow's `tokenContextID` at the task-complete audit append) → `audit.TokenisationStep.
ContextID` (via the trace builder) → the sealed reasoning trace → `GET /v1/tasks/{id}/reasoning-trace`
(`tokenisation.context_id`). Additive/optional (empty when MAL is off ⇒ existing audit bytes
unchanged). `go build ./...` + vet clean; audit suite green.

**L3.1 FE — `ReplayEditPanel`** (task detail main column, completed/failed tasks). A JSON editor
pre-filled with the task's input, a "commit for real" toggle (unticked ⇒ shadow re-run that commits
nothing), and a re-run button → `tasksApi.replayEdit` with a fresh idempotency key → links to the
new run. Handles invalid-JSON and 403 with designed error states. Makes clear it forks a new run and
leaves the original intact.

**L3.2 FE — `AuditRevealPanel`** (task detail main column). Gated by `useCurrentUserRole()`: renders
ONLY for `auditor` / `super_admin` (and only when the run tokenised something, i.e. the trace has a
`context_id`) — everyone else sees nothing, and the backend enforces the same gate. Paste tokenised
content → `tasksApi.auditReveal({token_context_id, text})` → shows the revealed values + count with
a "discloses PII, audited" warning. Handles 403 gracefully.

Both brand-locked (tokens, lucide `strokeWidth={1.5}`, sentence case, no em dashes, no live-dot);
`tsc --noEmit` + touched-file lint clean. **Plan 2 L3 is now complete in the FE as well.**

### Plan 2 — Postgres integration tests for the new queries (2026-07-22, run against Docker PG16)

DB-backed integration tests for the three new repository methods, `//go:build integration`, gated on
`ORCHESTRATOR_DATABASE_DSN` (skipped without it). Each package's new file carries a `TestMain` that
applies the full goose migration chain (embedded `migrations.FS`) once — idempotent, so a shared DB
across packages is safe when run serially (`-p 1`). **Verified green against a real `postgres:16`
container** (migrations applied cleanly through version 134, including 00134's `CONCURRENTLY` index):

- **`internal/assurance/attestation_integration_test.go`** — `TaskReceipts` returns only the target
  task's receipts in seq order with the right governance flags (and never another task's), and the
  full pipeline builds + verifies a real attestation from the DB rows; `OpenIncidentsForTask`
  attributes an open incident to the task via the action-id join and excludes other tasks'.
- **`internal/dpe/eval_context_integration_test.go`** — `ListForTask` returns one task's recorded
  evaluations chronologically, tenant-scoped (a different tenant sees nothing), and a candidate
  policy replayed over them flips exactly the expected decision.
- **`internal/mal/pgstore_integration_test.go`** — `PgTokenStore.Retain` extends a context's token
  TTL, **never shortens** it (GREATEST), leaves the value readable, no-ops on a non-positive TTL,
  and can **un-expire** a lapsed row.

**Bug found + fixed by actually running it:** the pre-existing `TestLedgerStatsRepository_Integration`
(and my new seed helper) inserted ledger rows with `prev_hash=''`, which violates the per-tenant
`UNIQUE(tenant_id, prev_hash)` chain index from migration 00089 — so that test had never actually
passed against the real schema. Both now seed a unique `prev_hash` per row (mirroring how the real
signer chains off distinct hashes).

Run: `ORCHESTRATOR_DATABASE_DSN=... go test -tags=integration -p 1 ./internal/{assurance,dpe,mal}/`.
Untagged unit builds are unaffected (integration files are excluded without the tag).

## Postgres Write-Scaling Tier 2 (tenant-sharding) — implementation log

Plan doc: `docs/Actrone_Postgres_Write_Scaling_Tier2_Buildout.md`. Operational hardening + a move
engine + observability over the already-built dormant routing seam. Inert-by-default (zero shard
DSNs ⇒ byte-identical); every phase ships dormant, irreversible data-move step last + behind the
most testing.

### P0.1 — Directory-first placement + unplanned-auto-placement guard ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

- `regionpool/shard.go` — the resolver is now explicitly **directory-first**: an authoritative
  `tenant_shard_assignments` pin wins; the consistent-hash ring is the fallback ONLY for a brand-new
  tenant (so adding a shard reshuffles nobody who already has a directory row). New
  `Placement(ctx, tenantID) → (shard, source, err)` exposes `PlacementOverride|PlacementHash|
  PlacementDefault`; `Shard` delegates to it. The source feeds the P0.1 guard + P3 override-hit/
  hash-assign telemetry.
- `repository/shard_assignment.go` — `LoadAll` now **fail-fasts on a corrupt directory row** (empty
  shard_id), and `PlacementCoverage(ctx) → (tenants, assigned)` counts active tenants vs directory
  rows in one round trip.
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` bootstrap — when sharding is active the authoritative directory load is
  **fatal on failure** (fail-fast, CLAUDE.md §3: never start routing against an unknown directory),
  and a **coverage guard** warns loudly (`>10%` uncovered ⇒ `Warn`) that hash-placed tenants would
  reshuffle on a shard add, prompting the operator to assign directory rows first.
- **Tests:** `regionpool.TestConsistentHashResolver_{PlacementSource,MovedTenantNeverReHashes}` — a
  moved/pinned tenant never re-hashes even as the ring grows; source classification is correct.
  `go build ./...` + regionpool suite green.

### P0.2 — Dynamic directory refresh (LISTEN/NOTIFY + TTL fallback + atomic swap) ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

The authoritative directory can no longer be a bootstrap-static snapshot: after a rebalance flips a
directory row, every instance must route the tenant to the NEW shard before the source rows drop.

- `regionpool/shard.go` — the resolver's overrides now live behind an `atomic.Pointer[map]` with
  `SetOverrides` (atomic swap, mirrors `PoolRouter`'s registry pattern); `Placement`/`Shard` read it
  lock-free, safe concurrently with a live swap.
- `migrations/00135_tenant_shard_assignments_notify.sql` — an `AFTER INSERT/UPDATE/DELETE` trigger
  fires `pg_notify('tenant_shard_assignments_changed', tenant_id)` on any directory write.
- `repository/shard_directory_refresher.go` — `ShardDirectoryRefresher`: a single serialised refresh
  worker fed by (a) a LISTEN loop (instant, reconnect-with-capped-backoff, re-subscribe re-reconciles)
  and (b) a bounded TTL ticker (default 30s, self-heals a missed/coalesced notification). Each
  successful re-read atomically swaps the whole directory into the resolver; **a read failure logs +
  KEEPS the last-good map** (fail-safe, never crashes routing, never applies a partial view).
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` — starts the refresher goroutine when sharding is active (inert
  otherwise); the directory table stays on the primary control plane.
- **Tests:** unit `regionpool.TestConsistentHashResolver_SetOverridesLiveSwap` (atomic swap changes
  placement; nil-safe). **Integration (real Postgres, verified in Docker):**
  `repository.TestShardDirectoryRefresher_Integration` — the NOTIFY path reflects an `Assign` within
  the SLA; disabling the trigger proves the **TTL fallback** re-reads a direct write; a **malformed
  row** (empty shard_id) makes `LoadAll` fail and the refresher **retains last-good** (A/B still
  resolve); fixing the row recovers. Self-bootstrapping `TestMain` applies the migration chain.

### P0.3 — Cross-shard Primary() audit + CI guard ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Every `router.Primary()` use bypasses tenant→shard routing and is a latent cross-shard bug once a
tenant moves to a shard. All 11 call sites inventoried, classified, annotated, and gated.

- **`docs/Actrone_Shard_Primary_Audit.md`** — the matrix: each of the 11 sites classified as
  `control-plane` (keyed by a non-tenant id — route/verify token, agent/workflow/connector id — needs
  a control-plane key→tenant→shard index), `route-by-tenant-todo` (the caller knows the tenant — thread
  it + `ForTenant`), or `cross-tenant-sweep` (fan out across shards, P4). Plus the "global control-plane
  data never sharded" note (the directory itself, plan catalog, cross-org marketplace/A2A trust).
- Each site now carries an inline `// shard-ok: <class> — <reason>` annotation (tasks.go ×5,
  agents.go ×2, identity.go, channels/store.go ×2, connector/store.go).
- **`internal/shardguard`** — a source-scanning guard: `ScanViolations(files)` flags any `.Primary()`
  call site lacking a `shard-ok:` annotation (inline or preceding line). `TestScanViolations_Logic`
  unit-tests the scanner (inline/preceding/blank-between pass; bare fails; the method definition is
  never flagged); **`TestNoUnauditedPrimaryUses` walks `internal/` + `cmd/` and fails a new un-audited
  use** — the CI regression guard. Both green.

### P0.4 — Shard↔region consistency + shard-key stability ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Enforces the region-then-shard nesting invariant: a tenant's shard always lives inside their
residency plane. Chose the self-describing **naming-convention** option (the plan offers this OR a
directory `region` column) — no per-row region to keep in sync, no migration/backfill.

- `regionpool/shard.go` — `RegionOfShard(id)` derives a shard's region from the `<region>-<suffix>`
  convention (`eu-0`→`eu`; a region-agnostic `s0`→`""`, the single-region default). New
  `ShardRegistry.ForTenantInRegion(ctx, tenantID, homeRegion)` returns the shard pool ONLY when the
  shard belongs to `homeRegion`; a cross-region assignment (corrupt/mis-planned directory row) is
  **refused, fail-safe to the default** with the reason as an error. Documented: the shard key is the
  immutable tenant/org id, never derived from mutable data.
- `regionpool/registry.go` — `Registry.HomeRegion(ctx, tenantID)` exposes the tenant's resolved
  region string (fail-safe "" on a resolver error) so the composed router can pass it down.
- `repository/region_router.go` — `PoolRouter.ForTenant` dimension-2 now calls `ForTenantInRegion`
  with the tenant's home region, so a wrong-region shard never routes (falls back to the region pool).
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` — bootstrap warns loudly when regions are active but a shard id is not
  region-tagged (or names an unconfigured region), since the nesting check would silently fail-safe it.
- **Tests:** `regionpool.TestRegionOfShard` + `TestShardRegistry_ForTenantInRegion` (in-region routes;
  **cross-region refused + fail-safe to default**; single-region default matches; no-shard → default).
  Inert for the single-region deployment (region-agnostic shard ids + empty home region both match).

### P1 — Schema fan-out across shards ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

A migration that reaches the primary but misses a shard is silent drift that corrupts the next
tenant move. The migration runner is now shard-aware and parity-checked.

- `internal/dbmigrate/fanout.go` — `FanOut(ctx, fs, targets, log)` applies the embedded goose chain
  to the primary AND every configured shard sequentially (race-free global goose state), then
  verifies every target reached the **same** version. A failure on any target aborts fail-fast with
  the target named (partial fan-out reported, never silent). `Parity(ctx, targets)` is a read-only
  drift audit (each target's `goose_db_version`, error if they differ). `checkParity` is a pure,
  stable-ordered helper.
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` — `runMigrations(dsn, shardDSNs, log)` builds `primary + sorted shards`
  and calls `FanOut`; a drift/partial fan-out fails startup (fail-fast). **Inert with no shards** (a
  single primary target ⇒ byte-identical to before). Dropped the now-unused `database/sql` + `goose`
  imports.
- **Tests:** unit `dbmigrate.TestCheckParity` (single/all-equal/behind-shard-reported/empty).
  **Integration (real Postgres, verified in Docker):** `TestFanOut_Integration` creates two shard
  databases, fans out the full chain to both → asserts identical version (parity), rolls back the
  last migration on one shard → **asserts drift is reported**, then re-applies to restore parity
  (reversibility). This is the multi-shard fixture the P1 CI gate runs in the integration lane.

**Deploy/CI follow-up (infra):** wire the integration lane to invoke `go test -tags=integration
./internal/dbmigrate/` with a 2-shard fixture so a single-cluster assumption is blocked in CI (the
code gate — this test — exists; the GH-Actions yaml is the infra step).

### P2.1 — Shard-move engine: authoritative table set + verify + mover ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Generalises the region-cutover primitive to a tenant SHARD move. A full move must relocate EVERY
tenant-owned row; the region list (10 tables) was a residency subset — a shard move needs all 104.

- `regionpool/tenant_tables.go` — `ResolveTenantOwnedTables(ctx, pool)` **introspects the live
  schema** for every `tenant_id` base table (excluding partition children), removes an explicit,
  reviewed `controlPlaneTables` exclusion set (the directory itself, org identity mirror, billing/
  metering/marketplace rollups, migration bookkeeping — data that stays on the primary), and returns
  the rest in **FK topological order** (Kahn's, deterministic alphabetical tie-break; a cycle errors).
  Self-adapting (a new tenant table is auto-included) + `TenantTableCoverage` drift check.
- `regionpool/pgx_executor.go` — the executor is now pool-based with a configurable allowlist
  (`NewPgxStepExecutorWithTables`) so it serves both region cutover (residency set) and shard move
  (the 104 tenant tables), preserving the table-name injection guard. New **`VerifyTable`**: source-
  vs-destination row count + an **order-independent content checksum** (`sum` of a per-row md5 hash,
  commutative so copy order is irrelevant) — the parity gate before cutover. A mismatch means no flip.
- `regionpool/shard_migrator.go` — `ShardMover.Plan(from, to)` validates the move (from≠to, both
  shards registered + distinct pools, **same region** — nesting invariant P0.4) and returns the
  FK-ordered steps read from the source shard's schema; `CopyAllTables` / `VerifyAllTables` /
  `DeleteAllSource` are the staged primitives (copy → verify → flip → drop) the move workflow drives,
  with delete in reverse FK order and separate from copy (never a lossy cut).
- **Tests:** unit `TestTopoSortTables_{FKOrder,Deterministic,CycleDetected}` + the residency-allowlist
  test. **Integration (real Postgres, verified in Docker):** `TestResolveTenantOwnedTables_Integration`
  pins the **104-table golden set** (drift fails the build), asserts coverage completeness + FK
  ordering + control-plane exclusion; `TestVerifyTable_Integration` proves the checksum catches a
  content mutation (equal counts) and a row deletion, and that a bystander tenant's rows never affect
  the checksum.

### P2.2 — Per-tenant write freeze (quiesce primitive) ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

Only per-agent pause existed; a safe move needs a tenant-level write freeze.

- `migrations/00136_tenant_write_freeze.sql` — `tenant_write_freeze(tenant_id PK, reason, frozen_by,
  frozen_at)` on the primary control plane (a tenant's freeze is checkable regardless of which shard
  holds its data). Presence of a row = frozen.
- `repository/tenant_freeze.go` — `TenantFreezeRepository`: `Freeze` (idempotent), `Unfreeze`
  (idempotent), `IsFrozen` (missing row ⇒ not frozen), `ListFrozen` (admin/telemetry).
- `domain.ErrCodeTenantMigrating` (`ERR_TENANT_MIGRATING`) → HTTP **423 Locked**, retryable.
- `handler/http/tasks.go` — a `tenantFreezeGate` on `TaskHandler` (`WithFreezeGate`); the new-work
  entry points (`Submit`, `SubmitSpeculative`, `ReplayEdit`) refuse a frozen tenant with the retryable
  423 via `tenantFrozen`. **Fail-open** on a gate read error (the move's parity verify is the
  correctness backstop, so a control-store blip never blocks legitimate work). Wired in `main.go`.
- **Design note (deliberate deviation):** the plan lists a governed-write-commit freeze check too. I
  do NOT block in-flight governed writes mid-flight — a freeze longer than the activity retry budget
  would fail a running task. Instead: the submit-freeze stops NEW work, the move workflow (P2.3)
  DRAINS in-flight tasks (bounded grace), and parity-verify aborts the move if an in-flight write
  slipped through (safe). Reads continue throughout.
- **Tests:** unit `handler/http.TestTenantFrozen` (no-gate/frozen-423/not-frozen/read-error-fail-open).
  **Integration (real Postgres):** `repository.TestTenantFreezeRepository_Integration`
  (freeze/isfrozen/unfreeze/list, idempotent, **tenant isolation** — one tenant's freeze never
  affects another).

### P2.3 — Tenant shard-move workflow (Temporal SAGA) ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

The load-bearing, highest-risk build: a durable, resumable, compensating move, ordered so the single
IRREVERSIBLE step (drop-source) is last and behind every verification.

- `internal/shardmove/activities.go` — all I/O lives in activities (the workflow body stays
  deterministic): `PlanMove` (validate + resolve/pin the FK-ordered tenant tables), `FreezeWrites` /
  `Unfreeze` (P2.2 quiesce), `CopyData`, `VerifyParity` (row count + checksum per table),
  `FlipDirectory` (upsert the authoritative assignment — its NOTIFY re-routes every instance via the
  P0.2 refresher), `DropSource` (reverse FK order), `DropDestination` (abort compensation via a
  REVERSED executor that deletes off the destination), `Seal` (append-only move receipt). Deps are
  interfaces (`FreezeStore`, `DirectoryStore`) so the engine is testable.
- `internal/shardmove/workflow.go` — `ShardMoveWorkflow`: plan → (dry-run stops) → freeze → drain →
  copy → verify → **[abort-compensate on mismatch]** → flip → propagate barrier → unfreeze → drop
  grace → drop-source → seal. Deterministic body (activity calls + Temporal timers only). Its own
  `shard-move` task queue keeps a slow bulk copy off the hot agent queue. Past the flip a failure is
  SAFE (data on both, routing points at the verified destination) so it retries forward rather than
  compensating; pre-flip failures compensate and leave the tenant intact on the source.
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` — builds the `ShardMover` + activities and starts a dedicated worker,
  only when sharding is active (inert otherwise).
- **Tests (Temporal test env, mocked activities):** dry-run stops after plan (no freeze/copy/flip/
  drop); **happy-path ordering asserts drop-source runs LAST** (after verify+flip+unfreeze) and that
  compensation never runs; **parity mismatch aborts** with `ERR_SHARD_MOVE_ABORTED`, compensating and
  asserting `FlipDirectory` and `DropSource` are NEVER reached; a copy failure compensates; a rejected
  plan never freezes or touches data. All green.

### P2.4 — Governed shard-move admin endpoint ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

The only sanctioned way to move a tenant between shards (never a raw SQL console move, CLAUDE.md §6.4).

- `handler/http/shard_move.go` — `POST /v1/admin/shards/move`, mirroring the residency-cutover safety
  posture: mounted behind `RequireOrgAdmin` and scoped to the **caller's own tenant** (tenant id from
  the authenticated context, never the body, so an admin can never move someone else's data);
  **`dry_run` defaults to TRUE** (a bare call previews and moves nothing); a real move **requires
  `X-Idempotency-Key`**, which becomes the Temporal workflow id so a retried request JOINS the
  in-flight move instead of starting a second (double-copy/double-delete) one; every action is
  audit-logged. A dry run waits for the plan and returns the exact table set (validation guards
  surface as a caller-actionable **422**); a real move returns **202 + workflow id** (it runs for
  minutes). Reuses the package's existing `workflowStarter` interface for testability.
- `cmd/orchestrator/main.go` — the handler is constructed and the route registered **only when
  sharding is active** (no shard pools ⇒ no move engine ⇒ the route does not exist).
- **Tests:** `TestShardMove_{DryRunByDefault, RealMoveRequiresIdempotencyKey,
  RealMoveStartsDurableWorkflowKeyedByIdempotency, PlanRejectionIs422, RequiresBothShards}` — asserts
  the dry-run default, own-tenant scoping, that a missing idempotency key never starts a workflow,
  that the workflow id is keyed by the idempotency key on the right task queue, and the 422 path.
  Full `go build ./...` + `go vet` green.

### P2.5 — Move tests: the gate before any real turn-up ✅ DONE (2026-07-22, real Postgres)

`internal/shardmove/move_integration_test.go` runs the **REAL workflow with the REAL activities
against two REAL Postgres shard databases** (both migrated via the P1 fan-out), with the primary as
the control plane holding the directory + freeze tables. The Temporal test env auto-advances the
grace timers, so the SAGA runs end-to-end in seconds.

- **`TestShardMove_EndToEnd_Integration`** — seeds a tenant on shard A and a BYSTANDER tenant on
  BOTH shards, then moves A→B and asserts: full parity on the destination, **zero rows left on the
  source**, the directory flipped to the new shard, the **write freeze lifted**, and the bystander
  **untouched on both shards** (isolation invariant). Then performs the **REVERSE move** B→A (the
  only real rollback path) and asserts parity is restored, the source repopulated, the destination
  drained, and the directory rolled back — with the bystander still untouched.
- **`TestShardMove_CopyFailureCompensates_Integration`** — injects a REAL failure (a conflicting row
  pre-planted on the destination so the bulk copy hits a PK conflict) and asserts the SAGA
  compensates: the tenant is **intact on the source**, the destination is **cleared of orphans**, the
  directory was **never flipped**, and the freeze is **lifted**.

**Two real bugs this gate caught (both would have broken every production move):**
1. **Generated columns.** `CopyTable` did `SELECT *` and copied every column, but Postgres rejects a
   direct write to a GENERATED column (`agents.build_mode`, SQLSTATE 42P10) — so the copy could never
   succeed. Fixed: `copyableColumns` reads `pg_attribute` and copies an EXPLICIT, sanitised column
   list excluding generated columns (the destination recomputes them from the copied base data).
2. **A misclassified control-plane table.** The P2.2 migration added `tenant_write_freeze` (it has a
   `tenant_id`), and the schema-derived move set auto-included it — which would have moved the move's
   OWN quiesce flag onto a shard. The golden drift test failed loudly and forced the classification;
   it is now in `controlPlaneTables`. This is exactly the "a new tenant table can never be silently
   missed" guard doing its job.

All Tier-2 suites green: unit (`regionpool`, `shardmove`, `dbmigrate`, `shardguard`) and integration
(`-tags=integration -p 1`) against Docker Postgres 16.

### P3 — Observability, SLO alerts, turn-up/rollback runbook ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

You can now SEE per-shard routing health and move safety, and there is a written, tested turn-up +
rollback procedure.

- **`regionpool/metrics.go` (routing telemetry):** `shard_placement_total{source}` (the
  override-vs-hash ratio — the P0.1 coverage signal), `shard_routes_total{shard}` (load distribution),
  **`shard_route_fallback_total{reason}`** (resolver_error|wrong_region — a non-zero rate is a routing
  bug or config gap and pages), `shard_directory_refresh_total{result}` + `shard_directory_entries`
  (refresh health/lag; the gauge only moves on success so a failed refresh cannot mask a stale map).
  Wired into `Placement`, `ForTenantInRegion`, and the directory refresher. Labels are closed enums or
  operator-configured shard ids (bounded cardinality, CLAUDE.md §6.3).
- **`shardmove/metrics.go` (move telemetry):** `shard_move_step_total{step,result}` (per-SAGA-step
  health), `shard_move_verify_total{result}` (parity match/mismatch), `shard_moves_in_flight` (should
  be 0 at rest — a stuck value means a tenant never unfroze), **`shard_move_freeze_seconds`** (the
  user-visible write outage), `shard_move_rows_copied_total` (copy throughput). The freeze duration is
  measured on the **deterministic workflow clock** (`workflow.Now`) and passed into `Unfreeze`, so it
  is replay-safe — never a wall-clock read inside the workflow.
- **`infra/observability/prometheus/alert_rules.yml`** — a new `actrone.sharding` group:
  `ShardRouteFallback` (critical), `ShardDirectoryRefreshFailing` (critical — do not start a move),
  `ShardMoveStuckInFlight` (critical, >30m frozen), `ShardMoveParityMismatch`, and
  `ShardMoveFreezeWindowLong` (p95 > 5m). Per-shard Aurora writer signals (CPU, commit latency,
  WriteIOPS, connections, replica lag) are noted as CloudWatch-exporter sourced and dashboarded per
  shard.
- **`infra/docs/Actrone_Shard_TurnUp_Runbook.md`** — provision (RDS Proxy per shard **mandatory**;
  region-tagged `<region>-<n>` naming is load-bearing) → apply schema via the P1 fan-out with an
  explicit parity check → canary-route a test tenant → **dry-run then move a pilot tenant** through
  the governed endpoint → what to watch → verify. Plus **§6 rollback**, leading with the asymmetry:
  removing shard DSNs is a clean kill-switch **only before any move**; afterwards it is an outage and
  the only correct rollback is a **reverse move** (a tested path). Includes the mid-flight failure
  matrix (pre-flip compensates; post-flip is safe and retries forward; stuck-move procedure) and
  per-shard backup/PITR reconciliation against the directory.
- A pre-existing router test used a region-agnostic shard id with a region-scoped tenant — exactly the
  misconfiguration the P0.4 guard now refuses. Updated to the documented convention and **extended
  with an explicit cross-region-refusal assertion**. All suites green; shardmove stable over 5 runs.

### P4 — Load-driven rebalancer + cross-shard aggregation ✅ DONE (2026-07-22)

- **`internal/shardbalance` (the advisor).** Load is measured entirely on the **CONTROL plane** — the
  tenant→shard directory joined to the metering ledger, both of which are control-plane tables that
  never move to a shard — so per-shard load needs **no cross-shard query at all**. `Loads` reports
  tenant count + metered usage per shard and **includes configured-but-empty shards** (a freshly
  provisioned shard must be visible as the least-loaded target). `PlaceNewTenant` puts a brand-new
  tenant on the least-loaded **in-region** shard and records the directory row, so a tenant is
  directory-placed from birth and never reshuffled by a later shard add (closing the P0.1 loop); it
  **refuses to re-place an already-placed tenant** (that is a governed move, not a placement).
  `Recommend` proposes **bounded, explainable, in-region** moves off hot shards and is **ADVISORY
  ONLY** — it never moves data; an operator executes it through the P2.4 governed endpoint. A
  balanced cluster recommends nothing (churn is worse than mild imbalance).
- **`regionpool/scatter.go` (cross-shard aggregation).** Generic `Scatter` fans a read-only function
  across every pool with bounded concurrency and merges in Go — **never a cross-shard JOIN**, never on
  the OLTP hot path. It returns **partial results alongside a naming error**, so a best-effort sweep
  can proceed while a completeness-critical caller can fail closed. `ScatterCount` is the
  count/sum helper for the `cross-tenant-sweep` call sites the P0.3 audit flagged. Documented: billing
  and metering do **not** need this — those tables are control-plane, so platform revenue aggregates
  still run on one database.
- **Tests:** unit `TestLeastLoaded{,_TieBreaks}`, `TestHotShards` (region-scoped placement — an EU
  tenant is never placed on an emptier US shard; deterministic tie-breaks; a balanced cluster and
  degenerate inputs stay quiet), `TestPolicyDefaults`. **Integration (real Postgres):**
  `TestBalancer_Integration` (control-plane load incl. empty shards, least-loaded in-region placement
  recorded in the directory, re-placement refused, bounded in-region advice heaviest-first, and an
  assertion that **advice moved nothing**); `TestScatter_Integration` (fan-out totals merged in Go,
  partial-results-plus-error on a failing pool, empty pool set is a no-op).

## Wiring audit: both plans (Reliability Substrate Plan 2 + Tier-2 sharding), 2026-07-22

A verification pass over both completed plans: "did we miss anything, is everything wired?" Every
route, workflow, worker registration, migration, SDK export and FE panel was checked against the plan.

### Verified wired (no action needed)

- **Plan 2 routes (9/9):** `/v1/assurance/tasks/{taskId}/attestation`, `/v1/assurance/attestation-key`,
  `/v1/tasks/speculative`, `/v1/tasks/{taskID}/speculative`, `/v1/tasks/{taskID}/replay-edit`,
  `/v1/tasks/{taskID}/counterfactual`, `/v1/tasks/{taskID}/audit-reveal`,
  `/v1/tasks/{taskID}/audit-retention`, `/v1/admin/shards/move`.
- **Workflows/workers:** `SpeculativeTaskWorkflow` and `shardmove.ShardMoveWorkflow` both registered,
  `shardmove` activities registered on their own task queue.
- **Migrations present:** `00134` (action-ledger task index), `00135` (directory NOTIFY trigger),
  `00136` (tenant write freeze).
- **Both SDKs at parity:** TS exports `verifyAttestation`, `Attestation`, `SpeculativeVariant`,
  `CounterfactualResult`, `AuditReveal` plus 6 client methods; Python exports the same surface plus the
  same 6 methods.
- **FE:** all five Plan-2 panels (`RunAttestationCard`, `SpeculativeBranchPanel`, `CounterfactualPanel`,
  `ReplayEditPanel`, `AuditRevealPanel`) are imported **and rendered** on the task detail page.

### Gaps found and CLOSED

1. **P4 rebalancer was unreachable.** `internal/shardbalance` was built and tested but never
   constructed and had no endpoint, so an operator had no way to see which shard was hot or which move
   to run: the governed move endpoint was a blind trigger. Added `Balancer.AdviseTenant` (the
   tenant-scoped projection of `Recommend`), `ShardBalanceHandler`, and two org-admin routes:
   `GET /v1/admin/shards/loads` (per-shard AGGREGATES only, no tenant ids) and
   `GET /v1/admin/shards/advice` (the caller's OWN tenant only). The library's cross-tenant
   `Recommend()` names other tenants' ids and is deliberately **not** exposed over HTTP. Both routes are
   read-only; executing advice still goes through the dry-run-default, idempotency-keyed move endpoint.
   Mounted only when sharding is active, and a construction failure is non-fatal (advice is an
   operability aid, not a serving path).

2. **The two cross-tenant sweeps still queried the primary only.** `regionpool.Scatter` /
   `ScatterCount` existed but had no production call site, so the `cross-tenant-sweep` sites flagged in
   the P0.3 audit would have **silently missed sharded tenants** at turn-up. Added
   `PoolRouter.AllPools()` (primary + every regional plane + every shard, via new `Registry.Pools()` and
   `ShardRegistry.Pools()`) and wired both sites:
   - `AgentRepository.ListTenantIDs` (retention sweep): scatters, dedupes, sorts. **Completeness is
     mandatory** here, because a missed tenant is a tenant whose expired data is never deleted, so a
     partial sweep is a hard error and the retention run retries.
   - `TaskRepository.ListBatchQueued` (batch picker): scatters, merges oldest-first, dedupes by task id,
     truncates to `limit`. **Best-effort by design**, because fanning out N ways multiplies the chance
     some pool is unhealthy and aborting would let one bad shard stall batching platform-wide. Nothing
     is lost: a `batch_queued` row is durable and is picked up on the next tick.

   New metric `shard_scatter_total{sweep,result}` makes a degraded (`partial`) sweep alertable.
   `docs/Actrone_Shard_Primary_Audit.md` rows 5 and 7 updated to **RESOLVED**.

3. **FE client lacked `auditRetention`.** The Plan-2 audit-retention endpoint had no browser binding.
   Added it, and corrected the contract to the server's real shape (`ttl_hours`, not `retain_until`).

### Pre-existing bug found and fixed: batch mode was dead at the database layer

Writing the new `ListBatchQueued` integration test surfaced `tasks_status_check` rejecting
`batch_queued` (SQLSTATE 23514). `00002` created the constraint over
(pending|running|completed|failed|cancelled); `00012` then added batch mode (the `batch_mode` column, a
partial index on `status = 'batch_queued'`, the batch job columns) but **never widened the
constraint**. So `domain.TaskStatusBatchQueued` and `TaskStatusBatchProcessing` could never be written,
`ListBatchQueued` always returned empty, and `BatchService` never submitted anything. The batch path has
been inert since `00012`.

Fixed by **migration `00137_tasks_batch_status_check.sql`**: it widens the CHECK to admit both batch
statuses, using `ADD ... NOT VALID` followed by `VALIDATE` under `NO TRANSACTION` so the ACCESS
EXCLUSIVE lock is a catalogue update rather than a full scan of the hot `tasks` table. The Down narrows
it back and **fails loudly** if any row still holds a batch status, rather than mutating task history to
force a rollback through.

### New tests

- `repository/scatter_sweep_integration_test.go`: `TestPoolRouter_AllPools_Integration` (the fan-out
  set, and that an unsharded deployment still fans out to the primary alone),
  `TestListTenantIDs_ScatterDedupes` (a tenant reachable through three pools is returned exactly once,
  sorted), and `TestListBatchQueued_ScatterMergesOldestFirst` (dedupe, global oldest-first merge, and
  that `limit` applies to the MERGED set rather than per pool). The topology registers one physical
  database under two shard ids plus the primary, the deliberate worst case for double counting.
- `shardbalance/balance_integration_test.go` extended with `AdviseTenant` assertions: a hot-shard tenant
  is told so and advised in-region, a healthy-shard tenant is advised nothing, an unplaced tenant is not
  an error (it is the P0.1 coverage signal), advice never names another tenant, and advice moves nothing.

### Verification

`go build ./...` OK, `go vet` clean, **full unit suite green**, and `go test -tags=integration -p 1
./...` green against Postgres 16 in Docker except `internal/agentschedule/temporalstore`. That one is a
pre-existing flake in the separate (unbuilt) Agent Scheduling workstream: it has **zero dependency** on
any package changed here (verified with `go list -deps`) and fails on Temporal visibility-store
eventual consistency. TS SDK: typecheck clean, 189/189 tests pass. Python SDK: `mypy` clean across 57
source files, 266 passed and 21 skipped. Control Tower `tsc --noEmit` clean.

All changes remain **uncommitted**.
